children.push(P("The book's preferred protection is not the fixed stop Section 3.61 measured but the trailing stop, 'an important technique in wealth preservation': a stop a fixed percentage below the highest price since it was set, so it rises with the stock and never falls. It quotes William O'Neill's 8%, and 20-25% for very volatile stocks. Its limit orders raise the mirror question: a sell order above the price fills if the price TOUCHES it. run_trailing_stops.py measures both on the same panel as Section 3.61 (183 names, 2018 to mid-2024, before the reserved period), with the trailing reference set to the highest adjusted high up to the bar BEFORE the one tested, so a low is never compared with a high printed later on the same bar."));
children.push(P("Median share of windows in which the stop is hit (fixed stop at the same distance in brackets):"));
children.push(table([2760, 1100, 1100, 1100, 1100, 1100, 1100], [
  ["Hit within 21 sessions", "5%", "8%", "10%", "15%", "20%", "25%"],
  ["All names", "95% (55%)", "72% (34%)", "53% (25%)", "21% (10%)", "8% (4%)", "3% (3%)"],
  ["Quietest fifth (27%/yr)", "82% (38%)", "45% (20%)", "25% (11%)", "5% (3%)", "3% (1%)", "1% (1%)"],
  ["Most volatile fifth (54%/yr)", "100% (66%)", "92% (50%)", "82% (42%)", "51% (25%)", "29% (14%)", "13% (8%)"],
  ["Hit within 63 sessions: all names", "100% (71%)", "96% (56%)", "92% (48%)", "64% (28%)", "36% (16%)", "16% (8%)"],
  ["63 sessions: most volatile fifth", "100% (81%)", "100% (72%)", "100% (68%)", "96% (52%)", "76% (33%)", "48% (28%)"],
]));
children.push(P("A trailing stop is hit about twice as often as a fixed stop at the same distance. The book's 8% trails out the median NSE stock within a month 72% of the time and within a quarter 96%: close to a guaranteed exit within a quarter, not protection against a rare fall. The book's 20-25% for volatile stocks is the right idea - on the most volatile fifth it brings the one-month exit rate to 13-29% - but over a quarter it is still close to a coin toss (48-76%)."));
children.push(P([B("Which estimate is calibrated. "), T("For a driftless random walk the fall from the running peak has the law of the walk's absolute value (Levy's theorem), so a trailing stop at distance a (in sigma units) is hit when |W| first leaves (-a, a), which has an exact series (stops.trailing_probability); a simulation converges on it as the steps get finer (0.52 at 252 steps, 0.57 at 4,000, 0.58 continuous, for a 10% trailing stop at sigma 10%). Upside, the reflection principle gives 2 x P(end above +x). Out of sample, sigma from the EWMA forecast at each window's start, ~170,000 predictions per period:")]));
children.push(table([5160, 2100, 2100], [
  ["Expected calibration error", "Calm 2022-24", "Crash 2020-21"],
  ["Trailing stop: fixed-stop estimate (2 x end-point)", "0.086", "0.141"],
  ["Trailing stop: Levy drawdown formula", "0.053", "0.056"],
  ["Target touch: end-point P(gain)", "0.111", "0.124"],
  ["Target touch: reflection (2 x P(gain))", "0.038", "0.036"],
]));
children.push(P("A 10% trailing stop over 21 sessions was hit 38.6% of the time in the calm period (formula 43.8%, fixed-stop estimate 22.9%) and 62.4% in the crash (61.2%, 33.5%); a +10% target was touched 37.6% and 41.9% of the time (reflection 26.8% and 37.6%, end-point 13.4% and 18.8%). The remaining errors have a direction: the trailing formula runs a little high in a rising market and the target estimate a little low at a month, both because the formula has no drift and NSE rose. Correcting that needs a drift forecast, which this project does not have with demonstrated skill, so the estimates stay driftless and the help text says which way they err."));
children.push(P([B("What shipped. "), T("Two rows in the Risk & Probability panel beneath the fixed-stop row, at the user's threshold: P(a -x% trailing stop is hit) - risk_metrics.trailing_stop_probability, the Levy formula with the distance read off the panel's own end-point P(loss), so it uses the same fat-tailed or log-normal distribution as the rest of the panel - and P(+x% is touched) - risk_metrics.target_touch_probability, 2 x P(gain), the chance a sell order at +x% fills before the horizon. 2 new tests.")]));

children.push(H2("3.64 Breadth, break-even and balance-sheet trends (Thomsett, Stock Market Math) - weak breadth forecasts a more volatile market, not a falling one"));
children.push(P("Thomsett's Stock Market Math is a formula reference: returns and their annualisation, leverage, balance-sheet and operating-statement tests, market breadth, volume indicators and oscillators. Most of the price and volume formulas are already here and were tested in Sections 3.53 and 3.56 (RSI, MACD, stochastics, moving averages, Bollinger bands, OBV, Chaikin A/D). Three parts were new and buildable: market breadth (Ch. 8-9), the break-even return after tax and inflation (Ch. 1, 4), and the balance-sheet and operating trend tests (Ch. 6-7). Building them turned up two bugs."));
children.push(H3("Market breadth (Ch. 8-9): measured, and it is about volatility"));
children.push(P("Breadth needs the whole market on every day, including names that later delisted - exactly what the survivorship-free bhavcopy cache holds - so no model here had seen it. breadth.daily_breadth builds it point-in-time: an advance is close > prevclose (NSE adjusts prevclose on an ex-date), and 52-week extremes use the chain of close / prevclose, so a split never prints a false new low. Every issue with at least Rs 1 crore of turnover counts (median 864 a day, 2017 to mid-2024). run_market_breadth.py pre-registered four measures, three targets and a Bonferroni bar of |t| > 2.87 (Newey-West errors; out of sample fitted to 2021, scored 2022 to mid-2024):"));
children.push(table([2900, 2200, 1700, 2560], [
  ["Measure", "Next 5-day market return", "Next 21-day return", "Next-month volatility beyond HAR"],
  ["ad10 (10-day advance/decline)", "t +3.07, OOS R2 -0.038", "t +1.88", "t -3.83, OOS R2 +0.062"],
  ["McClellan oscillator", "t +3.14, OOS R2 -0.024", "t +1.98", "t -4.03, OOS R2 +0.093"],
  ["hilo10 (net new highs)", "t +0.99", "t -0.48", "t -1.07"],
  ["hl_index (record-high percent)", "t +1.63", "t +0.82", "t -0.80"],
]));
children.push(P([B("Returns: no. "), T("The advance/decline measures clear the bar for next week's return only because of 2018-2020 (t +3.16, +3.04); in 2021-2024 they fall to t +0.84 and +1.36, and out of sample they lose to the plain mean. Short-horizon persistence in an equal-weighted index is partly mechanical (small stocks react late).")]));
children.push(P([B("Volatility: yes, and robustly. "), T("Weak breadth predicts a more volatile month beyond the market's own HAR forecast, in both halves (t -2.90 and -3.07 for ad10, -3.16 and -3.03 for McClellan), cutting out-of-sample error by 6-9%. Next-month market volatility by breadth quintile: 24.6% a year in the weakest fifth, then 19.9%, 19.0%, 17.3%, 17.4%. It survives the leverage effect (HAR + past 10-day return and its downside: ad10 t -3.46, OOS error -5.7%; McClellan -3.35, -6.8%) and India VIX on top (ad10 -3.44, -5.5%; McClellan -2.87, -5.2%).")]));
children.push(P([B("For a single stock it is too small to use. "), T("Added to each stock's own HAR forecast (187 names, 283,731 name-days, Driscoll-Kraay errors), breadth is still significant (t -2.37 to -2.95) but changes out-of-sample error by -0.2% to +0.7%. The cones are not adjusted. What shipped is the market-level fact as context: a caption under the Risk & Probability panel with the latest 10-session advance/decline ratio, its fifth on frozen 2018-2024 cut-points, the measured next-month market volatility for that fifth, and the statement that this stock's range is not adjusted for it (breadth.live_breadth; AD10_EDGES and NEXT_MONTH_VOL are constants from the study, never refitted on live data).")]));
children.push(H3("The break-even move (Ch. 1, 4)"));
children.push(P("Thomsett's break-even return is inflation / (1 - tax rate). A trade also pays round-trip costs first, and only the gain after costs is taxed, so over h sessions B = cost + inflation x h / 252 / (1 - tax) (risk_metrics.breakeven_move). Cost is Section 3.56's itemised delivery round trip (22.2 bp, almost all STT, before brokerage and spread); inflation is RBI's 4% CPI target; tax is 20% short-term capital gains on listed equity (from 23 July 2024) plus 4% cess - dated constants. Over 5 sessions B = +0.32% (0.22% costs + 0.10% inflation after tax); over 1 session +0.24%. The panel shows the break-even move and P(the move clears break-even) from the same distribution as the rest of the panel; on TCS for 5 days (18 October 2026), 48% against a P(price up) of 49%. Annualising a short forecast was not added: it multiplies the noise along with the move."));
children.push(H3("Thomsett's balance-sheet tests (Ch. 6-7), added to the company check"));
children.push(table([2400, 3300, 3660], [
  ["Test", "Formula", "Flag"],
  ["Inventory turnover", "cost of goods sold / average inventory", "down a tenth in a year (project cut-off); n/a below 2% of assets"],
  ["Debt capitalization", "long-term debt / (long-term debt + equity)", "rose every year shown, by 2+ points (rising over years is 'a serious warning')"],
  ["Operating margin trend", "operating income / revenue", "lower than last year by over half a point"],
  ["Tangible book value", "net worth - goodwill and intangibles", "context: price / tangible book and intangible share; negative flagged"],
  ["Average collection period", "365 / receivables turnover", "shown in days on the sales-to-receivables row (same ratio)"],
]));
children.push(P("Live: MRPL's inventory turnover fell 11.4 -> 7.3 and its collection period rose 14 -> 25 days, both flagged; TCS's debt share 7% -> 8% -> 8% was flagged until the 2-point floor was added; HDFC Bank's intangibles are 36% of net worth (goodwill from the HDFC Ltd merger); Vodafone Idea's tangible book value is negative, and its debt share on negative equity now reports the red flag rather than a number."));
children.push(H3("Two refinements to the company check (Ch. 2, 6)"));
children.push(P([B("Returns on average balances. "), T("Thomsett (Ch. 2) divides a year's profit by the AVERAGE net worth over the year, not the closing figure, because the profit was earned on the capital employed through the year; year-end alone understates a growing company's return and overstates a shrinking one's. ROE and ROA now use the average of the opening and closing balance (year-end when only one year is reported). No live verdict changed, but TCS's ROE moved from 45.9% to 48.7%, MRPL's from 13.6% to 14.2% and HDFC Bank's from 8.6% to 8.9%.")]));
children.push(P([B("The payout trend. "), T("Thomsett (Ch. 6): as earnings grow the share paid out ideally should remain the same, and slipping over several years is a negative sign, though buybacks can replace dividends. The ratio is erratic year to year (MRPL 5% -> 624% -> 36%), so the Income group flags it only after two falls in a row by a company that stayed profitable, with the buyback caveat in the rule. None of the four live names is slipping. One more test.")]));
children.push(H3("Two bugs found on the way"));
children.push(table([2200, 3900, 3260], [
  ["Where", "Bug", "Fix"],
  ["bhavcopy.fetch_day", "A failed download looked like a holiday and was cached as an empty 'market shut' file, permanently: ten trading days, 29 October - 11 November 2024, sat in the cache as holidays (the defect class Section 3.60 fixed in fno_members)", "Only real files are cached; the ten were deleted and re-fetched (1,906-1,924 lines each). No published result is affected: every bhavcopy study ends by mid-2024, and run_stress_test.py reads yfinance."],
  ["get_data.get_bse_nse_data", "BSE's listing API answered 403 to every request on 18 October 2026; its failure emptied the company list and the app could not be used", "BSE is optional: on failure the NSE listing (2,605 names) is used and a warning logged."],
]));
children.push(P("Not taken: the put/call ratio (offered and not run: one F&O snapshot a month in the cache, so ~1,600 downloads, and India VIX - already a control - is priced from the same options, so the expected result is a null; no option chain, Section 3.49); short interest, mutual fund cash and large block ratios (no historical NSE source); the Money Flow Index (a volume-weighted RSI, redundant by Section 3.56's measure); RSI, MACD, stochastics, moving averages, Bollinger bands and the random walk (already tested, and the random walk is the baseline everywhere); core earnings and combined price-fundamental tests (no footnote data; fundamentals history blocked, Section 3.33); option returns, leverage and compound-interest formulas. 7 new tests."));

children.push(H2("3.65 Results days and the options market (Benklifa, Think Like an Option Trader) - a 95% band covered only 82% of one-day windows holding a results day"));
children.push(P("Benklifa's book is about a way of thinking more than a technique. A stock trader looks at a price and asks where it goes next; an options trader sees a distribution of prices the market has already priced, and trades the gap between that and their own. This app's forecasts are already distributions, so the idea fits, and two parts could be built: Ch. 5's known unknowns became a measured accuracy fix, and the option trader's reading of a chain (Ch. 2-3, 6) became a live view beside the forecast. The work also found a ticker bug that had been forecasting the wrong company."));
children.push(H3("Results days: a known unknown the cones ignored"));
children.push(P("A results announcement is the clearest known unknown: its date is public (SEBI requires at least two working days' notice; most give one to three weeks), its outcome is not. corporate_events.results_calendar reads NSE's board-meeting calendar, which goes back years (44,955 results meetings, 2017-2024). run_results_days.py measured 175 names, 2018 to mid-2024: event days are the session on or after the meeting (day0) and the next (day1, since many report after the close); a day's surprise is z = return / one-step EWMA sigma; fitted on 2018-2021, scored 2022 to mid-2024."));
children.push(table([3360, 2000, 2000, 2000], [
  ["E[z^2] (ordinary day ~ 1)", "day0", "day1", "other days"],
  ["2018-2021", "3.24", "4.24", "1.14"],
  ["2022-2024", "2.85", "4.17", "1.12"],
]));
children.push(P("Relative to an ordinary day a results session carries 2.86x the variance and the one after it 3.74x, stable across the periods; 9.2% of results days move more than 3 sigma, against 1.5% of other days. A 95% band calibrated on all 2018-2021 windows then covers:"));
children.push(table([1700, 1600, 1300, 1700, 1300, 1760], [
  ["Horizon", "With results: plain", "Widened", "Without: plain", "Widened", "Widening"],
  ["1 session", "81.9%", "94.8%", "95.2%", "94.8%", "x1.88"],
  ["5 sessions", "89.3%", "94.5%", "96.0%", "95.4%", "x1.33"],
  ["10 sessions", "93.0%", "95.2%", "96.7%", "96.2%", "x1.15"],
]));
children.push(P("The widening adds each results session's excess variance, sigma_h^2 = sigma^2 (h + n0 (2.86 - 1) + n1 (3.74 - 1)), and restores ~95% out of sample. A window starting within five sessions AFTER a results day is only mildly over-covered (95.3-96.8%): the volatility forecast is 5-10% too wide just after one, too little to change the models for."));
children.push(P([B("What shipped. "), T("When NSE's calendar shows a results meeting whose day0 or day1 falls inside the horizon, a notice names the date and the Risk & Probability panel is widened by risk_metrics.results_widening = 0.96 x sqrt((h + 1.86 n0 + 2.74 n1) / h), the 0.96 being the 95% quantile's measured shrinkage once results windows stop inflating it. Every panel number is read off the widened distribution; the per-model charts and tables are not widened, and the notice says so. Caveat: the calendar gives meeting dates, not announcement dates, so the study treats each event as known from the window's start - true for horizons up to ten sessions with the usual notice.")]));
children.push(H3("What the options market prices (live, F&O stocks)"));
children.push(P("option_market.py reads NSE's live option chain (the option-chain-v3 endpoint answers a browser-like session; iv_data.py had found the older one unusable): implied volatility from the at-the-money straddle with Black-76 on a forward from put-call parity; the expected move to expiry (the straddle as a share of the price, about 0.8 sigma); the 1-sigma move over the horizon against the panel's; the results-day move the market prices (iv1^2 T1 = forward-vol^2 T1 + v, the forward volatility between the two expiries having no event in it); and defined risk (Ch. 6) - a put at the stop level, its cost, and the most a holder can lose through expiry, gaps included."));
children.push(table([3360, 2000, 2000, 2000], [
  ["16 Oct 2026 close", "TCS", "Infosys", "Reliance"],
  ["ATM implied vol, 27 Oct expiry", "26.6%", "34.9%", "23.9%"],
  ["Expected move to expiry", "+/-3.7%", "+/-4.8%", "+/-3.3%"],
  ["1 sigma over 5 sessions", "+/-3.75%", "+/-4.9%", "+/-3.4%"],
  ["Protective put at -2%", "2,400: 0.90%, loss capped 3.5%", "1,120: 1.07%, capped 4.6%", "1,180: 0.65%, capped 3.2%"],
]));
children.push(P("Infosys reports on 23 October and the market prices that day as a +/-3.0% move, 2.2x an ordinary session's variance - below the panel-wide 2.86x and 3.74x, as expected for a large, closely followed company. TCS shows the opposite: its results were on 9 October, the stock then rose about 20% in a week, and HAR-RV's 5-day spread (roughly +/-7%) is about twice the options market's +/-3.75%; the models still carry the results-day moves, which the options market prices as over. Option prices are risk-neutral (India VIX ran a median 27% above NIFTY's realised volatility; Section 3.49) and NSE keeps no option history, so the view sits in a collapsed expander beside the forecast and is never fed into it."));
children.push(H3("A ticker bug: five Nifty 100 names were forecast from the wrong listing"));
children.push(P("The app's first lookup passed the listing symbol to yfinance without the exchange suffix, and yfinance resolves a bare symbol to a US listing whenever one exists: 'Infosys Limited' fetched the NYSE ADR INFY in USD and converted it to rupees, and the data-quality banner passed it because the series is real. A Nifty 100 scan found 5 such symbols - HAL (Halliburton, not Hindustan Aeronautics), IDEA, INFY, LTM and TMCV; the other 95 resolve to nothing and fall through correctly. The listing now records each symbol's exchange and get_data.get_yf_ticker returns INFY.NS (or .BO); the nselib path still gets the bare symbol. The prediction log holds only 3M India, which is unaffected."));
children.push(H3("Other scheduled events: measured, and not shipped"));
children.push(P("Three other events have public dates and unknown outcomes and hit every stock at once, so run_market_events.py ran the same pre-registered test (fit 2018-2021, score 2022 to mid-2024; an event qualifies only if a plain 95% band clearly under-covers its windows AND the widening restores ~95% out of sample):"));
children.push(table([2400, 2500, 1600, 1500, 1360], [
  ["Event", "E[z^2] day0/day1, 2018-21 -> 2022-24", "1 session: plain -> widened", "5 sessions", "Verdict"],
  ["Union Budget (5 + 3)", "4.36/3.98 -> 2.20/1.11", "93.2% -> 98.9%", "93.7% -> 97.1%", "Not stable"],
  ["RBI policy (22 + 15, scheduled)", "1.66/1.19 -> 1.18/0.92", "96.4% -> 97.2%", "96.1% -> 96.4%", "No effect"],
  ["F&O expiry (48 + 30)", "1.37/1.55 -> 1.62/1.47", "92.4% -> 94.0%", "94.2% -> 94.6%", "Small, not fixed"],
]));
children.push(P("Budget days were large in 2018-21 and have since calmed, and with three test events the fitted widening over-corrects; RBI decisions are already covered; expiry days leave a small one-day shortfall the widening does not fix. Results days remain the only scheduled event that widens the panel. RBI dates for 2019-20, 2021-22 and 2023-24 were checked against RBI's published schedules; unscheduled moves (27 March and 22 May 2020, 4 May 2022) are excluded."));
children.push(H3("Starting the option-chain history"));
children.push(P("Because NSE keeps no option history, nothing option-based here can be backtested - not whether single-stock implied volatility would improve the cones, nor Thomsett's put/call ratio. tools/collect_option_chains.py records it from now on: one snapshot a day per F&O stock (spot, ATM strike, call and put prices, parity forward, straddle-implied volatility, total call and put open interest, next two expiries) appended to .option_history/option_chains.csv; idempotent, retried when throttled, with its logic in option_market.collect_snapshots so it is tested like product code. The first run (16 October 2026 close) recorded 421 rows across all 213 F&O stocks (median first-expiry ATM IV 27.2%, range 15.8-45.3%) and found a bug in the live view: M&M and GVT&D returned no chain because the & was not URL-encoded. Run it daily after the close (for example from Windows Task Scheduler); after six to twelve months the implied-volatility test can be pre-registered."));
children.push(P("Not taken: Ch. 1's trader psychology (duplicating a strategy and correlation versus causation are what the walk-forward, Diebold-Mariano and SPA layers already enforce, Section 3.16); the Ch. 3-4 option-strategy builds, Greeks, synthetic straddles and directional trades (no option history to backtest, and the app forecasts rather than recommends trades); Ch. 6 layering and portfolio risk (single-stock app); the appendix's price uncertainty principle (an analogy for the cone). 9 new tests."));

children.push(H2("3.66 The O'Neil-disciple signals (Morales & Kacher, Trade Like an O'Neil Disciple) - pocket pivots add nothing, and major gap-ups lag the market"));
children.push(P("The book is mostly the authors' trading memoir and psychology, but three chapters give rules precise enough to code: pocket pivots and buying major gap-ups (Ch. 5), and Dr. K's market direction model from distribution and follow-through days (Ch. 7), reported at +33.1% a year 1974-2009. The same chapter says the follow-through threshold was re-chosen several times 'based on perceived volatility' and that the model has undisclosed 'fail-safes' - exactly how a backtest is overfitted - so run_oneil.py fixes every rule in advance at the book's published values, on 197 liquid NSE names and NIFTY, 2018 to mid-2024. Volume is rupee turnover (share volume jumps on a split); the market's volume is NSE's total equity turnover, since NIFTY's own volume is zero on 25 days of the window."));
children.push(H3("Pocket pivots (Ch. 5): the volume signature adds nothing"));
children.push(P("An up day on volume at least the largest down-day volume of the prior 10 sessions, above the 50-day average; a proper one also starts from the 10-day (low within 1% of it) and does not follow 'wedging'. The control is up days above the 50-day WITHOUT the signature. Excess returns over the equal-weighted panel; t-statistics average events by calendar month first."));
children.push(table([3360, 1200, 1200, 1800, 1800], [
  ["Group", "Events", "5-day", "21-day (t)", "Beat market, 21d"],
  ["Core pocket pivot", "31,781", "-0.08%", "+0.03% (0.20)", "46.5%"],
  ["Proper pocket pivot", "9,254", "-0.02%", "+0.23% (1.16)", "46.8%"],
  ["Control: up, above 50-day, no signature", "56,737", "+0.08%", "+0.11% (0.55)", "46.3%"],
]));
children.push(P("The core pivot minus the control, month by month, is -0.07% (t -0.42), and after a 0.22% round trip the pivot loses 0.18%. It fires on about one stock-session in nine - not the discriminating buy point the book describes."));
children.push(H3("Major gap-ups (Ch. 5): the wrong sign on NSE"));
children.push(P("A gap of 0.75x the 40-day ATR on 1.5x the 50-day volume; 'leading stock' proxied by above the 50-day and within 15% of the 52-week high (fundamentals are unavailable point-in-time, Section 3.33). Held 21 sessions: all major gap-ups -0.98% at 5 days (t -2.85) and -1.65% at 21 (t -2.93); the leading-stock proxy -0.72% (t -1.16); with the book's sell rule (exit below the gap day's low) +0.09% (t 0.26), 78% stopped out. A major gap-up on NSE has on average given back ground against the market - a short-term reversal (Section 3.40), the opposite of the book's advice, and not tradeable short for most of the universe."));
children.push(H3("The market direction model (Ch. 7): timing indistinguishable from luck"));
children.push(P("Distribution day: index down 0.1%+ on higher volume, or churning (up under 0.1%, closing in the bottom quarter of the range, on higher volume); five in 20 sessions sells. A follow-through day on or after session 4 of a rally attempt, up at least the threshold on higher volume, buys back. Long NIFTY in an uptrend, cash in a correction, 10 bp per switch, against 1,000 circular shifts of the model's own state series:"));
children.push(table([3260, 1300, 1100, 1000, 1300, 1400], [
  ["", "Return/yr", "Vol", "Sharpe", "Worst DD", "Shifts as good"],
  ["Model, 1.0% follow-through", "9.4%", "11.2%", "0.84", "-13.1%", "14.4%"],
  ["Model, 1.5% (the book's 2010 value)", "7.0%", "10.4%", "0.67", "-11.5%", "28.5%"],
  ["Buy and hold NIFTY", "13.0%", "18.2%", "0.72", "-38.4%", "-"],
]));
children.push(P("At 1.0% the model has the better Sharpe and a third of the drawdown but earns less than holding, and one in seven random shifts of the same signal does as well: the timing is not distinguishable from luck, and the smaller drawdown comes mostly from being out half the time. At the book's 1.5% it trails holding outright. The book's +33.1% is not reproduced; market breadth (Section 3.64) remains the market-level signal this project found robust."));
children.push(H3("The moving-average sell guide (Ch. 5): the one rule that worked in its own direction"));
children.push(P("The book's sell guide is a close below the 50-day (or the 10-day, for a stock that has 'obeyed' it) on heavy volume. The 'obeys' choice is judgement, but the core rule can be fixed: a close crossing below the 50-day on volume at least 1.5x its 50-day average, against the same crossing on ordinary volume."));
children.push(table([3660, 1300, 1500, 1500, 1400], [
  ["Close crossing below the 50-day", "Events", "5-day (t)", "21-day (t)", "63-day (t)"],
  ["On heavy volume", "1,410", "-0.52% (-2.14)", "-0.94% (-2.26)", "-1.14% (-1.66)"],
  ["On ordinary volume (control)", "8,277", "+0.11% (2.01)", "+0.08% (0.49)", "+0.04% (0.11)"],
  ["Heavy minus ordinary, by month", "", "-0.63% (-2.59)", "-1.02% (-2.55)", "-1.18% (-1.77)"],
]));
children.push(P("Heavy-volume breaks go on to lag the market and quiet ones do not - the only signal in the book that behaved as the book says. The sign holds in both halves (2018-20: -1.00% at 5 days, -0.70% at 21; 2021-24: -0.35% and -1.28%). It is a lead, not a finding: about twenty tests have run in this section and t of about -2.6 does not clear a correction for that many. Selling avoids about a 1% lag over the next month against a 0.22% round trip. It is the fourth candidate, with residual reversal, the 52-week high and dividend payer status, for the single pre-registered reserved-period test: heavy-volume (1.5x) crossings below the 50-day against ordinary-volume ones, 21-session excess return."));
children.push(P([B("What the app shows. "), T("A collapsed O'Neil-disciple checklist for the selected stock (daily data): whether the last session was a pocket pivot and whether it was extended (the book's own 'do not buy'); any major gap-up in the last 21 sessions and whether its low has held; the close against the 50- and 10-day averages and any heavy-volume 50-day break in the last 5 sessions (with the measured lead); and NIFTY's distribution days in the last 20 sessions from NSE's total turnover - each beside its measured verdict (oneil.MEASURED), so no signal is shown without its result. On 16 October 2026, TCS and Infosys showed extended pocket pivots (lows 8.2% and 7.0% above the 10-day) and NIFTY had 8 distribution days in 20 sessions, against about 4 in a typical 20.")]));
children.push(P("Not taken: Ch. 1-4 and 8-10 (memoir, psychology, commandments; 'cut losses' was measured as stops in Sections 3.61 and 3.63, 'buy expensive' as the 52-week high and momentum in Sections 3.54 and 3.39); Ch. 5's choice between the 10- and 50-day guides, continuation and bottom-fishing pivots and chart bases (no fixed definition); Ch. 6 short set-ups (Section 3.40); Ch. 7's hindsight thresholds and undisclosed fail-safes. 7 new tests."));

children.push(H2("3.68 Stock screens (Hough, Your Next Great Stock) - a falling stock's last week tells you nothing, and a mined screen's edge is what chance produces"));
children.push(P("Hough's book is a guide to stock screening: eleven screen recipes (Part Three), each resting on a published US study, guru screens (Ch. 23), and what to do with the stocks that survive (Part Four). Nearly all of it is fundamental, and Section 3.33 measured about a year of point-in-time statements per NSE name, so none of the screens can be back-tested here. The app now scores the selected stock live against every recipe the free data supports (hough.py), and the two claims that need only prices were tested on NSE before the reserved period (run_hough_screens.py), with the hypotheses written down first."));
children.push(P([B("The falling-knife deal breaker (Ch. 24). "), T("The book: if a stock has fallen over the past year and was still moving lower over the past week, set it aside until it stabilises, even at a higher price. This project's reversal finding (Section 3.39) predicts the opposite. 332 liquid names, 2018 to mid-2024; a 12-month loser is 'still falling' when its 5-session return is below zero.")]));
children.push(table([3600, 1500, 1420, 1420, 1420], [
  ["Group", "Stock-days", "5-day excess (t)", "21-day (t)", "63-day (t)"],
  ["12-month loser, still falling", "90,031", "-0.11% (-1.71)", "-0.60% (-2.24)", "-1.90% (-2.99)"],
  ["12-month loser, stabilised", "76,471", "-0.18% (-2.33)", "-0.70% (-2.39)", "-1.72% (-2.57)"],
  ["12-month winner, falling this week", "106,655", "+0.20% (2.57)", "+0.36% (1.30)", "+0.92% (1.00)"],
  ["12-month winner, up this week", "135,138", "+0.09% (0.85)", "+0.45% (1.34)", "+1.06% (1.15)"],
]));
children.push(P("A1, still falling minus stabilised over 21 sessions by month: +0.10% (t 0.57). A2, buying now rather than waiting until the 5-session return turns non-negative: waiting saved 0.31% (t -2.12, median wait 4 sessions), short of the Bonferroni bar of 2.24. Net of the average 12-month loser over the same sessions (added after the first run) it is -0.08% (t -0.70). So every 12-month loser lags, by 0.6-0.7% a month, whatever its last week did: that is momentum. The week's direction adds nothing, and the book's rule has no support beyond 'it fell over the past year'."));
children.push(P([B("Data mining (Ch. 6). "), T("Hough's XYZ strategy, on NSE: group the panel by the first letter of the symbol (22 letters), pick the best three on 2018-01 to 2021-03, hold them on 2021-04 to 2024-06. N, T and B earned +10.0%/yr in sample and +2.3%/yr out of sample (88th percentile of random picks). The best three of 1,000 random relabellings earn a median +12.8%/yr in sample (95th percentile +18.0%) and -1.7%/yr after, so the letters did a little worse than chance. The survivor, T, holds 24 names, 14 of them Tata group companies, an apparent lurking variable (split tested after the first run). It was not: out of sample the Tata names earned +2.9%/yr (t 0.46) and the other ten +13.1%/yr (t 2.02), and the three letters' +2.3% sits at the 88th percentile of random picks - better than most, not rare. A rule found by searching 22 options must beat about +18%/yr in sample before the figure means anything.")]));
children.push(P([B("Follow the Leaders (Ch. 19): insider buying, the one screen with point-in-time data. "), T("SEBI's insider-trading rules make promoters, directors and key managerial personnel disclose a trade within two trading days, and NSE publishes each with its time, mode, category, value and holdings, so a disclosure can be dated to when the market could see it. insiders.py cached 199,395 disclosures, 2018 to mid-2024. The book's screen: in the trailing 90 days, at least two open-market purchases by executives, directors or promoters, Rs 88 lakh ($100,000) or more in total, under 5% of the shares (ESOP allotments, pledges, gifts, transfers between promoters and employees' trades excluded). Insiders tend to buy after a fall (median stock -3.2% over the prior 63 sessions), so the one pre-registered test compares each event with stocks in the same 12-month and 1-month return quintiles, over 63 sessions.")]));

children.push(H2("3.69 Second end-to-end audit - a polluted prediction log, a mislabelled median, and a runner that cannot read the reserved period"));
children.push(P("With the last book done, one pass over everything: static analysis (ruff over every module, test, driver and tool: no undefined name or syntax error; the loop-closure warnings all safe), 710 tests passed, the document and dependency checks clean, every research driver re-run and compared with its stored result, the app's interaction scenarios (0 problems), the page at five widths in a browser (clean), and all 20 models (Reliance, log stubbed: after the fixes all 20 render with no exception or error; the first run lost Regime Switching to the random-state bug)."));
children.push(P([B("A runner that cannot read the reserved period. "), T("tools/run_drivers.py runs each driver in its own process with a stand-in yfinance that raises, safe_fetch's fallback cache emptied and get_data's live fetchers (including its nselib fallback) blocked; a marker flags a driver that tried even if it caught the error. Output goes to a file and a timeout kills the process tree (a pipe held open by worker processes once hung two finished drivers for 9.5 hours). Result CSVs are compared and restored. Of 62 drivers: 42 ran offline (the 3 Kanwar & Gupta drivers after TA-Lib was installed); the 20 that read live prices, and so the reserved period, ran with the owner's approval on 20 October 2026, all clean, their result CSVs restored and their output read for errors only (34 ledger rows: 17 drivers, twice). In a final sweep after the fixes, all 48 result files reproduce exactly or within 1e-6.")]));

children.push(P("Not reproduced, and why: open_interest.csv gained the ten trading days recovered in Section 3.64 (489 dates; naive IC +0.0181, t 2.65; honest -0.0000, t -0.01; same conclusion as Section 3.42); the universe-expansion and seed files had not been refreshed after bb_position was removed, and now match the 29-feature numbers Section 3.60 published; the dividend file was the same-day bug. Not covered: live sources changing under the app. Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM's missing 95% band, confirmed here, was then fixed (Section 3.70)."));

children.push(H2("3.70 A 95% band for Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM - the last model without one, and two slow paths found on the way"));
children.push(P("Section 3.69 left Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM as the only model with no 95% band: no range on its chart or table, no Conviction, no part in the Risk & Probability panel. The other recursive models already get theirs from model_utils.forecast_nn_conformal with one fit; Hybrid now uses the same horizon-wise split conformal. ARIMA and the residual LSTM are fitted on the earlier 80% of the window; from up to 80 recent origins the ARIMA is brought up to date with the actual returns and the hybrid's own 5-step path is run; the 95% quantile of the absolute log errors at each step is the half-width, so the band widens with the horizon."));
children.push(P([B("Two slow paths found on the way. "), T("The first version took 282-351 s instead of about 45. pmdarima's update() re-estimated the ARIMA's parameters on each of its own predictions (about 300 calls); with fixed parameters that path is exactly the ARIMA's multi-step forecast (statsmodels append(refit=False) then forecast), 75x faster and within 0.00009 in log return on an AR(2). And calling the Keras LSTM directly ran eagerly, a Python loop per call with recurrent_dropout, 365 times; all origins now move one step per batched predict, 5 calls. A forecast takes 54 s (45 s of it the unchanged LSTM training).")]));
children.push(table([1800, 2200, 2400], [
  ["Step", "Coverage", "Mean band width"],
  ["1", "95.8%", "8.8% of the price"],
  ["2", "97.9%", "12.1%"],
  ["3", "97.9%", "15.5%"],
  ["4", "93.8%", "17.2%"],
  ["5", "91.7%", "19.6%"],
  ["All 240", "95.4% (Wilson 92.0-97.4%)", ""],
]));
children.push(P("run_hybrid_coverage.py, written down before running: six of the 40 most liquid NSE names, 8 origins each from July 2021 to June 2024 (before the reserved period), each forecast scored on the next 5 prices; the test was pooled coverage of at least 90%. It passes with the 95% target inside the interval (by name 90-100%; on the 48 origins 85.4-98.7%). In the app Hybrid now shows its band, a Conviction (-0.05 for Reliance instead of a dash), and feeds the risk panel; all 20 models now return a band. Four tests."));

children.push(H2("3.71 The charts - a cone from the last close, trading days side by side, and every model on one chart"));
children.push(P("Each chart was a line through the last 50 closes with a dot on each (Plotly calls every line trace Scatter). The charts now come from charts.py, plain functions that can be tested directly."));
children.push(table([3400, 5960], [

children.push(P("Narrowing bands were common: run to 10 days, 15 of 20 models narrowed at some step on Reliance and 7 on TCS (worst: N-BEATS on TCS, 19.6%, 15.3%, 35.1% wide at days 1-3). The simulation-based models and Chronos never did; the per-step calibrated ones did, from each step's sampling noise. widen_to_monotone takes each side's running maximum, inside HAR-RV and once on every model in the app; it only widens, so coverage cannot fall, at the cost that a noisy spike carries forward. Checked in Chrome at phone and laptop widths; six tests."));

children.push(H2("3.72 Adaptive conformal for every model, and the novelty gate in a real crash - neither earns its place"));
children.push(P("Two follow-ups after the last book, both about whether the 95% range holds when the market changes, on data before the reserved period. Both failed their pre-registered tests, so the app is unchanged."));
children.push(P("Adaptive conformal (ACI) for the eleven models that take a fixed quantile of about 190 recent calibration errors (run_aci_bands.py: 200 names, every session July 2018 to December 2024, about 290,000 windows per horizon, naive-forecast errors). The learning rate was chosen on 2018-2021 and scored on 2022-2024. CC is the mean gap from 95% across five quintiles of a volatility-shift measure."));
children.push(table([3600, 1920, 1920, 1920], [
  ["h = 5 sessions", "Static (shipped)", "ACI 0.01 (chosen)", "ACI 0.05 (HAR-RV's)"],
  ["Coverage 2022-24", "95.0%", "94.6%", "92.1%"],
  ["Mean width", "20.6%", "20.0%", "18.3%"],
  ["CC", "0.009", "0.007", "0.030"],
  ["2020 crash", "80.9%", "83.1%", "82.6%"],
  ["Results-day windows", "89.2%", "88.2%", "84.2%"],
]));
children.push(P("The CC improvement's interval, [-0.0050, +0.0008], includes zero, so ACI is not shipped. One session ahead it does help (CC 0.011 to 0.007, interval [-0.0056, -0.0030]). At five and ten sessions the windows overlap, one shock becomes h misses in a row, and a fast learning rate under-covers (92.1%, 90.3%). Every rule covered only about 81% in the 2020 crash."));
children.push(P("HAR-RV's own ACI was then checked at scale (run_har_aci_scale.py: 57 names, about 8,000 origins). In 2022-24 the ACI cone covered 95.0% / 94.8% / 95.7% at 1 / 5 / 10 sessions against static's 96.7% / 95.7% / 97.1%, with bands 5-13% narrower, and CC could not tell them apart. ACI stays the default (since Section 3.101, DtACI on daily runs of 13 sessions or fewer): HAR-RV standardises its errors by its own variance forecast, which absorbs the shifts that make raw-error ACI over-react."));
children.push(P("The novelty gate (Section 3.26) in a real crash (run_novelty_stress.py: 20 names, refitted at 22 quarter ends, about 24,700 sessions, against a plain 20-day volatility reading under the same percentile rule). It flagged 95% of names in the crash, but reached 58% only in the week to 13 March 2020, with the market 28% down, the same week as the volatility reading. It called 5.9% of calm sessions 'unprecedented' against 0.5% by design (2.7-3.3% with three or more years of history). With the volatility reading quiet, its flag left the band's miss rate at 4.8% against 4.6%. A recalibrated threshold could only make it a slower volatility detector, so it stays off, with the measured reason in config.py."));
children.push(P("Five tests cover model_utils.aci_quantile (test_aci.py), including its agreement with HAR-RV's procedure to 1e-16."));

children.push(H2("3.73 Third audit - a withheld row that blamed the model, a failed forecast that voted Bearish, and a banner ahead of its gate"));
children.push(P("A third pass on 31 October 2026, aimed at the serving layer: what the user is TOLD, rather than what any model computes. It found three bugs there, in gating.py and UI.py, two more in the fetch layer underneath (safe_fetch.py, get_data.py), and three passages of the documentation that had drifted from the code."));
children.push(table([3600, 5760], [
  ["Layer", "Result"],

The three bugs below"],
  ["Read by hand: get_data.py (ticker resolution, INR conversion, nselib fallback, source selection) and safe_fetch.py", "The nselib fallback is sound (its four earlier defects stay fixed, Section 3.19). The two fetch-layer bugs below"],
]));
children.push(P("Not run this time: the browser check at five widths, and the research drivers (no code they use changed)."));
children.push(P([B("A withheld row blamed the model. "), T("When the data-quality gate (Section 3.18) or the novelty gate (Section 3.26) withholds a row, its Basis should read 'withheld - data quality' or 'withheld - unseen market regime'. In the app it read 'no edge vs random walk (DM p=0.03)': the reason a model that may have rated High was withheld was put down to the model. The gates wrote their reason with setdefault, which writes only when the key is absent, and UI._model_confidence always sets note to '' (or 'assessment failed: ...'), so the reason was never written. The unit tests passed because their entries had no note key at all. gating._withhold now writes the reason unless an earlier gate already withheld the row. Three tests in test_gates.py build entries the way the UI does; two of them fail on the old code.")]));
children.push(P([B("A failed forecast voted Bearish. "), T("A model whose final price came back NaN or infinite was kept in the run. In the comparison table it read Bearish, because NaN >= 0 is false, and it was counted in the consensus. In the risk panel's log-normal fallback (no HAR-RV in the run), one NaN made the median range and VaR NaN. It was also written to the prediction log (Section 3.29). run_selected_models now treats a non-finite final price as a failed model: it warns and leaves the model out, the same way an exception is handled.")]));
children.push(P([B("The skill banner was drawn before the novelty gate. "), T("With USE_REGIME_NOVELTY on and an 'unprecedented' window, the page could show 'k of n models showed a measurable edge' above a table in which every call had just been withheld, and nothing on the page said why. The banner is now drawn after both gates, and an 'unprecedented' verdict replaces it with an 'Unseen market regime' notice (Section 4.3). The flag ships off, so the default page is unchanged.")]));
children.push(P("Neither UI fix has an automated test. Both live in the Streamlit script, which the unit tests do not import, and the scenarios never reach either path (no model returns NaN, and the novelty flag is off)."));
children.push(P([B("A short fetch replaced the last-known-good copy. "), T("safe_fetch (Section 3.22) flags a live result under half the cached length as 'partial', and then saved it over the cache anyway. The short frame became the baseline: the same truncated answer on the next run passed as 'live', and if the fetch then failed outright the fallback served the truncated history, not the last good one. The cache is now written only from a full 'live' result. The trade-off: if a stock's history genuinely shrinks for good, every fetch stays flagged 'partial' (a warning, not abstention) until .data_cache/ is cleared. test_partial_result_does_not_replace_last_known_good fails on the old code.")]));
children.push(P([B("INR conversion dropped the ticker. "), T("_convert_to_inr (Section 3.17) joins the FX history with merge, and pandas 3 does not carry attrs through a merge, so a converted stock lost the ticker the UI reads for its NSE lookups (the ex-dividend notice, the results-day widening, the company check and the Hough screen). The attrs are now restored after the merge. The conversion runs only for a foreign-currency ticker, which the NSE/BSE dropdown cannot select, so this was close to unreachable. It is the first test of the conversion at all: test_inr_conversion_keeps_the_ticker_and_uses_each_dates_rate also checks that each bar takes its own day's rate.")]));
children.push(P([B("Documentation that had drifted. "), T("The README's dependency table still listed the versions from before the pins were moved (Section 3.22): streamlit 1.54.0, yfinance 1.0, xgboost 3.1.2, scikit-learn 1.8.0 and tensorflow 2.20.0. It now matches requirements.txt, and lists pandas_market_calendars, which was missing. A caveat still described the forward prediction log as deferred, though prediction_log.py has implemented it since Section 3.29; it now states the log's real limits.")]));

children.push(H2("3.74 The label-horizon paradox on the NSE panel - a shorter label scores better, and nothing can pick it"));
children.push(P("Song, Liu & Chen (ICML 2026, arXiv 2602.03395) argue that the best training label for a horizon-T forecast is often a return over a shorter horizon h < T: a shorter label carries less accumulated noise, and that gain can outweigh the signal it gives up. It was the one paper in a review of Papers with Code's finance tasks that applies to the project's only real signal, the cross-sectional ranking of Section 3.39, and could be run on data the project already holds. Their evidence is one test year on Chinese indices, five seeds and no significance test. The test here uses data before the reserved period only (2018-01 to 2024-06); nothing from 2025 on was read."));
children.push(P([B("Design, registered in the driver before the first run. "), T("tests/validation/run_label_horizon.py takes Section 3.39's panel exactly (400 survivorship-free bhavcopy names, 29 features, the combined name-and-date split with its embargo, the calibrated LightGBM classifier), retrains on h-day labels for h = 1 to 5, and scores every arm on the same validation rows against the same 5-day target. The eight seeds are those of universe_seeds_n400_201801_202406.csv, and the split and model seed are shared by every arm within a seed. The horizon used, h*, is picked by an inner name-and-date split of each seed's training block, never by the validation block. The rule: the h = 5 arm must reproduce the stored per-seed IC to four decimals, or nothing is read; label-horizon selection is adopted only if the paired gain IC(h*) - IC(5) is positive in at least 6 of 8 seeds AND its median Newey-West t (4 lags) is at least 2; the full grid is descriptive only.")]));
children.push(P([B("The harness failed on the first run, and what fixed it. "), T("On the first run the h = 5 arm did not reproduce the stored file: median IC +0.0380 against +0.0372, with single seeds up to 0.005 apart (seed 114: +0.0398 against +0.0438). The driver was not the cause: run_universe_seeds.py's own code path, run the same day, gave the same figures, the validation row counts matched, the code and the in-window day cache were unchanged since 20 October 2026, and the classifier is bit-reproducible (Section 3.31). What was left was the corporate-action feed, which build_panel fetched live from NSE on every run. NSE revises it (Section 3.39 had already recorded this). NSE also returns its rows in a different order from one request to the next: two live fetches 30 seconds apart held the same 9,497 actions, but GLOBE's bonus and split, both on 2021-08-03, swapped places, because the table was sorted on symbol and ex-date only. The adjusted price then differs only in the last bits of a float, and that is enough to change the trees: with nothing else changed, the tie-break alone moved seed 121 from +0.0338 to +0.0320. Either could explain the 20 October file. Which one did cannot be told now, because no copy of that day's feed was kept.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("A snapshot. "), T("A live fetch in bhavcopy.corporate_actions now writes each chunk once under .bhav_cache/actions/ and reads it from there afterwards, so a research run sees the same inputs every time. A chunk ending within 90 days of today is never frozen. To take a fresh snapshot, delete the folder. The app's two callers (get_data's nselib fallback and the ex-dividend notice) pass snapshot=False, because they want NSE's latest records.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("A tie-break. "), T("The subject now breaks ties on symbol and ex-date, so two identical fetches give identical tables.")]));
children.push(P("Checked on the real feed: two live fetches and the snapshot read-back give identical tables for 2016-11 to 2024-06. Three tests in test_bhavcopy.py cover the read-back (including a symbol 'NA', which a careless reader would turn into a missing value), a recent chunk never being frozen, and an injected feed not being snapshotted. The seed file was then refreshed with run_universe_seeds.py, and the label-horizon driver, run afterwards as a separate process, reproduces it to four decimals on all eight seeds. This time the harness passed. Both were run once more after the renamed-company fix (Section 3.75), and the harness passed again; the figures below are from that run."));
children.push(table([2700, 2400, 1700, 2560], [
  ["Training label", "IC on the 5-day target (mean of 8 seeds)", "sd across seeds", "Score rank correlation, 5 sessions apart"],
  ["h = 1", "+0.0380", "0.0049", "0.41"],
  ["h = 2", "+0.0441", "0.0061", "0.45"],
  ["h = 3", "+0.0427", "0.0069", "0.51"],
  ["h = 4", "+0.0397", "0.0041", "0.57"],
  ["h = 5 (shipped)", "+0.0386", "0.0061", "0.59"],
  ["Raw 5-day reversal, same rows", "+0.0352", "", ""],
  ["What a book clearing ~22 bp of cost would need", "about 0.23", "", ""],
]));
children.push(P([B("Result: not adopted. "), T("The inner split picked h* = 3, 2, 5, 1, 3, 1, 5, 5 across the eight seeds. The paired gain IC(h*) - IC(5) averaged +0.0022 and was positive in 4 of 8 seeds; one was negative (-0.0042, where the pick was h = 1). The median t was +1.16 (+0.24 if the three seeds where h* = 5 count as zero), which fails the bar of 2, so the 5-day label stays. The two earlier runs failed the same way: median t +0.49 on the unfrozen feed, and +0.95 on the frozen feed before the renamed-company fix.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("The direction the paper reports is there, at h = 2 to 4. "), T("Those labels beat h = 5 on average (h = 1 does not: +0.0380 against +0.0386), and h = 2 beats it in 7 of 8 seeds, by +0.0055 on average (from -0.0026 to +0.0096). It is the best of five, read off the block registered to decide nothing, and the seeds share most of their data, so it is a hypothesis for a fresh period, not a result.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("The inner split cannot find it. "), T("On average the training block agrees that h = 3 is best (+0.0594 there, against +0.0556 for h = 5). Within any one seed, though, the horizons sit 0.005-0.015 apart in IC on the inner block, about as much as the gain being sought or more, so the pick is close to a draw.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("A shorter label buys IC with turnover. "), T("The score's persistence falls from 0.59 at h = 5 to 0.45 at h = 2 and 0.41 at h = 1, and turnover is the cost that already sinks this signal (Section 3.40).")]));
children.push(bullet([B("It does not move the economics. "), T("Section 3.40's raw sort broke even at 5.0 bp with |IC| 0.052. At that turnover gross return scales roughly with IC, so clearing about 22 bp needs an IC near 0.23. h = 2's +0.0055 covers about 3% of the gap from 0.039. Nothing in the app changes.")]));
children.push(bullet([T("Across these eight draws the model edges the raw reversal sort (+0.0386 against +0.0352, higher in 5 of 8 seeds), unlike Section 3.39's single split, where the raw feature won. That does not change the reading that the model has rediscovered the reversal factor. It does say the gap between the two is within the split noise.")]));
children.push(P("Not run: a fixed h = 2 scored on a period this run has not touched. The only such period is the reserved one from 2025 (Section 3.22). It was deliberately left unread: a gain this small cannot change a decision, and the reserved period can be read only once. Refreshed: universe_seeds_n400_201801_202406.csv (the seed study now reads median IC +0.0392, median t +2.58, 7 of 8 seeds above 2, none negative). Every other stored result built on this panel was re-run afterwards (Section 3.75). Per-seed results: tests/validation/label_horizon_n400_201801_202406.csv."));
children.push(H2("3.75 Renamed companies lost their corporate actions"));
children.push(P("After Section 3.74 froze the corporate-action feed, every stored result built on the bhavcopy panel was re-run on the frozen copy, to see which published figures still held. The first pass turned up a data bug larger than the one it was looking for: dividend_dates.csv had lost all eight of ICICIPRULI's dividends since October. NSE renamed ICICIPRULI to ICICILIFE on 23 October 2026, and its feed now files the company's whole history under the new symbol."));
children.push(P([B("The bug. "), T("The bhavcopy records each day under the symbol the company traded under THAT DAY; NSE's corporate-action feed records every action under the company's CURRENT symbol. adjust_prices joins the two on symbol, so a company that had since been renamed kept none of its actions from before the rename: no splits, bonuses or dividends. Its adjusted price was its raw price, and a split showed up as a crash.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("MCDOWELL-N "), T("(now UNITDSPR) split 1:5 on 2018-06-15, which the panel recorded as a -163% log return. With the split restored, its worst day is -14.3% on 2020-03-23.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("INFIBEAM "), T("(now CCAVENUE) had three splits and bonuses unadjusted. Two of them showed up as -85% and -73% days (2021-03-18, 2022-03-14). Its 2018-09-28 drop of -123% (log) is real.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("MOTHERSUMI "), T("(now MOTHERSON) lost two bonuses. Its worst day was -34.6% and is now -26.2%.")]));
children.push(P("Over the window the panel reads (2016-11 to 2024-06), the fix restores 639 actions on 137 symbols, 29 of them splits or bonuses and 598 dividends. In Section 3.39's 400-name panel, 25 of the 339 names were affected, with 153 actions, 6 of them splits or bonuses: AMARAJABAT, CADILAHC, CENTURYTEX, GATI, HEG, IBULHSGFIN, IBVENTURES, ICICIPRULI, IDFCBANK, INFIBEAM, INFRATEL, JUBILANT, KPIT, L&TFH, MCDOWELL-N, MOTHERSUMI, NIITTECH, PVR, SRTRANSFIN, STRTECH, TATAGLOBAL, TATAMOTORS, TATASPONGE, TWL and WELSPUNIND."));
children.push(P([B("Why Section 3.39's check missed it. "), T("It validated the adjustment against yfinance on 25 large caps. Only one of them, TATAMOTORS, has been renamed, and its only action in the check's window was a Rs 2 dividend in July 2023. A return correlation cannot see that.")]));
children.push(P([B("The fix. "), T("bhavcopy.symbol_changes() reads NSE's symbol-change list (old symbol, new symbol, effective date) and freezes it beside the action chunks. bhavcopy.add_former_symbols() copies each action to the symbol the company traded under on its ex-date, following chains of renames and stopping on a cycle. corporate_actions applies both by default whenever it uses the snapshot. The app's two live callers pass snapshot=False and work on current symbols, so they are unchanged. Four tests in test_bhavcopy.py cover a chain of two renames, the frozen list, an explicit renames= list, and a feed passed in by a test not being snapshotted. The suite was then 758.")]));
children.push(P([B("Every stored panel result, re-run. "), T("tools/run_drivers.py re-ran all 46 drivers that build on the panel, offline and with live prices blocked. 43 ran; run_bhavcopy_audit, run_market_breadth and run_oneil need live yfinance prices and stay blocked by design. 39 of the 65 stored result files changed. Three drivers that read reversal_costs_h5.csv had run alongside the one that rewrites it, so they were run again in sequence (run_residual_reversal, run_event_backtest, run_deflated_sharpe), and Section 3.46's four extra starting days were re-run too. Every figure that moved at the precision printed has been updated in its own section. The ones worth knowing:")]));
children.push(table([3200, 3080, 3080], [
