children.push(P([B("No verdict changed. "), T("Every pass is still a pass and every failure still a failure, but three results are now weaker: Section 3.57's DFA crash warning is now below 2.24, the bar used for its pre-registered test (it was a post-hoc diagnostic, and still clears 2); Section 3.68's out-of-sample half-year now only just clears a delivery round trip; and Section 3.74's h = 1 label no longer beats h = 5. The reversal books moved both ways (Section 3.40's raw sort down, Section 3.50's residual book up), and the cost verdict of Sections 3.40, 3.50 and 3.56 is unchanged.")]));
children.push(P([B("One systematic shift is not from this fix. "), T("Section 3.41's range-volatility A/B now gives bands about 2% wider (median; 0.3-5.0%), and coverage held or rose on all 48 rows. None of its 12 names was renamed. The stored file predates Section 3.71's widen_to_monotone, which can only widen a band, so that change is the likely cause. Its verdict is unchanged.")]));
children.push(P("Not re-run: the three blocked drivers; sub-window figures that no current driver prints (Sections 3.45 and 3.46's 2022-2024 runs); Section 3.68's power estimates; and the accounts of earlier runs that sections keep as history (for example Section 3.50's first reconciliation at -0.12%)."));

children.push(H2("3.76 A no-trade band on the residual reversal - it trims turnover by 29% and still falls 4.6 bp short"));
children.push(P("A full screen of the 151 papers in Papers with Code's Finance task (the review behind Section 3.74 had read only the first 20) found one more idea aimed at what actually sinks this project's signal. 'Finance-Grounded Optimization for Algorithmic Trading' (arXiv 2509.04541) trains on Sharpe- and P&L-based losses with a turnover penalty. The paper is thin (no code, one citation, no significance test), but its point applies: Section 3.50's residual reversal has the strongest IC in the project and loses to cost, and Section 3.40 showed that holding longer barely reduces turnover, because the signal has no persistence. A turnover penalty needs no new model. Its portfolio form is a NO-TRADE BAND: a name enters a leg at the extreme decile but leaves only when its rank drops out of a wider band, so a position whose signal is still good is kept rather than sold and bought back."));
children.push(P([B("Design, registered in the driver before the run. "), T("tests/validation/run_trade_band.py uses Section 3.50's signals (the raw and the k = 5 PCA-residual trailing 5-day return; long losers, short winners, equal weight) on the same 400-name panel, 2018-01 to 2024-06; nothing from the reserved period was read. Exit bands 0.10 (no band), 0.15, 0.20, 0.30 and 0.40 of the cross-sectional rank; rebalance every session, or every 5 sessions averaged over the five starting days. Cost: a cash-delivery round trip, 22.22 bp. Rule: the band rescues the signal only if some configuration breaks even above 22.22 bp in both log and simple returns and its return net of that cost has t > 2. Check first: the 5-session, no-band k = 5 book reproduces Section 3.50's stored breakeven (15.15 bp, turnover 3.470, both identical).")]));
children.push(table([2700, 1500, 1200, 2000, 1960], [
  ["k = 5 residual, every 5 sessions", "Gross per rebalance", "Turnover", "Breakeven (log / simple)", "Net of 22.2 bp, t (median of 5 starts)"],
  ["Exit 0.10 (no band, = 3.50)", "+0.263%", "3.47", "15.1 / 15.2 bp", "-0.55"],
  ["Exit 0.15", "+0.272%", "3.29", "16.5 / 16.6 bp", "-0.40"],
  ["Exit 0.20", "+0.261%", "3.12", "16.8 / 16.8 bp", "-0.41"],
  ["Exit 0.30", "+0.238%", "2.79", "17.0 / 16.9 bp", "-0.26"],
  ["Exit 0.40", "+0.217%", "2.46", "17.6 / 17.4 bp", "-0.46"],
]));
children.push(P([B("Result: the band does not rescue it. "), T("At the widest band turnover falls 29% (3.47 to 2.46) while gross falls 17%, so breakeven rises 2.5 bp: about a third of the 7.1 bp gap to a delivery round trip. Every configuration still loses money after cost, and the gain flattens (16.5, 16.8, 17.0, 17.6 bp) as each extra width keeps fewer good positions and more stale ones. Rebalancing every session is worse in every cell: it trades 0.83-1.61 a day against 0.49-0.69 for the 5-session book, earns less per unit traded (best breakeven 13.6 bp), and the raw sort rebalanced daily loses money before any cost. Nothing changes for the app: the reversal is real and not tradeable in cash delivery, and a band does nothing about Section 3.40's other constraint, that a cash short cannot be held overnight. Per-configuration results: tests/validation/trade_band.csv.")]));
children.push(H2("3.77 Prophet 1.5.0 - the requirements conflict removed at its source, and a band that holds 96%"));
children.push(P([B("The conflict. "), T("requirements.txt pinned prophet==1.2.2, which declares numpy<2.4.0 and pandas<3, against pins of numpy 2.4.6 and pandas 3.0.3 that cannot move down (SciPy 1.14+ needs numpy 2.x; Section 3.22). A plain pip install -r requirements.txt therefore failed with ResolutionImpossible. The project worked around it twice: dependencies.py installed Prophet separately with --no-deps, and fb_prophet_model.py patched a Prophet bug that breaks every fit on pandas 3.")]));
children.push(P([B("The source is gone. "), T("Prophet 1.3.0, 1.4.0 and 1.5.0 have been released since the pin, and from 1.3.0 the caps are gone (1.5.0 declares numpy>=1.21.6, pandas>=1.3.5). With prophet==1.5.0 a dry-run install of the whole file resolves, keeping numpy 2.4.6 and pandas 3.0.3; with 1.2.2 it still fails. Without the patch, 1.2.2 crashes on pandas 3 and 1.5.0 does not, so the bug is fixed upstream. The full suite passes on 1.5.0.")]));
children.push(P([B("What changed. "), T("requirements.txt pins prophet==1.5.0, and its header says both install routes work. dependencies.NO_DEPS is empty; the --no-deps path stays for the next package that over-constrains a pin, and its tests now inject their own example. The compat patch stays: on 1.5.0 it changes nothing, but run_app.py never replaces an installed package, so an environment still on 1.2.2 depends on it until it is upgraded with pip install prophet==1.5.0.")]));
children.push(P([B("A new Prophet version is a new model. "), T("On the same data 1.5.0 does not forecast like 1.2.2, and no setting brings the old behaviour back (the constructor defaults are identical). A one-off comparison ran both versions in throwaway environments sharing every other package: 20 liquid NSE names, an origin every 63 sessions from 2020 to mid-2024, 327 five-day forecasts. It was not kept as a driver, because 1.2.2 can no longer be installed under the pins.")]));
children.push(table([4360, 2500, 2500], [
  ["5-day forecast, 327 origins", "Prophet 1.2.2", "Prophet 1.5.0"],
  ["Mean absolute log error, against the random walk", "1.70x", "1.87x"],
  ["Paired difference, averaged by origin", "", "t -0.00 (worse on 12 of 23 origins)"],
  ["Direction right", "44.3%", "46.2%"],
  ["Native 95% band holds the outcome", "67.3%", "51.7%"],
  ["Median band width, share of price", "13.5%", "11.2%"],
]));
children.push(P("Point accuracy did not change in any way the data can detect; both versions are well behind the random walk. The two disagree a great deal from forecast to forecast (correlation 0.56). The native band, which the app showed as the 95% range, was narrower on 1.5.0 and covered half the outcomes. It was never good: the caveat in Section 6.2 already recorded about 0.7 on 1.2.2."));
children.push(P([B("The band is now calibrated on Prophet's own errors. "), T("fb_prophet_model.conformal_halfwidths uses the split-conformal rule the neural models already use (model_utils.forecast_nn_conformal). At 20 past origins, 5 sessions apart and ending one horizon before the last bar, Prophet is refitted exactly as for the forecast (same preparation, same 750-session window, but no uncertainty sampling, which is most of a fit's cost). The error at step h is |log(actual / predicted)| on the dates that actually traded, and the half-width is the finite-sample 95% quantile of the 20 errors: the largest, which a new error exceeds with probability 1/21 if the errors are exchangeable. The forecast keeps Prophet's point and wraps it in price x exp(+/- half-width); the native band stays in attrs['prophet'], and with too little history for 20 origins the native band is used and band_mode says so. A forecast now takes about 55 s instead of 5 s; calibrate=False turns the calibration off.")]));
children.push(P([B("Tested before it shipped "), T("(tests/validation/run_prophet_coverage.py, written down before running): the 17 of the 20 most liquid names that trade on every session, 8 origins each from July 2021 to June 2024 (before the reserved period), every forecast through the app's own function and scored on the next 5 prices. The bar was Section 3.70's: pooled coverage of at least 90%.")]));
children.push(table([2900, 1800, 2860, 1800], [
  ["Same 680 forecast-steps", "Pooled coverage", "Wilson 95% (on the 136 origins)", "Mean width"],
  ["Conformal (shipped)", "96.3%", "91.7% - 98.4%", "27.5%"],
  ["Prophet's native band", "73.2%", "65.2% - 80.0%", "13.0%"],
]));
children.push(P("It passes, and coverage holds at every step (95.6, 97.8, 94.9, 96.3 and 97.1% for steps 1 to 5, where the native band falls from 85% to 67%). The price is a band about twice as wide, which is what Prophet's errors are: its point forecast drifts away from the last price, so the honest 95% range at five sessions is about +/-13%. The native coverage here (67% at step 5) is higher than in the comparison above (52%) because this window starts in July 2021 and leaves out the 2020 crash. Three tests (test_prophet_band.py) replace Prophet with a stand-in that predicts the last price: the half-width is the largest of the 20 real errors, the forecast's band is replaced and the native one kept, and too short a history keeps Prophet's own band. With the installer test, that brought the suite to 745."));
children.push(P("Not re-run: the published Prophet figures from run_full_backtest.py and run_multi_backtest.py (the model table, and the caveat's coverage of about 0.7). Both fetch live yfinance prices up to today and so read the reserved period. They describe 1.2.2's native band."));
children.push(H2("3.78 The forward prediction log had never scored a forecast - a pandas 3 date bug, and a monitor that runs on its own"));
children.push(P("Section 3.29's forward log is the project's one source of evidence that nobody could have selected. It scores the forecasts the app actually served, once their outcomes exist. It is also the only such evidence that does not spend the reserved period: it reads only the prices on the target dates of forecasts already served, with no origins chosen and nothing refitted. With the research arc run its course (Sections 3.74 and 3.76 were both negative), the next step was to make the forward log routine."));
children.push(P([B("It had never resolved a single row. "), T("The log held 38 rows from three runs (3M India on 22 August 2026; 20 Microns and ITC on 21 October). Every target date had passed, and none had an outcome. run_drift_report.py --resolve, the command Section 3.29 says to run, crashed with a pandas MergeError: 'incompatible merge keys ... datetime64[us] and datetime64[s]'. pandas 3 keeps a datetime at whatever resolution its source had; the log's ISO strings parse to microseconds, the fetched history arrived in seconds, and merge_asof refuses mismatched keys. So on this stack the drift alert, the live gate validation and the paper portfolio had never had data. The tests passed because their fixtures build both sides the same way.")]));
children.push(P([B("Fix. "), T("prediction_log._wall_clock puts the log's dates and the feed's dates on one footing: tz-naive, at nanosecond resolution. A timezone is dropped, not converted: a daily NSE bar stamped 00:00+05:30 is that session, and converting it to UTC would move it to the previous day. A new test feeds a history at second resolution in Asia/Kolkata time; it fails on the old code and passes on the new one. The suite was then 746.")]));
children.push(P([B("The first live read. "), T("All 38 resolved. The point is that the numbers now exist, not what they say:")]));
children.push(table([3360, 2000, 2000, 2000], [
  ["Model", "Resolved", "95% band held", "Direction right"],
  ["LightGBM (ML)", "10", "90%", "40%"],
  ["XGBoost (ML)", "10", "90%", "30%"],
  ["ARIMA-GARCH", "5", "100%", "20%"],
  ["ARIMAX-GARCH", "5", "100%", "40%"],
  ["Prophet", "5", "100%", "20%"],
  ["LSTM (DL)", "3", "100%", "67%"],
]));
children.push(P("Every model is below the 30 points per model the drift alert needs before it may fire, so no alert fires and no verdict is drawn. The confidence gate put 33 forecasts in 'No edge' (36% direction right) and 5 in 'Low' (20%); the report says the gate did not sort realised skill, but on three runs that is no evidence either way. The paper portfolio has too few final-step forecasts to trade. These are 1.2.2-era Prophet forecasts, served before Section 3.77."));
children.push(P([B("Routine. "), T("run_monitor.bat (double-click) runs run_drift_report.py --resolve --save. It fetches the prices that have arrived since, fills in matured outcomes, and writes the report to .prediction_log/reports/report_YYYYMMDD.txt, one file per day, so the live record keeps its own history; a second run on the same day changes nothing in the log. Daily after the close is the natural cadence, with a read once a week. In Windows Task Scheduler, call Python directly (the batch file pauses on an error, which would hang an unattended run):")]));
children.push(Code('schtasks /Create /TN "Stock Prediction monitor" /SC DAILY /ST 18:30 /TR "\\"C:\\path\\to\\python.exe\\" \\"C:\\path\\to\\Stock Prediction\\tests\\validation\\run_drift_report.py\\" --resolve --save"'));
children.push(P("It was not created automatically, because it changes the machine rather than the project. 18:30 IST is three hours after the NSE close, so the day's bar has printed."));
children.push(P([B("The limit is how much the app is used, not how often the monitor runs. "), T("Evidence grows only as forecasts are served: three runs in ten weeks. A five-day forecast adds five resolved points for each model in the run, so a model needs about six runs that include it to reach the 30 points an alert needs: at this rate, months rather than weeks. The faster way would be a fixed watchlist, chosen in advance and forecast on a schedule through the app's own pipeline, which keeps the no-selection property. (Later: built, Section 3.79.)")]));
children.push(H2("3.79 A fixed watchlist, forecast every week - the forward record no longer waits for the app to be used"));
children.push(P("Section 3.78 ended on the forward log's real limit: it grows only as fast as people use the app, three runs in ten weeks so far. run_watchlist.py fills that gap. Every week it forecasts a fixed list of companies through the app's own pipeline and logs the results like any served forecast. The list was chosen by a rule before the first forecast, so nothing in the record can be picked after the fact: every name, every week and every model is in it."));
children.push(P([B("The app's pipeline, without the page. "), T("The serving steps (fetch, run each model, the walk-forward confidence check, the novelty check) lived only inside UI.py, wrapped in Streamlit's cache, and importing UI.py draws the page, so no other program could call them. They now live in serving.py, with no Streamlit: the model registry, fetch_history, run_model (with Section 3.71's monotone band), is_usable, model_confidence and regime_novelty. UI.py's cached functions call these and render what they return, so the page and the watchlist serve a forecast the same way. The UI's interaction scenarios (tools/ui_check.py scenarios) all pass after the change.")]));
children.push(P([B("The watchlist, fixed by rule on 3 November 2026. "), T("watchlist.json holds the list, the rule, the horizon (5 sessions, daily) and the model set (all 20). The rule: the 10 NSE equities with the highest median daily turnover over January-June 2024 in the bhavcopy cache (before the reserved period), among those that traded on every session of that window. One mechanical exclusion was added before any forecast: a name whose NSE symbol has changed since may not have a continuous price history. That drops Tata Motors (now TMPV after the October 2025 demerger) and brings in the 11th name. The list: HDFC Bank, ICICI Bank, Reliance Industries, State Bank of India, Infosys, Axis Bank, Kotak Mahindra Bank, Larsen & Toubro, Jio Financial Services, Vodafone Idea. The file says not to edit it; a change means a new file with a new date, reported separately.")]));
children.push(P([B("What a run does. "), T("For each company, in the order the app's Submit uses: fetch the history (a daily bar dated today is dropped before 16:00 IST, so a run during market hours forecasts from the last close); run every model and keep the usable forecasts; take the anchor price; run the data-quality gate and the walk-forward confidence check; apply the data-quality override, then the novelty gate (it ships off, so the verdict is 'unknown', as in the app); log each forecast with source='watchlist' and a 'wl-' run id. A company already logged from the same origin is skipped. Companies run in parallel worker processes (--jobs, default 3), but only the main process writes the log. If a worker dies, the companies left over run one at a time, so the week is not lost. --dry-run does everything except logging, and run_drift_report.py now counts rows by source.")]));
children.push(P([B("Cost. "), T("One company takes about an hour: 20 models plus their walk-forward confidence checks, most of it the checks. Three companies in parallel took 118 minutes, with free memory falling to 3.0 of 31 GB. The full list of 10 takes about 6-7 hours with the default 3 workers, which is why it runs on Saturday morning; on a machine with less memory, use --jobs 2 or 1. Keep the computer awake for the run: the first one took 883 minutes because the laptop went into standby overnight, while nine of the ten companies were done in 306 (Section 3.124). Chronos-2 fails on this machine because its weights are not at the configured local path; the run records the failure and continues with the other 19 models, as the app does.")]));
children.push(P([B("Scheduling. "), T("Two Windows Task Scheduler entries make the routine: the weekly forecast on Saturday morning, after Friday's close, so successive 5-session forecasts do not overlap, and the daily monitor. From a command prompt, with the paths adjusted:")]));
children.push(Code('schtasks /Create /TN "Stock Prediction watchlist" /SC WEEKLY /D SAT /ST 06:00 /TR "\\"C:\\path\\to\\python.exe\\" \\"C:\\path\\to\\Stock Prediction\\run_watchlist.py\\""'));
children.push(Code('schtasks /Create /TN "Stock Prediction monitor" /SC DAILY /ST 18:30 /TR "\\"C:\\path\\to\\python.exe\\" \\"C:\\path\\to\\Stock Prediction\\tests\\validation\\run_drift_report.py\\" --resolve --save"'));
children.push(P("The tasks were not created for you: creating a scheduled task is a change to the machine, and the session's permissions refused it. schtasks /Delete /TN \"<name>\" removes either one. run_watchlist.bat and run_monitor.bat run the same jobs by double-click."));
children.push(P([B("What it buys. "), T("Each week adds about 950 forecast-steps (10 companies x 19 working models x 5 steps). Every model gets 50 resolved points a week, past the drift alert's floor of 30 after the first week, against months at the app's own pace. The confidence gate's live validation gains 190 forecasts a week, each served before its outcome existed. Eight tests (test_watchlist.py) replace the network and the models: every usable model is logged with its gates and its source and an unusable one reported; degraded data and an unprecedented regime withhold the call; the same origin is never logged twice; a dry run logs nothing; a dead worker pool falls back to running in process; a session still open is not the origin; and the frozen file is complete. The suite was then 754.")]));
children.push(H2("3.80 A GPU machine ran on the CPU - what Windows allows, and a device check that asked only TensorFlow"));
children.push(P("The owner ran the app on a machine with an NVIDIA GPU, and everything ran on the CPU. Three separate things were behind it, and only one was a code bug."));
children.push(P([B("1. TensorFlow has no GPU on native Windows. "), T("Since TensorFlow 2.11 (the pin is 2.21), GPU support on Windows exists only inside WSL2; TensorFlow says so itself when it starts. Every Keras model (LSTM, GRU, Transformer, TCN, N-BEATS, CNN-LSTM, Hybrid, Vol-LSTM) therefore runs on the CPU on native Windows, whatever GPU is installed. This is not fixable in this code: the options are WSL2 with pip install \"tensorflow[and-cuda]\", or the CPU.")]));
children.push(P([B("2. pip installs PyTorch's CPU build on Windows. "), T("Chronos-2, the one PyTorch model, already picks cuda when torch.cuda.is_available(). But PyTorch comes unpinned with chronos-forecasting, and its PyPI wheel for Windows is CPU-only: this machine has torch 2.12.0+cpu, and the GPU machine almost certainly got the same through run_app.py. The CUDA build comes from PyTorch's own index, matched to the driver (https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/, e.g. pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cuXXX). It was not changed here, because this machine has no NVIDIA GPU to test a CUDA build on.")]));
children.push(P([B("3. The bug: the device check asked only TensorFlow. "), T("model_utils.configure_compute looked at TensorFlow's GPU list alone, which on native Windows is always empty. So the sidebar said 'CPU', the console 'no GPU detected', the caption promised that deep models and Chronos auto-use a GPU (not true on Windows), and nothing said why or what to install. model_utils.compute_status now checks TensorFlow's GPUs, PyTorch's CUDA, and whether an NVIDIA driver is present (nvidia-smi on the PATH), separately. The label says what runs where - 'CPU', 'GPU for Chronos-2 (PyTorch); CPU for the TensorFlow models', 'GPU (TensorFlow and PyTorch)', or 'CPU (an NVIDIA GPU is present but unused)' - and when a GPU is there but unused, the sidebar and the console say which framework cannot use it and how to change that. The gradient-boosted trees stay on the CPU on purpose.")]));
children.push(P("Four tests (test_compute_status.py) replace the probes, so they run the same on any machine: a GPU machine on CPU builds is told why, CUDA PyTorch puts Chronos-2 on the GPU, both frameworks on the GPU need no hint, and a machine with no GPU says 'CPU' and nothing else. This machine reports CPU (no NVIDIA driver). The suite was then 758."));
children.push(P([B("Chronos-2's weights folder is also per machine. "), T("The path in chronos_model.py is this machine's OneDrive folder, so on another machine Chronos-2 fails to load. Set STOCKAPP_CHRONOS_PATH to the folder there; the default is unchanged.")]));
children.push(H2("3.81 Hourly charts ran backwards - Plotly's hidden-time rules, replaced by one slot per bar"));
children.push(P("The owner found that on hourly charts the history line crossed itself: it ran right, fell, then came back from the left across the chart, and some date labels sat on top of each other. It reproduced offline on 20 Microns' hourly bars. The data was not the cause: the bars were IST wall-clock times, in order, with no duplicates, seven a session from 09:15 to 15:15."));
children.push(P([B("The axis was. "), T("Section 3.71 put sessions side by side by hiding time with two Plotly rangebreaks: an hour pattern (15:30 to 09:15) and a list of day values (weekends and holidays). With both in force, Plotly placed whole sessions in the wrong slots, so a line drawn through bars in time order ran back across the chart.")]));
children.push(P([B("Fix. "), T("charts._bar_axis puts hourly charts on a category axis: each bar takes one evenly spaced slot, in time order, labelled with its date and time, with a tick at the first bar of each session (thinned to about eight). The last-close marker and its label are moved onto the same slots. There is no hidden time left to get wrong. The line, candle and all-models charts were checked by rendering them again; daily charts keep the day-value rangebreak, which on its own lays out correctly. A new test checks, for both chart types on a Monday-to-Friday hourly history with a forecast after the weekend, that the axis is a category axis with no rangebreaks, that there is one slot per bar, that the history never runs backwards, and that the last-close line sits on its own bar. It replaces an assertion that had pinned the hour-pattern rangebreak itself.")]));
children.push(H2("3.82 Listing and dividends for the selected company"));
children.push(P("A new 'Listing and dividends (NSE)' panel sits under the company check. Listing: the date the company listed on NSE (and how long ago), its face value, ISIN and series, from NSE's equity listing file, the one the company list is built from. Dividends: the trailing-12-month dividend per share and its yield at the last price, the number of dividends in five years, the next announced dividend if any, and a table of the last 12 with ex-date, amount and NSE's description."));
children.push(P([B("Source and speed. "), T("NSE's corporate-action feed through bhavcopy.corporate_actions, with no snapshot because the panel wants NSE's latest records. Five years of actions for every company are fetched once and cached for six hours: the first company of the day takes about 16 seconds, every other one is instant. Amounts are per share as declared; for the yield, each dividend is first divided by any split or bonus that went ex after it, so a split does not inflate a yield measured against today's price. Checked live: Infosys has 10 dividends in five years, ITC 10, 20 Microns 4.")]));
children.push(P([B("Not shown: the IPO issue price. "), T("It is in neither NSE source the app reads; NSE publishes it only through its IPO archive, an unofficial endpoint, and the owner chose to leave it out. Three tests cover the listing lookup, the split adjustment (a dividend before a 1:2 split counts at half for the yield) and the empty cases.")]));

children.push(H2("3.83 The forward record, run from the app instead of scheduled tasks"));
children.push(P("Sections 3.78 and 3.79 left two routines to Windows scheduled tasks: scoring matured forecasts (run_monitor.bat) and the weekly watchlist forecast (run_watchlist.bat). The owner chose not to set those up. Without them the forward record would grow only when someone remembered, so the app now does both."));
children.push(P([B("The Forward record panel. "), T("It sits in the sidebar under Submit and Reset, and its title says whether the record is up to date, the watchlist is due, or a run is in progress. It shows how many forecasts are logged, scored and still waiting. 'Score matured forecasts' fetches the prices that have arrived and fills in every outcome whose date has passed: the same resolve_outcomes the monitor runs, safe to click any number of times. 'Run this week's watchlist' starts run_watchlist.py in its own process, with its output in .prediction_log/watchlist_runs/; the app stays usable and the run carries on if the browser tab is closed. The button is enabled once the last watchlist forecast is 7 days old, and while a run is alive the panel shows its latest progress instead, so a second run cannot be started. run_watchlist.py also skips any company already logged for the same origin. The best time to click is after the Friday close. Nothing is scheduled and nothing is left running; the code is forward_record.py, and the .bat files still work.")]));
children.push(P([B("A gap closed on the way. "), T("resolve_outcomes reads the log, fetches prices for a few seconds, then rewrites the whole file. A forecast appended in those seconds, by a Submit or a running watchlist, would have been dropped. The log is append-only, so the rewrite now first re-reads it and keeps every row past the ones it started from. Three tests (test_forward_record.py) cover the status and when the watchlist is due, a launch that will not start a second run, and a forecast appended during scoring surviving it. The suite was then 768.")]));
children.push(H2("3.84 Forecasts up to 90 days - the range is the forecast, four models are left out, and a Vol-LSTM band that exploded"));
children.push(P("The owner asked for forecasts up to 90 days. Every skill measurement here is at about five sessions and the up/down call has no reliable edge even there, so the design is range-first: daily horizons of 20, 40, 60 and 90 trading days, where the 95% range is the forecast; hourly stays at most five days. Beyond 13 sessions gating.apply_horizon_gate sets every call to No edge ('the range is the forecast'), the walk-forward confidence check is skipped, a banner says to read the range and not the median line, and the models that failed the coverage test below are not run, with a note saying why (gating.long_horizon_models)."));
children.push(P([B("The coverage test. "), T("run_long_horizon_coverage.py ran every model through serving.run_model for 90 sessions on 8 liquid NSE names (HDFC Bank, Hero MotoCorp, IOC, Kotak Bank, L&T, SBI, Sun Pharma, Yes Bank) at 4 origins from January 2021 to February 2024, before the reserved period, scored at steps 20, 40, 60 and 90. The rule, written down first: a model keeps its band beyond 13 sessions only if pooled coverage is at least 90%, the bar of Sections 3.70 and 3.77. The first run lost all its work when the driver's time limit ended it with nothing saved; the script now saves each forecast and resumes. Twelve models covered 96-99% (median 90-session widths 58-89% of the price); ARIMAX-GARCH 93.0%, XGBoost and Stacking 91.4%. Left out: LightGBM 88.3%, Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM 88.3%, Prophet 87.5% (median width 127%) and Regime Switching 81.2% (the narrowest band, 72% at 20 sessions). Update: Regime Switching came back after its range was fixed (Section 3.89), and LightGBM with larger leaves and a shrunk centre (Section 3.98).")]));
children.push(P([B("Vol-LSTM passed with an absurd band. "), T("Its 90.6% cleared the bar, but its mean width at 90 sessions was 6.5 billion percent: in 3 of 32 forecasts the band exploded (2 x 10^11 % for HDFC Bank from January 2021) and in one it collapsed to 0%. At the app's ordinary horizons the same histories gave two absurd bands: HDFC Bank 572% at five sessions and 91 million percent at 13, Sun Pharma 325% and 559%; the other 30 had medians of 14.6% and 25.5%. The data was clean (the 2019 split adjusted, the largest moves the real 2020 crash). The network's per-step volatility swung far from anything realised, 5.5-8.3% a day for a stock moving 1.8%; in calibration the typical forecast was three times too high while 5% were twelve times too low, so the conformal quantile took the bad tail and the multiplier came out at 13 (normal about 2). Static and adaptive conformal both failed.")]));
children.push(P([B("The fix. "), T("vol_lstm_model._guard_reason replaces a fitted cone with the EWMA one (normal multiplier, the short-history path) when any step's forecast volatility is above 4x or below a quarter of the realised volatility of the last 60 returns, any multiplier is above 6, or anything is non-finite; the thresholds were set before their effect was measured and the reason is recorded in the run. run_vol_lstm_guard.py re-ran the guarded model on the same 32 forecasts under rules written first: the widest band fell to 67% at five sessions and 66% at 13 (rule: at most 100%), coverage stayed 93.8% and 96.9% (at least 90%), the guard fired on exactly the two broken forecasts (at most 3), and at 90 sessions pooled coverage was 93.8% with the widest band 166%, so Vol-LSTM keeps its band, now a real one. Tests: the long-horizon gate and the four exclusions (test_gates.py), the guard's cases (test_vol_lstm_guard.py), and the EWMA band being what is served when it fires (test_new_models_smoke.py).")]));
children.push(H2("3.85 Two paper screens, an X channel and an interview guide - and volatility-scaled bands, better in a crash and worse the rest of the time"));
children.push(P("Papers with Code's time-series forecasting task (506 papers, 210 with code) and time-series classification task (112 in the feed, 48 with code) were read in full; each paper's group and decision is in papers_tsf_screen.csv and papers_tsc_screen.csv. No forecasting benchmark uses stock prices; the one financial dataset, Exchange (currency rates), is topped by a linear model. Two finance papers (Re(Visiting) Time Series Foundation Models in Finance, arXiv 2511.18578; Forecast Collapse in Time-Series Foundation Models, arXiv 2608.14106) agree with Chronos-2's result here, so no further foundation models were added. The online-conformal papers are versions of ACI, which Section 3.72 rejected. The classification papers label whole recordings, and their nearest ideas are price-pattern features of the kind Sections 3.66 and 3.68 found no edge in. One idea was untested: scaling errors by volatility (ResCP, arXiv 2510.05060; VolaRM)."));
children.push(P([B("The X channel and the interview guide. "), T("@quantscience_, a promotional account for a paid course, was read through Thread Reader: momentum and moving-average rules (Section 3.53), trailing stops (Section 3.63), regime and ARCH models (already in the app), an XGBoost factor pipeline (Section 3.39 on) and a list of libraries the project's tools already replace. Its one specific strategy, Zarattini and Antonacci's industry trend-following, failed an independent replication (arXiv 2412.14361: negative recent alpha, trailing the market walk-forward). The MIT Sloan Business Club's Quant Bible (51 pages) is an interview-prep guide: statistics and regression basics already here, interview cases, market making and puzzles; its Kelly criterion was declined in Sections 3.51 and 3.53 for want of an edge. Nothing was taken from either.")]));
children.push(P([B("The test. "), T("run_vol_bands.py is Section 3.72's harness (200 names, every session July 2018 to December 2024, naive-forecast errors, 190 calibration windows, CC = mean |coverage - 95%| over five volatility-shift quintiles) with one change: each error is divided by the RiskMetrics EWMA volatility at its origin (lambda 0.94, fixed in advance) and the band is the conformal quantile times today's volatility. Rules written first, at five sessions: on 2022-24 CC improves with a bootstrap interval excluding zero, coverage within half a point, width at most 15% wider; and coverage in the 2020 crash higher. Results: coverage 95.0% for both, width 22.7% vs 20.6%, crash coverage 89.3% vs 80.9%, but CC doubled from 0.009 to 0.020 (interval +0.005 to +0.016). It overcorrects: 92% coverage just after volatility falls, 98% just after it rises, because volatility mean-reverts and today's reading does not. It was also fragile: 1.2% of ten-session windows got bands over 200% wide at names with collapses, suspensions or demergers. Not shipped. The suite was then 774.")]));
children.push(H2("3.86 A sanity check on every served range - what coverage could not see"));
children.push(P("Vol-LSTM's exploding bands (Section 3.84) went unseen because every earlier check looked at coverage, and a band wide enough covers everything. serving.band_sanity compares each served range with a random walk's 95% band from the same stock, exp(+-1.96 * sd * sqrt(h)) around the last price with sd from the last 60 returns in the history's own bar units. A range more than 4 times wider or narrower gets a warning on that model's card ('Implausible range ... Nx the width this stock's own volatility implies') and a console line. Nothing is withheld: it is a flag, not a gate."));
children.push(P([B("Calibrated before shipping "), T("on the long-horizon test's 32 saved forecasts, each band set against its own history's volatility. The 15 healthy models had median ratios of 0.93-1.58 at 20 sessions and 0.95-1.38 at 90, and were flagged on 0-1 of 32 at 20 sessions and 0-2 at 90, about 0.6% of healthy forecasts at 20 sessions. Vol-LSTM before the guard was flagged on 4 and 4 (its blow-ups and the collapsed band), with the guard on none. Prophet (median 2.12 at 90 sessions, flagged 8) and Regime Switching (0.61, its under-coverage seen from the other side) are both left out beyond 13 sessions. Four tests (test_band_sanity.py): honest and within-limit bands pass, far wider or narrower ones are flagged, the same relative width is honest for a volatile stock and flagged for a calm one, and missing or non-finite inputs give nothing. The suite was then 778.")]));
children.push(H2("3.87 No other model's five-session range is broken - but Regime Switching's holds only 62% of outcomes"));
children.push(P("Vol-LSTM's range blew up at five sessions (Section 3.84) and coverage could not see it, so every model was run at the app's default five sessions on the long-horizon test's 32 forecasts (8 NSE names x 4 origins, 2021-2024) and each range put through the sanity check of Section 3.86. Written down before running (run_band_audit.py): a forecast is broken when band_sanity flags it, and a model flagged on more than 2 of 32 is investigated."));
children.push(P([B("No model is flagged on more than 2 of 32. "), T("Coverage at five sessions: Hybrid 100%; ARIMA-GARCH and Prophet 96.9%; LSTM, GRU, XGBoost, LightGBM, Transformer, Chronos-2, TCN, CNN-LSTM, Stacking, Quantile, Meta-Ensemble, HAR-RV and Vol-LSTM (with its guard, widest range 49%) 93.8%; ARIMAX-GARCH, N-BEATS and Kalman 90.6%. Median width ratios 1.04-1.25, Prophet 2.16. HAR-RV was flagged once (YESBANK from 2021-01-01, 5.8 times the reference, 155% wide: its monthly volatility term still carried the 2020 collapse while the last 60 sessions had calmed). Stacking on SBIN from 2022-01-14, which had a 286% range at 90 sessions in the data used to calibrate Section 3.86's check, is 18% wide at five sessions and held the price; the blow-up was at 90 sessions only.")]));
children.push(P([B("What the flag cannot see: Regime Switching. "), T("Its five-session range held only 20 of 32 outcomes (62.5%; 75%, 62.5%, 50% and 62.5% at the four origins), with a median width 0.61 times the reference and the narrowest 0.26, just inside the 1/4 limit, so the flag never fires. It is already left out beyond 13 sessions for the same fault (Section 3.84), but at the default five sessions it is served with a range that misses about a third of the time. That is a coverage failure the written rule does not act on, so nothing was changed; it is the next investigation. Update: the cause was a bug (the model never used its own fit), fixed in Section 3.89.")]));
children.push(H2("3.88 Cutoff checks on recent data - every model from October 2026, and batches you can repeat"));
children.push(P("The owner's idea: cut the data at a recent past date, forecast with every model, and score the forecasts against the prices that followed. Every earlier model test stops at June 2024 because 2025 onward is reserved (Section 3.22). On 2026-11-06 the owner approved opening it for this, only for cutoffs from 2026-10-01; every opening is written to holdout_ledger.csv, and January 2025 to September 2026 stays unread. Report only: no model, gate or threshold is changed because of these checks."));
children.push(P([B("The October test "), T("(run_oct2026_check.py): 20 NSE names drawn with a fixed seed from the 40 most liquid that traded every session from 2018; cutoffs Thursday 8, 15, 22 and 29 October 2026, five sessions each, meant to be non-overlapping; every model through serving.run_model under the offline guard, so none can fetch a later price. Reference: a random walk with the 95% range exp(+-1.96 sd sqrt(h)) from the last 60 returns. Scored at day 5: price error against no change, direction (a forecast within 0.01% is flat), range coverage, width and the interval score (width plus 40 times any miss). The week is the unit: better or worse only if so in all four weeks, under 90% coverage is too narrow, direction skill needs the Wilson lower bound above 50%. Its Regime Switching and Prophet rows measure those models before the changes of Sections 3.89 and 3.90. run_multi_backtest.py was not used: it spreads cutoffs over 700 days (the whole reserved period), downloads from yfinance and covers 10-12 models called directly.")]));
children.push(P([B("October results. "), T("Finished 7 November (1,252 minutes; all 80 forecasts from every model). Median five-session move +2.8%, -2.3%, +1.3%, +5.9%. Error against no change: ARIMAX-GARCH 0.93, LSTM 0.95, GRU, TCN and Stacking 0.96, most others 0.97-1.03, Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM 1.07, Prophet 1.41. No model beat no change in all four weeks; ARIMAX and LSTM were worse in the falling week of 15 October (1.10, 1.05). Prophet (the configuration before Section 3.90) was worse in all four (1.01, 2.07, 1.77, 1.18). The reference range held only 85% (60% in the week of 29 October, when the median stock rose 5.9%), so 16 of 20 models were flagged narrow; only Prophet (94%, 25.6% wide), N-BEATS, Stacking and HAR-RV held 90% or more. LSTM, TCN, Stacking and GRU had the best interval scores (about 25% against the reference 30%), but none was better in all four weeks. Regime Switching (before Section 3.89) held 65% at 8.7% wide and was worse than the reference in all four weeks. Direction: LSTM 72%, TCN and ARIMAX 71% pass the Wilson rule, but 71% of outcomes were rises and LSTM, GRU and TCN forecast a rise 99-100% of the time, so always-up would have scored the same; in the week of 15 October they hit 25-30%. 20 October was a holiday (Dussehra), so the 15 October window ended on 23 October and shares the 22-23 October move with the 22 October window; cutoff_check.py counts three independent batches and gives no verdict, and the Prophet and Regime verdicts should be read with that. Nothing is changed because of these results. The four weeks are loaded into the cutoff tool as its first batches (import-oct, day 5 only). Section 3.91 runs the fixed Regime Switching and Prophet on these cutoffs.")]));
children.push(P([B("Cutoff checks you can repeat "), T("(tools/cutoff_check.py: run --horizon N [--cutoff] [--stocks] [--models], status, summary [--batch], import-oct). One batch is one cutoff date: every chosen model and stock forecasts N sessions and is scored on every day 1..N, so one five-day batch also gives the two- and three-day results. Batches by horizon were considered and not used: a day apart they would be scored on the same market days and train every model again. The cutoff is chosen so every scored day has a price; one before 2026-10-01 is refused. Missing NSE daily files are downloaded first, then the batch runs under the offline guard and is saved stock by stock, so it resumes. Files in tests/validation/cutoff_checks/: batches.csv (cutoff, horizon, stocks, models, times, status, progress), <batch>_parts.csv (every stock, model and day) and <batch>.csv. One batch gives no better/worse verdict; across batches only non-overlapping ones count, and a verdict needs at least four with the same result in every one. A two-stock smoke batch was run into a scratch folder to test the tool; its results are not used. Six tests (test_cutoff_check.py). The suite was then 784.")]));
children.push(H2("3.89 Regime Switching never used its own fit - a fixed 1% volatility for every stock, now fixed"));
children.push(P("Section 3.87 found Regime Switching's five-session range holding only 62.5% of outcomes at 0.61 times the reference width. The cause was a bug. The Markov switching model is fitted on a NumPy array, so its params are an array, and the simulation looked each regime's mean and variance up by name (params['sigma2[0]']). That always failed, and the code fell back to a mean of 0 and a standard deviation of 1% a day for every regime of every stock: two different stocks got the same range to the decimal (8.75% wide at five days, 2 x 1.96 x 1% x sqrt(5)). Every earlier Regime Switching figure, range and price, measured those defaults, including Section 3.84's 81% at 20-90 sessions, Section 3.87's 62.5% and the October test of Section 3.88."));
children.push(P([B("The fix "), T("(RegimeSwitching_model.regime_parameters): parameters are read by position in model.param_names, and a missing one or a non-finite or non-positive variance is now an error, not a default. The simulation draws from its own generator instead of reseeding NumPy's global state, which changed what every later model drew (the fit was cleared of the same fault in Section 3.69). HDFCBANK from 2023-01-25 now gets a five-day range 14.6% wide and HEROMOTOCO from 2022-01-14 15.3%, from their own regimes (about 1.4% and 3.7-3.9% a day).")]));
children.push(P([B("Tested before shipping "), T("(run_regime_fix.py) on the same 32 pre-2025 forecasts, rules written down first: at five sessions at least 90% coverage and at most 2 flags; at 20-90 sessions at least 90% pooled coverage to come off the long-horizon exclusion list. Before -> after: coverage at 5 sessions 62.5% -> 100%, median width ratio 0.61 -> 1.19, mean width 8.7% -> 18.8%, flags 0 -> 0, price error 1.01 times no change; pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions 81.2% -> 98.4% (96.9/96.9/100/100% at 20/40/60/90). Both rules pass: it is taken off gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED (Prophet, LightGBM and Hybrid stay; LightGBM came back in Section 3.98), and its five-session range sits with the others'. The 100% is no sign of skill: the range is about a fifth wider than the reference and the price forecast is close to no change. The simulation still uses only each regime's mean and volatility, not the two fitted lags. The October test's Regime Switching rows measure the old default: its workers loaded the model before the change. Four new tests (test_regime_switching.py); test_gates.py keeps Regime Switching beyond 13 sessions and ui_check.py's 60-day scenario uses LightGBM. The suite was then 788.")]));
children.push(H2("3.90 Prophet forecasts worse than no change at every horizon - a better version, and off by default"));
children.push(P("Section 3.87 found Prophet's five-session range 2.16 times the reference, and Section 3.84 left it out beyond 13 sessions (87.5% at 20-90). Its range is split-conformal (Section 3.77), so a wide range means large errors. On the 32 diagnosis forecasts its error was 2.67, 2.07, 1.70 and 2.92 times the no-change error at days 1, 5, 20 and 90. Two causes: it forecasts from its smooth fitted curve, which sat 2.9% from the last price on average (6-10% for YESBANK at three of four origins), so that gap was error from the first step; and the trend and monthly, quarterly and yearly cycles it projects make the forecast worse at every horizon. Six configurations were tried; none beats no change anywhere. The least bad, started from the last price with a stiffer trend and no monthly or quarterly seasonality, scored 1.23, 1.18, 1.18 and 1.84; a flat trend with weekly seasonality only reached 1.10 at day 90 but 2.48 at day 1."));
children.push(P([B("The change "), T("(fb_prophet_model, old configuration kept as LEGACY): anchor_shift starts the forecast from the last price, keeping only Prophet's predicted change; changepoint_prior_scale 0.01 instead of 0.1; no monthly or quarterly seasonality; the conformal band is calibrated on the new forecast's own errors. Judged on forecasts it was not chosen on (run_prophet_fix.py): eight other names (BAJFINANCE, BANKBARODA, BHARTIARTL, HINDPETRO, INFY, ITC, TATASTEEL, VEDL) at 1 June 2020, 23 August 2021, 16 November 2022 and 13 February 2024 (that last date is also a diagnosis origin; the names all differ). Rules written down first: five-session error lower pooled and in at least 3 of 4 origins; five-session coverage at least 90%; 20-90 session coverage at least 90% to leave the exclusion list.")]));
children.push(P([B("Result. "), T("Error against no change, old -> new: day 1 2.21 -> 0.97, day 5 1.84 -> 1.27, day 20 2.08 -> 1.55, day 90 2.19 -> 1.96; lower in all four origins at day 5 (2.10/1.51/2.73/1.24 -> 1.26/0.99/2.44/1.05). Five-session coverage 96.9% -> 100%, mean width 36.4% -> 33.0%, median width ratio 1.73 -> 1.42, interval score 41.5% -> 33.0%. Rules 1 and 2 pass and the new version ships; it is still worse than no change after the first day. Rule 3 fails (89.1% at 20-90 sessions; the old version had 90.6% with wider ranges), so Prophet stays excluded beyond 13 sessions. Because even its best configuration loses to no change, Prophet is listed but no longer selected at start-up or by Reset (serving.OFF_BY_DEFAULT, DEFAULT_MODELS; 19 of 20 selected), and its card says why. No ensemble uses it; the weekly watchlist still runs it, so the forward record keeps measuring it. Update: the owner reversed this on 2026-11-08 (Section 3.93); Prophet is selected by default again, with its note kept. The October test's Prophet rows measure the old configuration. Three new tests (test_prophet_band.py), and ui_check.py checks that Reset leaves Prophet unselected. The suite was then 791.")]));
children.push(H2("3.91 The fixed Regime Switching and Prophet on October 2026, and a fairer bar for direction"));
children.push(P("Section 3.88's October test ran Regime Switching and Prophet as they were before Sections 3.89 and 3.90. run_oct2026_refit.py runs them as they are now on the same 20 stocks, four cutoffs and five sessions, within the owner's approval (cutoffs from 2026-10-01, report only, in the ledger). Both changes were chosen on pre-2025 data, so this is a report on new data; the random-walk reference is checked to match Section 3.88's exactly. Rules written down first: Regime's fix holds if its range covers at least as many outcomes as the reference and its interval score is lower than the old one's in at least 3 of 4 weeks; Prophet's change holds if its error is lower over the 80 and in at least 3 of 4 weeks. A drift reference (the random walk plus the mean of the last 60 log returns) is scored too."));
children.push(P([B("Result. "), T("Regime Switching: coverage 65% -> 88% (reference 85%), width 8.7% -> 15.1%, interval score 53.7% -> 30.4% (reference 30.1%), lower in all four weeks; rule R holds. Its new direction calls hit 61%, below the constant call of 71%. Prophet: error against no change 1.41 -> 1.09, lower in 3 of 4 weeks (worse in the week of 8 October, 1.10 against 1.01); rule P holds, but it is still worse than no change and stays off by default. Its range narrowed from 25.6% to 20.9% and held 92%, with an interval score of 26.0%, below the reference. The drift reference called direction worse than chance (41%; 30-40% in three weeks). The 15 and 22 October windows share one session, so neither rule is strong evidence. Nothing changes because of the result.")]));
children.push(P([B("A fairer bar for direction. "), T("In October 71% of outcomes were rises, so calling up every time scored 71% with no skill. cutoff_check.py now scores the drift reference in every batch, and direction counts as skill only if the Wilson 95% lower bound is above the highest of 50%, the drift reference's hit rate and the constant call (the more common direction every time; it needs the outcomes, so it is a bar, not a forecast). Added after the October test and before any later batch. On the three independent October batches the bar is 72% and no model clears it (LSTM 73%, lower bound 61%). Two new tests in test_cutoff_check.py; the suite was then 793.")]));
children.push(H2("3.92 Direction calls before 2025: no model only calls up, and LSTM passes the bar narrowly"));
children.push(P("In October LSTM, GRU and TCN forecast a rise 99-100% of the time and 71% of outcomes were rises (Sections 3.88, 3.91). run_direction_bias.py asks whether they do the same before 2025. Written down first: eight names (ADANIPORTS, AXISBANK, BANKBARODA, HEROMOTOCO, IBREALEST, IBULHSGFIN, M&M, YESBANK) at twelve non-overlapping five-session windows from June 2019 to June 2024, 96 forecasts per model, for the eight models with a Wilson lower bound above 50% in October. Q1: always up if at least 90% rise calls overall and in every window where most stocks fell. Q2: rise calls when the last 60 or 250 sessions fell against rose. Q3: the Section 3.91 bar (Wilson lower bound above the highest of 50%, the drift reference and the constant call). Nothing in the app changes; what follows is the owner's choice. The run took 15.7 hours under the offline guard; 60% of outcomes were falls."));
children.push(P([B("Result. "), T("Q1: no model calls up regardless; LSTM called a rise in 45% of forecasts and 29% in falling windows, so October's near-100% was a response to October, not a habit. Q2: LSTM, TCN, ARIMAX-GARCH, GRU and Stacking lean with the 250-session trend (Stacking 25% rise calls after a falling year, 67% after a rising one); CNN-LSTM leans against it; N-BEATS hardly at all. Q3: hit rates LSTM 72% (lower bound 62%, bar 60%: passes), GRU 67%, TCN 62%, ARIMAX and Stacking 61%, Quantile 59%, CNN-LSTM 56%, N-BEATS 54%; drift reference 53%. LSTM's pass is weak: by window it beat the constant call in 5 of 12, lost 5 and tied 2; one pass in eight models is about what chance gives; in October its 72% was the constant call. Checked afterwards, as description: calling the sign of the 250-session trend hit 54%, and LSTM agreed with it 61% of the time; without YESBANK, IBULHSGFIN and IBREALEST it hit 76% of 59. So the models do not just say up, but no direction skill holds by window. Neither written option (a card note, or hiding the direction call) is applied; LSTM's direction is a candidate for the cutoff batches and the forward record.")]));
children.push(H2("3.93 Prophet selected by default again: the user decides, with the evidence on its card"));
children.push(P("Section 3.90 stopped selecting Prophet at start-up and on Reset because even its best configuration forecast worse than no change. The owner reversed that on 2026-11-08: a model left unselected gets ignored, and the choice should be the user's. Every model now starts selected, Prophet included. Kept: the note on Prophet's card whenever it runs (worse than no change at every horizon tested before 2025; weigh its range, not its line), now serving.MODEL_NOTES; the improved configuration of Section 3.90; and the long-horizon exclusion beyond 13 sessions (Section 3.84). serving.OFF_BY_DEFAULT is now empty but kept as the mechanism, so serving.DEFAULT_MODELS is every model; the sidebar shows 20 of 20 and Reset selects all 20. This is not at odds with the evidence: no model has shown a reliable edge over no change (Sections 3.88, 3.91, 3.92), and Prophet's range, after Section 3.90, held up in October; leaving it out singled it out on the point forecast, which the app already says not to rely on. test_prophet_band.py now checks that every model is selected and that Prophet's note is there, and ui_check.py that Reset selects every model, Prophet included. The suite was then still 793."));
children.push(H2("3.94 HAR-RV's range: three published fixes tested on 57 stocks, none kept"));
children.push(P("The owner's plan, agreed on 2026-11-08: for each model, look on the web, arXiv and GitHub for published improvements, build the promising ones as switches, test them before 2025, and keep only what improves. The range comes first (interval score and coverage), and the price error must not get worse. HAR-RV was the first model; nothing tested was kept."));
children.push(P([B("Which model first. "), T("On Section 3.87's 32 forecasts at five sessions (band_audit.csv), interval score as a multiple of the no-change reference: Prophet 2.06 and Regime Switching 1.62, both before their fixes (Sections 3.89, 3.90); HAR-RV 1.71, N-BEATS 1.46, CNN-LSTM 1.17, LightGBM 1.15, Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM 1.05, ARIMA-GARCH 0.91. LightGBM and Hybrid also cover 88% at 20-90 sessions, and N-BEATS has the worst price error (1.25 times no change). Order chosen: HAR-RV, N-BEATS, Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM, LightGBM, with CNN-LSTM in reserve.")]));
children.push(P([B("What was wrong with HAR-RV's range, on those 32 forecasts. "), T("It covered as often as the no-change range (93.75%) but was 24% wider at the median. Its volatility forecast was 17% above the last 60 sessions at the median, and the multiplier was 2.35 before ACI and 2.03 after, against 1.96. A few forecasts did most of the damage: YESBANK's multiplier reached 8.6 because its calibration still held the 2020 collapse, and on IOC ACI moved it from 3.0 to 4.9, close to the largest of about 75 past errors.")]));
children.push(P([B("What the research turned up. "), T("(1) Weight recent calibration errors more so old crashes fade (Barber, Candes, Ramdas and Tibshirani, Conformal prediction beyond exchangeability, Annals of Statistics 2023, arXiv 2202.13415 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.13415)). (2) Keep ACI out of the extreme tail with a higher floor on its level, or DtACI's mixture of eight learning rates (Gibbs and Candes, arXiv 2208.08401 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.08401), tested there on stock volatility). (3) Fit HAR and its level offset on recent history only (the DtACI paper refits on the last 1,250 days). Dropped: HARQ and jump-robust HAR need intraday prices; range-based volatility (Section 3.41) and other lags were already negative; the VIX tilt and asymmetric cone already exist and are off; Temporal Conformal Prediction (arXiv 2507.05470 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05470)) combines ideas 1 and 2 with no published code; the GitHub HAR repositories were basic or added a neural network, and none was installed or run.")]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(HAR_RV_model.run_har_rv_prediction, all off by default): fit_window, cal_halflife (weights halve every N calibration points, not sessions, because on long histories the origins are about 27 sessions apart and weights by session age could not reach 95%), aci_alpha_lo (0.005 as shipped) and aci_method=\"dtaci\". With every switch off the cone matches Section 3.72's published rows to six decimals.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_har_improve.py). Data: Section 3.72's setup, the 57 most liquid names, a forecast every 10 sessions from 2019 with 90 sessions of outcomes, all before 2025. Settings: half-lives 20 and 40; ACI floors 1% and 2%; DtACI; fits on the last 750 and 1,250 sessions. Choosing on 2019-21: per idea, the lowest five-session interval score among settings covering at least 93% at five sessions and 90% at 20-90 and scoring below shipped; chosen ideas are combined. Deciding on 2022-24: ship the combination if its five-session score beats shipped with a month-block bootstrap 95% interval above zero and coverage holds; else the best single idea that passes; else nothing.")]));
children.push(P("2019-21, the choosing period (4,243 forecasts per version at five sessions; widths and scores as % of price):"));
children.push(table([1860, 1050, 1050, 1050, 1450, 1450, 1450], [

]));
children.push(P([B("No idea passed, so nothing was taken to 2022-24 and HAR-RV is unchanged. "), T("Weighting made the range wider (39.27% and 40.23% against 37.84%); the cause was not investigated since the rule drops it either way. Fitting on recent history could not be judged: the archive starts in July 2017, so before 2022 no name has more than about 1,100 sessions; the 1,250 fit is identical to shipped and the 750 fit barely differs.")]));
children.push(P([B("What the larger test shows that the triage did not. "), T("At scale HAR-RV's five-session interval score is 2.6% above the reference in 2019-21 (37.84% against 36.89%) and 0.3% above in 2022-24 (24.02% against 23.95%), not 71%; the triage's 32 forecasts were dominated by a few blow-ups such as YESBANK. Its five-session range is close to the no-change range, which leaves little for any fix to gain.")]));
children.push(P("2022-24, shown for the record only (3,562 forecasts per version at five sessions); read after the decision, these change nothing:"));
children.push(table([2360, 1600, 1800, 1800, 1800], [
  ["Version", "Cov. 5", "Score 5", "Cov. 20-90", "Score 20-90"],
  ["No change (reference)", "92.3%", "23.95%", "91.9%", "83.76%"],
  ["HAR-RV as shipped", "94.8%", "24.02%", "93.1%", "82.55%"],
  ["Weighted, half-life 20", "94.5%", "24.19%", "92.5%", "84.82%"],
  ["Weighted, half-life 40", "94.6%", "24.71%", "93.0%", "84.74%"],
  ["ACI floor 1%", "94.8%", "23.73%", "92.9%", "82.62%"],
  ["ACI floor 2%", "93.9%", "23.52%", "92.3%", "83.39%"],
  ["DtACI", "94.1%", "23.31%", "92.9%", "82.58%"],

children.push(P("DtACI scores 3% lower there (23.31% against 24.02%) and covers 94.1% and 92.9%. Shipping it on that result would be choosing on the confirming data; a further try needs its own test written down in advance, on data not used here, for example the next 60 names by liquidity before 2025 (Update: tested in Section 3.100; it again covered 89.9% at 20-90 sessions and was not kept). Checks: test_har_improve.py (5 tests: equal weights give the usual conformal quantile; old extremes fade; DtACI stays in bounds and widens after misses; a higher floor caps the multiplier; every switch off leaves the cone unchanged). The suite was then 798. Files: run_har_improve.py and har_improve_parts.csv. Next in the plan: N-BEATS."));
children.push(H2("3.95 N-BEATS on its own returns: a smaller error and a narrower range, confirmed on a fresh draw"));
children.push(P("The second model in the owner's improvement plan (Section 3.94). On Section 3.87's 32 pre-2025 forecasts N-BEATS' five-session interval score was 1.46 times the no-change reference's and its price error the worst of the twenty (1.25 times no change). One change passed every rule written down in advance: on daily bars N-BEATS now reads only the stock's own returns. On a fresh draw of 72 forecasts it lowered the five-session interval score from 32.1% to 24.6% of the price and the error from 1.40 to 0.99 times no change. It still does not beat no change."));
children.push(P([B("How the app's N-BEATS differed from the published model "), T("(Oreshkin, Carpov, Chapados and Bengio, N-BEATS, ICLR 2020, arXiv 1905.10437 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.10437)). Inputs: the paper is univariate; the app read nine price features over 60 days flattened to 540 inputs against about 600 training rows (under-determined, Section 3.34), one of them the raw price level, which drifts outside the range the scaler saw. One network: the paper takes the median of many trained from different seeds and look-backs, and calls ensembling a much more powerful regularization technique than dropout or L2. Look-back: the paper uses 2 to 7 times the horizon; the app 60 days. A June 2026 study (arXiv 2606.27100 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27100)) found N-BEATS, N-HiTS and pretrained models' gains over a random walk on stock returns small and sparse. Dropped: N-HiTS (arXiv 2201.12886 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.12886)), whose gains come from smooth long-horizon signals and whose own ablation shows input pooling adds little; RevIN in full (Kim et al., ICLR 2022), parts of which a 2026 ablation (arXiv 2603.11869 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11869)) finds redundant, so only its window scaling was tested; GitHub code was read for ideas, never installed or run.")]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(NBeats_model.run_nbeats_prediction; model_utils.forecast_nn_direct gained feature_fn and use_context): inputs=\"returns\", the stock's one-step log returns only (60 inputs, no market context); window_norm=True, each window centred and divided by its own standard deviation, targets divided by the same number and the forecast multiplied back; n_seeds and lookbacks, the median of several networks (NBeatsEnsemble), the first trained exactly as before.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_nbeats_improve.py). Choosing on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, each arm at five sessions and at 90: eligible if it covers at least 90% at five sessions, its five-session error is no higher than shipped's, its five-session interval score is lower, and it covers at least 90% pooled at 20-90; each family (input; ensemble) keeps its lowest-scoring eligible arm, and a combination of the two is the candidate only if eligible and better than the better single arm. Confirming once on 12 names never used in Sections 3.84, 3.90 or 3.92 (seed 20261109, 40 most liquid), six origins 2021-04-01 to 2024-02-13, 72 forecasts: ships for daily bars only if (1) the five-session score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of 6 origins, (2) the error is no higher pooled, (3) coverage at five sessions is at least 90%, (4) pooled coverage at 20-90 is at least 90%. Hourly bars keep the old configuration whatever happens.")]));

"), T("The score is lower pooled (24.64% against 32.12%) and at 5 of 6 origins (at 2022-12-19 it is 20.82% against 20.12%); the error falls from 1.40 to 0.99; coverage is 94.4% at five sessions and 97.6% at 20-90. The error is lower at every horizon tested, and at 40-90 sessions it falls from about 2 times no change to about 1.1.")]));
children.push(P([B("What it does not show. "), T("No beat over no change: 0.99 at five sessions is a tie, and every other horizon is above 1 (1.31 at day 1, 1.05-1.18 at 20-90); the change made a model clearly worse than no change about as good as it, mainly by removing inputs that hurt. Ranges are still conservative at long horizons (97-99% coverage at 20-90 against a 95% target), though narrower than before. The market-context switch no longer reaches N-BEATS on daily bars, because the tested model read none; the app never attaches the market index that would make its targets excess returns. The October cutoff test (Section 3.88) and every earlier section describe the old N-BEATS.")]));
children.push(P("Code: NBeats_model.DEFAULT_INPUTS = {daily: returns, hourly: features}; NBeats_model.LEGACY gives the network served before; the served N-BEATS reproduces the test's SBIN forecast from 2022-01-14 exactly (497.95, band 458.86-540.37). Checks: test_nbeats_improve.py (5 tests: the returns column; a member keeps its last steps and window scaling is reversible; the ensemble is the median and reads attributes from its first network; window scaling without returns and unknown inputs are refused; the daily default equals own returns and LEGACY still gives the old network). The suite was then 803. Files: run_nbeats_improve.py, nbeats_improve.csv, nbeats_improve_parts.csv. Next in the plan: Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM."));
children.push(H2("3.96 Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM: a direct network and a volatility-scaled band tested, none kept"));
children.push(P("The third model in the owner's improvement plan (Sections 3.94, 3.95). Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM is left out beyond 13 sessions because its range covered 88.3% at 20-90 sessions against a bar of 90% (Section 3.84); the plain no-change range covers 97.7% of the same forecasts. Two published ideas were tested and neither passed the rules written down in advance, so Hybrid is unchanged; the test did find what is wrong."));
children.push(P([B("Diagnosis on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87. "), T("Auto-ARIMA chose order (0,0,0) with no mean on all 32, so its forecast is no change and Hybrid is in effect a recursive LSTM on returns fed its own corrections. The recursion makes the centre drift: at 20-90 sessions the median distance from the last price was 0.68 standard deviations of the move (0.8 in the misses); on LT from 2022-01-14 the 90-session centre sat 13.2% above the last price, against 0.0% for the ARIMA alone. The band is symmetric around that centre. In several misses the calibration block was also calmer than the last 60 sessions, and the band was 0.4-0.6 times the reference width.")]));
children.push(P([B("Research. "), T("A direct (multiple-output) residual network, one network giving the cumulative correction at every step with nothing fed back (Ben Taieb, Bontempi, Atiya and Sorjamaa, Expert Systems with Applications 2012, arXiv 1108.3259 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1108.3259): multiple-output strategies did best on NN5), aimed at the drift. A volatility-scaled band, each calibration error divided by the 60-session volatility at its origin times the square root of the step and the quantile multiplied back by today's (normalized conformal scores: Papadopoulos, Gammerman and Vovk 2008; Lei et al., JASA 2018), aimed at stale calibration. Dropped: an ARIMA without a mean (it never fits one here; built, found a no-op on all 32, removed); EnbPI (Xu and Xie, ICML 2021), whose bootstrap fits each train an LSTM; ACI, already tested at scale (Section 3.72). Because ARIMA-network hybrids are not reliably better than their parts (Taskaya-Temizel and Casey, Neural Networks 2005), the ARIMA alone ran beside them as a diagnostic that could not ship.")]));
children.push(P([B("Switches and rules. "), T("Hybrid_model.run_hybrid_prediction gained strategy=\"direct\", band=\"vol_scaled\" and lstm=False (diagnostic), all off by default; with every switch off the forecast matches Sections 3.87 and 3.84 exactly. Written down before running (run_hybrid_improve.py): on the 32, an arm is eligible if its 20-90 interval score is lower than shipped's, its five-session score and its error at five sessions and 20-90 are no higher, and it covers at least 90% at five sessions and at 20-90. A candidate would then be confirmed on Section 3.95's fresh draw of 72 forecasts (20-90 score lower pooled and at 5 or more of 6 origins, the rest no worse, coverage holding) and, if it shipped, come off the long-horizon exclusion list.")]));
children.push(P("Choosing (32 forecasts; scores and widths as % of price; error as a multiple of the no-change error):"));

The volatility-scaled band was wider and covered less (86.7% at 20-90).")]));
children.push(P([B("What the diagnostic shows. "), T("The ARIMA alone forecasts no change, and its range has the best 20-90 score of the four (65.8%) yet covers only 89.8% (84.4% at 20 sessions). Once the centre stops drifting the LSTM adds nothing, and the band itself is the remaining problem: it is calibrated on the last 20% of a 750-session window, so at 90 sessions about 60 almost wholly overlapping paths stand for one market episode. The random-walk band (60-session volatility times the square root of steps) covers 97.7% of the same forecasts. A longer calibration span at long horizons, or a volatility-based band like the reference's, is the next thing to test; nothing was tuned on these numbers, and it would need its own rules and a fresh draw. Hybrid stays left out beyond 13 sessions. Update: tested in Section 3.97; nothing kept.")]));
children.push(P("Checks: test_hybrid_improve.py (5 tests: direct targets are cumulative residuals; the direct path adds the correction once; the volatility at an origin uses only earlier returns; bad switch values are refused; every switch off leaves the forecast unchanged and each arm gives a valid band). The suite was then 808. Files: run_hybrid_improve.py, hybrid_improve.csv, hybrid_improve_parts.csv. Next in the plan: LightGBM."));
children.push(H2("3.97 Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM beyond 13 sessions: a longer calibration and the reference band tested, none kept"));
children.push(P("Section 3.96 left a lead: once the direct network stops the centre drifting, the band is what keeps Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM below 90% coverage beyond 13 sessions, and a longer calibration span or a volatility-based band might fix it. Both were tested with rules written down in advance. The best version cut the 20-90 score by a quarter on fresh data but won at only 4 of the 6 dates, and the rules need 5. Nothing ships; Hybrid stays left out beyond 13 sessions."));
children.push(P([B("Scope and arms. "), T("Daily forecasts beyond 13 sessions only, the range where Hybrid is left out (Section 3.84); 13 sessions or fewer were not touched, so the five-session results of Sections 3.87 and 3.96 stand. All arms use Section 3.96's direct network: its shipped band (baseline); a calibration block of at least 150 forecast dates instead of about 60 (at 90 sessions, 60 overlapping paths are in effect one market episode); the volatility-scaled band; both together; and the reference band, the no-change range of 1.96 times the last 60 sessions' volatility times the square root of steps, around Hybrid's own centre. New switches in Hybrid_model.run_hybrid_prediction, both off by default: min_cal_origins and band=\"rw\"; with both off the forecast is unchanged.")]));

children.push(P("The fourth model in the owner's improvement plan. LightGBM was left out beyond 13 sessions because its range covered 88.3% at 20-90 sessions against a bar of 90% (Section 3.84). LightGBM now runs again beyond 13 sessions with larger leaves and a shrunk centre; daily forecasts of 13 sessions or fewer, hourly forecasts and XGBoost are unchanged (XGBoost: the same since Section 3.102)."));
children.push(P([B("Diagnosis, from the bands Section 3.84 stored. "), T("The band is about the right width, but the centre wanders: its median distance from the last price was 7% at 60 sessions, the price error was 1.17-1.52 times the no-change error at 20-90 sessions, and the misses followed the centre (L&T from 2021-01-01: the 20-session centre 13% below the last price, and the price rose). A likely cause is overlapping targets: 1,000 daily rows hold only about 11 independent 90-session outcomes, so a leaf of 20 rows is a fraction of one market episode.")]));
children.push(P([B("Research. "), T("Purge the early-stopping block of the last h training rows, whose labels overlap it (purged validation, Lopez de Prado, Advances in Financial Machine Learning, 2018, ch. 7, https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/-/9781119482086/). Larger leaves, at least min(20 h, a fifth of the rows) per leaf, because overlapping long-horizon observations add little information (Boudoukh, Israel and Richardson, Financial Analysts Journal 2019, https://doi.org/10.1080/0015198X.2018.1547056). Shrink the centre toward no change by the calibration block's least-squares slope of realised on predicted returns, clipped to [0, 1], and take the band from the shrunk residuals (Campbell and Thompson, Review of Financial Studies 2008, https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055). Switches purge, leaf_scale and shrink in ML_model.run_ML_prediction; with all off the forecast matches Section 3.84's stored band exactly.")]));
children.push(P([B("Rules written down before running (run_lgbm_band.py, the same as Section 3.97's). "), T("Scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90 sessions of one 90-session run. children.push(P([B("Decision: shipped. "), T("All three rules pass: the score is lower pooled (62.2% against 80.1%) and at all 6 dates, the error is 1.05 against 1.14, and coverage is 94.4%. ML_model.resolve_switches turns on leaf_scale and shrink for LightGBM on daily bars when the horizon is beyond gating.MAX_DIRECTIONAL_SESSIONS (13), for every step of the run as in the test. LightGBM is off gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED, which now holds Prophet and Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM. children.push(P([B("What this does not show. "), T("The centre is still no forecast of direction: its error is 1.05 times no change's, and beyond 13 sessions the app already withholds every up/down call and presents the range. What improved is the range: 28% narrower on the fresh draw (54.3% against 75.0% of the price) at 94.4% coverage. The original exclusion was close: the shipped LightGBM covered 95.5% on the fresh draw against 88.3% on the 32, within sampling noise of the bar, so the fix earns its place by a narrower band and a smaller error, not by rescuing a broken band. One date (2023-07-17) covered 87.5%. XGBoost runs the same pipeline and was not tested here; update: the same test on XGBoost passed and it shipped (Section 3.102).")]));
children.push(P("Checks: test_lgbm_improve.py (5 tests: the calibration parts reproduce the shipped half-width; the leaf size reaches both engines and None keeps the defaults; purging drops rows before the stopping block only; LightGBM beyond 13 daily sessions defaults to larger leaves and shrinkage and nothing else changes default; every switch off leaves the forecast unchanged and shrinkage never moves the centre further away). test_gates.py: LightGBM is kept past 13 sessions. ui_check.py: the 60-day scenario uses Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM as its left-out model. The suite was then 815. Files: run_lgbm_band.py, lgbm_band.csv, lgbm_band_parts.csv; the shipped arm now passes the old switches explicitly. Next in the plan: CNN-LSTM, in reserve, since it already passes at long horizons."));
children.push(H2("3.99 The skill banner says how small its check is, and one line on direction under every short run"));
children.push(P("Two wording changes the owner approved, both on the results page. No model, gate or number changed."));
children.push(P([B("1. The skill banner now says what its check is. "), T("The orange banner read \"Only k of n models beat the random-walk baseline with any reliability here\", which oversold it: the buckets come from a walk-forward check of 3 past forecast dates on this one stock (config.GATE_SPLITS), enough to catch a broken model but not to prove a good one; with 20 models one or two High or Medium rows is about what luck gives. It now reads \"Only k of n models did better than a no-change forecast in a quick check of 3 recent forecast dates here, which is not proof. Weight the range over the point line.\" The green banner (at least half) now reads \"k of n models did better than a no-change forecast in a quick check of 3 recent forecast dates here: a small sample, not proof.\" The red banner is unchanged. The number of dates is read from the check itself (n_eval), and the console's verdict line prints the same sentence.")]));
children.push(P([B("2. One general line on direction. "), T("Under every run that can show an up/down call (daily runs of 13 sessions or fewer, and every hourly run) the page now shows UI.DIRECTION_CAVEAT: \"No model's up/down call has yet proven reliable across separate weeks of testing. Read a Bullish or Bearish label as a pointer, not a prediction.\" It rests on Sections 3.88 (20 models, 20 stocks, 4 weeks), 3.91 (the fairer direction bar, which no model passed in October) and 3.92 (only LSTM passed before 2025, narrowly, and the weekly count did not back it). Beyond 13 sessions every call is already withheld and the long-horizon banner says so, so the line is not repeated there.")]));
children.push(P("Checks: tools/ui_check.py scenarios requires the line on the daily run and its absence on the 60-day run. The suite was then still 815; no unit test imports UI.py."));

children.push(P("In Section 3.94, DtACI (Gibbs and Candes's dynamically tuned adaptive conformal) had HAR-RV's lowest five-session interval score but covered 89.9% at 20-90 sessions against a bar of 90%, so it was not chosen; its better 2022-2024 numbers had been read after the decision, so it was left for a separate test on stocks not used there. The owner approved that test. The result was the same: a clearly better five-session range, 0.1 point short of the long-horizon bar. Nothing ships; HAR-RV keeps ACI."));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running (run_har_dtaci_fresh.py). "), T("Ranks 61-120 of Section 3.94's liquidity screen, none of its 60 (the script refuses to run otherwise); 58 have more than 600 sessions.
