DtACI ships as the daily default only if (1) its five-session interval score is lower with the 95% month-block bootstrap interval of the gain above zero, (2) its five-session coverage is at least 93%, and (3) its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%.")]));

children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("HAR-RV's five-session score is now lower than the no-change reference's (35.98% against 36.33%), the first time in these tests, because it misses less often (94.0% against 91.9% coverage) at a wider width (24.7% against 21.6%). The gain is modest: about 3.5% on the score and 9% on the width. The three tests are consistent: Section 3.94's choice period showed almost nothing; Section 3.100 and this test, on different stocks, a clear gain. Ranks 121-180 are less liquid than the stocks most people look up, and the large caps of Section 3.94 gave the smallest gain. The point forecast is unchanged (no change by design). The weekly cutoff batches and the forward record now show the DtACI band for HAR-RV at five sessions; earlier batches used ACI.")]));
children.push(P("Checks: test_har_improve.py gained one test, making 6 (DtACI is the default only on daily runs of 13 sessions or fewer; the switch-off test passes aci_method='aci' explicitly). ui_check.py scenarios passes. children.push(H2("3.102 XGBoost beyond 13 sessions: LightGBM's fix, confirmed on a fresh draw and shipped"));
children.push(P("Step A of the owner's plan for the neural family, run alongside it. XGBoost goes through the same pipeline as LightGBM (ML_model.run_ML_prediction). Section 3.84 kept its band beyond 13 sessions at 91.4%, but Section 3.98 found the same band on LightGBM wide with a wandering centre, so the same six arms were run on XGBoost with Section 3.98's rules unchanged. Larger leaves with a shrunk centre passed every rule and is now XGBoost's configuration on daily runs beyond 13 sessions, as it is LightGBM's; daily runs of 13 sessions or fewer and hourly runs are unchanged."));
children.push(P([B("Switches and rules (run_xgb_band.py), Section 3.98's. "), T("leaf_scale sets XGBoost's min_child_weight to at least min(20 h, a fifth of the rows); shrink scales the centre toward no change by beta = clip(sum py / sum p^2, 0, 1); purge drops the last h rows before the early-stopping block. The shipped arm passes all three off explicitly (ML_model.LGBM_LEGACY). Scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90 sessions of one 90-session run. Eligible on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87: pooled coverage of at least 90%, a lower score than shipped and an error no higher; the candidate is the eligible arm with the lowest score. Ships for daily horizons beyond 13 sessions only if, on Section 3.95's fresh draw (72 forecasts): (1) its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates; (2) its error is no higher; (3) its coverage is at least 90%.")]));

children.push(P("Knock-on, found later: Meta-Ensemble's XGBoost member takes these defaults, so this change also reached Meta-Ensemble on daily bars beyond 13 sessions. It was not tested here; Section 3.111 checked it on unused draws and kept it."));
children.push(H2("3.103 CNN-LSTM on its own returns: a smaller error, but a better range at only 3 of 6 dates, not kept"));
children.push(P("Step B1 of the owner's plan, the first of the neural family. On the 32 triage forecasts CNN-LSTM's five-session interval score was 1.17 times the no-change reference's, the worst of the untouched neural models. The best change found cut its price error by an eighth on fresh data but lowered its range score at only 3 of the 6 dates, against the 5 the rule needs. Nothing ships; CNN-LSTM is unchanged. It runs on the shared direct engine (model_utils.forecast_nn_direct): nine price features plus market context over a 90-session window, one network output per step, split-conformal bands."));
children.push(P([B("Research (read for ideas only; nothing downloaded was run). "), T("Mehtab and Sen (arXiv 2010.13891 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.13891)): a univariate CNN-LSTM with two weeks of input was the most accurate of their models on NIFTY (arms returns, lb20). DLinear (Zeng et al., AAAI 2023, arXiv 2205.13504 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.13504)) and PatchTST (Nie et al., ICLR 2023, arXiv 2211.14730 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14730)): one series per channel is a strong default, though it depends on the setup (arXiv 2502.09683 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09683)) (arm returns). RevIN (Kim et al., ICLR 2022; https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN): scale each window by its own statistics and scale the forecast back (arm ret_norm). Section 3.95: the stock's own log returns helped N-BEATS on a fresh draw.")]));
children.push(P([B("Switches "), T("(CNN_LSTM_model.run_cnn_lstm_prediction, all off by default). inputs='returns': the stock's own log returns only, through NBeats_model.returns_only, with no market context. window_norm=True: WindowNormModel centres each window and divides it by its own standard deviation, divides the targets by the same number and multiplies the forecast back (needs inputs='returns'; the window's mean is not added back, since a window's average return is not a forecast). look_back: the window length (default 90 daily, 48 hourly; at least 10). LEGACY holds the configuration before this section; with every switch off the five-session forecast for SBI from 2022-01-14 matched the pre-change model at every step. Smoke times per forecast: shipped 54 s, returns 53 s, ret_norm 29 s, lb20 24 s, lb40 39 s.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running (run_cnn_improve.py, Section 3.95's rules). "), T("An input family (returns, ret_norm) and a look-back family (lb20, lb40); each arm runs once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60, 90). Eligible on the 32 if at five sessions it covers at least 90%, its error is no higher than shipped's and its score lower, and its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%. Each family keeps its eligible arm with the lowest five-session score; the two are combined, and the combination goes forward only if eligible and lower than the better single arm. Ships as the daily default only if, on Section 3.95's fresh draw (72 forecasts): (1) its five-session score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of 6 dates; (2) its five-session error is no higher; (3) it covers at least 90% at five sessions; (4) its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%.")]));

children.push(P("Step B2 of the owner's plan. On the 32 triage forecasts the Transformer's five-session interval score was 1.106 times the no-change reference's. Hourly bars keep the nine features. children.push(P([B("Research (read for ideas only; nothing downloaded was run). "), T("Zeng et al., 'Are Transformers Effective for Time Series Forecasting?' (AAAI 2023, arXiv 2205.13504 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.13504)): simple linear models beat the published forecasting Transformers, whose gains came mostly from forecasting all steps at once, which this model already does. PatchTST (Nie et al., ICLR 2023, arXiv 2211.14730 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14730); https://github.com/yuqinie98/PatchTST): one series per channel, patches of 16 steps every 8 as tokens, instance normalisation, a flatten-and-linear head (arms patch, returns, ret_norm), and a reduced model (4 heads, 16 wide, feed-forward 128) on small datasets 'to mitigate the possible overfitting' (arm small). Sections 3.95 and 3.103: own returns helped N-BEATS, and with window scaling cut CNN-LSTM's error but not its range enough.")]));
children.push(P([B("Switches "), T("(Transformer_model.run_transformer_prediction). inputs='returns': own log returns only (NBeats_model.returns_only), no market context. window_norm=True: each window centred and divided by its own standard deviation, the forecast scaled back (CNN_LSTM_model.WindowNormModel; needs inputs='returns'). arch='small': PatchTST's reduced size (SMALL). arch='patch': build_patch_transformer_model, a Patcher layer padding the window by its last step 8 times and cutting patches of 16 every 8 (11 tokens from 90; n_patches = (L - 16) // 8 + 2), a learnable position embedding, three blocks attending both ways (transformer_encoder(..., causal=False); every token lies inside the window), and a flatten-and-linear head at the reduced size. LEGACY holds the configuration before this section.")]));

children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("The margin is small: the score is 0.6% lower. At four of the five dates it won, it won by 0.3-0.5 points, and at 2022-05-26 by 0.04; it lost 2022-12-19 by 0.81. The rule is met, not cleared by a distance. On the 32 the gain was 4.6%; as with CNN-LSTM, most of the choosing gain did not survive the fresh draw. The price error is no better than no change (0.98 against shipped's 1.00): no skill on direction, and the app's up/down gates still decide what is shown. Beyond 13 sessions the score is worse (73.27% against 70.23% fresh; 71.16% against 60.52% on the 32), though the error there is lower (1.07 against 1.13) and coverage is 96.5%. The rules ruled only on coverage beyond 13 sessions and set the change as the default for daily bars, so it applies at every daily horizon; splitting the default by horizon now would be choosing after the result and would need its own test. PatchTST's ideas did not transfer to 1,000 daily bars of one stock: the paper's datasets are long multichannel series with periodic structure, and a stock's returns have little of either. The weekly cutoff batches and the forward record will now show the new Transformer. Run time: 669 minutes to choose (about 7.5 hours of it the PC asleep), 387 to confirm.")]));

Borovykh, Bohte and Oosterlee, 'Conditional time series forecasting with convolutional neural networks' (arXiv 1703.04691, https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.04691): a dilated-convolution network on daily S&P 500 returns with normalised one-day returns, one filter per layer and a 16-session receptive field, naming overfitting as the cost of size (arms returns, small, lb20). "), T("This is the opposite failure to the GRU's (Section 3.105): there the gain was large but carried by two dates; here it is broad but small, 2.6% lower pooled and above half a point at only 2 of the 6 dates (1.87 points on 2022-12-19, 0.64 on 2023-07-17; 0.02 on 2024-02-13). The error gap is 0.010 and both equal no change to two decimals, so neither has skill on direction; but rule 2 says 'no higher', was written before the run, and is not loosened after seeing the result. children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_tcn_improve.py (3 tests: the dilations follow the window, 90 sessions giving 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 20 giving 1, 2, 4, the standard network has 64 filters and a dense head, and small has 16 filters, a single dense output layer and under a tenth of the parameters; bad switch values are refused, an unknown input, window scaling on the nine features, an unknown size, a window under 10; the defaults equal LEGACY, and own returns + window scaling + small + a 20-session window gives valid bands). children.push(H2("3.107 Every model stays selected by default, and a tracker of what was researched and changed"));
children.push(P("Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM was briefly set to start unselected, and that was reversed the same evening. On 2026-11-10 the owner approved the recommendation to stop selecting Hybrid at start-up and on Reset (Sections 3.96 and 3.97 found nothing to fix). The change was made and passed its checks, but was never documented as shipped. The owner then reversed it: every model stays selected by default, because a model that fails today's tests may give good results later, and the user should decide. The code was restored exactly: serving.OFF_BY_DEFAULT is empty, serving.DEFAULT_MODELS is all 20, and no note was added to Hybrid's card. This is Section 3.93's principle, now a standing rule. Exclusions beyond 13 sessions (gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED) are separate and were not touched."));
children.push(P("A tracker of the improvement programme now sits in Chapter 2 (Model improvement tracker): for every model, what was researched with sources, what was tested, the result, what is in the app now, and where it is recorded. It covers the programme from Section 3.94, the earlier fixes to Prophet, Regime Switching and Vol-LSTM, and the models not yet in the programme, and is updated after every step. This section changes no code."));
children.push(H2("3.108 LSTM with one layer instead of three: a 7% lower range score, but at only 4 of 6 dates, not kept"));
children.push(P("Step B5 of the owner's plan. The LSTM shares its code (DL_model) with the GRU, so it was tested with Section 3.105's four arms and rules, run before anything about the LSTM was seen. A single recurrent layer of 64 in place of the shipped three lowered the pooled five-session score on the fresh draw by 7% (22.81% against 24.64%), with a lower error (1.00 against 1.05), but at only 4 of the 6 dates, against the 5 the rule needs. Nothing ships; the LSTM is unchanged. Unlike the GRU, its own-returns arms were not even eligible. Before: the shared direct engine (model_utils.forecast_nn_direct), nine price features plus market context over 90 sessions, three LSTM layers of 64, 64 and 32 units (dropout 0.3, 0.3, 0.2; recurrent dropout 0.1), a dense layer of 16, one output per step and split-conformal bands; in Section 3.87's 32 forecasts its five-session band was in the group 1.04-1.15 times as wide as the no-change reference's."));
children.push(P([B("Research (Section 3.105's, since the two models share the code; read for ideas only, nothing downloaded was run). "), T("Fischer and Krauss, 'Deep learning with long short-term memory networks for financial market predictions' (European Journal of Operational Research 2018, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054; working paper read in full, https://www.econstor.eu/bitstream/10419/157808/1/886576210.pdf), a study of the LSTM itself: one standardised daily return as the only input, one LSTM layer of 25 units with dropout 0.1 on inputs and recurrent connections, 2,752 parameters (arms returns, small). A smaller network than the shipped 64-64-32 stack (the same paper; Section 3.34's samples-per-input table) (arms single, small). Sections 3.95 and 3.103-3.106, and RevIN (https://iclr.cc/virtual/2022/poster/6034, https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN): own returns, alone and with window scaling (arms returns, ret_norm). The switches are Section 3.105's (DL_model.run_DL_prediction with model_type='LSTM'), all off by default; Section 3.105's smoke test already showed the LSTM unchanged with every switch off (day-5 band 423.80-515.23). No code changed for this step.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running (run_lstm_improve.py, Section 3.105's rules). "), T("An input family (returns, ret_norm) and a size family (single, small); each arm runs once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60, 90). Eligible on the 32 if at five sessions it covers at least 90%, its error is no higher than shipped's and its score lower, and its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%. Each family keeps its eligible arm with the lowest five-session score; if both keep one, they are combined, and the combination goes forward only if eligible and lower than the better single arm. Ships as the LSTM's daily default only if, on Section 3.95's fresh draw (72 forecasts): (1) its five-session score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of 6 dates; (2) its five-session error is no higher, pooled; (3) it covers at least 90% at five sessions; (4) its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%. Hourly bars and the GRU keep the shipped configuration either way.")]));

children.push(H2("3.109 Own returns with window scaling on a new draw: shipped on the GRU beyond 13 sessions and on CNN-LSTM up to 13"));
children.push(P("The follow-up queued after Section 3.105, with its rules written down before it ran, on 12 stocks no forecasting test had used. Two of its three questions passed, and both changes ship. The GRU on daily bars beyond 13 sessions now reads the stock's own returns with each window scaled by its own volatility: its 20-90 session score fell from 79.86% to 63.46%, lower at 5 of the 6 dates, and its error from 1.43 to 1.09 times no change's. CNN-LSTM on daily bars up to 13 sessions now reads the same input: its five-session error fell from 1.31 to 1.06 times no change's, lower at 5 of the 6 dates, and its score from 19.96% to 19.22%. The GRU up to 13 sessions is unchanged: its five-session error was lower at only 1 of the 6 dates."));
children.push(P("Why this test: own returns with window scaling had failed only the breadth rule on the five-session range score on CNN-LSTM (Section 3.103) and the GRU (Section 3.105). Two results were reported there but not ruled on. A: beyond 13 sessions the shipped GRU was poor, its 20-90 session error on Section 3.95's fresh draw 1.96 times no change's against 1.03 with own returns. B: the five-session price error fell on both (CNN-LSTM 1.14 to 1.00, the GRU 1.00 to 0.99), but those sections ranked by range, not error. Deciding either from those numbers would have changed the rules after seeing the result, so each got its own test on a new draw. The input is the one of Sections 3.103 and 3.105, from RevIN (https://iclr.cc/virtual/2022/poster/6034, https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN), Mehtab and Sen's univariate CNN-LSTM on NIFTY (arXiv 2010.13891, https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.13891) and Fischer and Krauss's LSTM on standardised returns (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054). No new research was done for this step."));
children.push(P("The draw (run_returns_followup.py): almost every name among the 40 most liquid had already been used, so the screen was widened to the 80 most liquid NSE names of the same pre-2025 period as Sections 3.84 and 3.95, keeping names that trade on every session and leaving out exchange-traded funds and every name in the result files of the earlier pre-2025 forecasting tests (USED_BEFORE). That left 33 names. Twelve were drawn with seed 20261111: ASHOKLEY, ASIANPAINT, BHARATFORG, DRREDDY, JUSTDIAL, LICHSGFIN, MRF, PETRONET, PFC, POWERGRID, TECHM and ULTRACEMCO; six origins from 2021-04-01 to 2024-02-13, 72 forecasts. The draw was computed offline before the run, and the run drew the same names."));

"), T("DL_model.GRU_LONG is own returns with window scaling; DL_model.resolve_switches gives it to the GRU on daily bars beyond gating.MAX_DIRECTIONAL_SESSIONS and LEGACY (the nine features) to everything else, including the LSTM. CNN_LSTM_model.SHORT_DEFAULT is the same input; CNN_LSTM_model.resolve_switches gives it to CNN-LSTM on daily bars up to 13 sessions and LEGACY beyond 13 and on hourly bars. In run_DL_prediction and run_cnn_lstm_prediction, inputs and window_norm now default to None, which takes the resolved default, as the Transformer's did (Section 3.104). An explicit value is kept: an explicit inputs that differs from the default keeps window scaling off unless asked for, and LEGACY gives the old network. Every improvement script's shipped arm passes its old configuration explicitly, so reruns still compare against it.")]));

children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("Less wrong is not skilled: both errors are still above no change's (the GRU's at 20-90 sessions 1.09 times it, CNN-LSTM's at five sessions 1.06 times); neither carries skill on direction, and the app's up/down gates still decide what is shown. The GRU's gain at long horizons is large and broad, at all four horizons and five of six dates, and matches Section 3.105 (error 1.96 to 1.03 there, 1.43 to 1.09 here); on the one date it lost, 2022-12-19, the shipped GRU happened to be close to no change (1.01). A GRU forecast beyond 13 sessions now comes entirely from the new network, days 1-13 included: the horizon the user picks sets the configuration, as for LightGBM and XGBoost (Sections 3.98, 3.102); since B failed for the GRU, a 5-session GRU forecast and the first 5 days of a 20-session one now come from different networks. CNN-LSTM's error gain has now held on three sets of names (1.14 to 1.00 in Section 3.103, 1.31 to 1.06 here); per step it was largest at steps 1, 2 and 5 (1.21 to 1.02, 1.44 to 1.07, 1.31 to 1.06), with step 4 slightly worse (1.02 to 1.04); coverage rose from 93.1% to 97.2% with a band slightly wider (18.17% to 18.44%). CNN-LSTM horizons 6-13 were not tested one by one; the rule set the default for daily horizons up to 13, as Section 3.101 did for HAR-RV. Beyond 13 sessions CNN-LSTM keeps the nine features: its long-horizon gain in Section 3.103 was reported, not ruled on, and was not part of this test. The names are a different slice of the market: the screen was widened to 80, so most of these 12 sit below the 40 most liquid that earlier draws came from.")]));
children.push(P("The weekly cutoff batches and the forward record will now show the new GRU beyond 13 sessions and the new CNN-LSTM up to 13; batches before this change used the nine features. Validation scripts that call these models with default arguments (run_full_backtest.py, run_multi_backtest.py, run_spa.py, run_epistemic_eval.py) will use the new defaults if rerun. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107). Run time: 211 minutes."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_dl_improve.py (4 tests; 1 added: the GRU's default is GRU_LONG on daily bars beyond 13 sessions and LEGACY at 13, for the LSTM and on hourly bars; explicit values are kept). tests/test_cnn_improve.py (4 tests; 1 added, 1 rewritten: the default is SHORT_DEFAULT on daily bars up to 13 sessions and LEGACY beyond and on hourly bars; explicit values are kept; a default run equals an explicit own-returns-with-window-scaling run, and LEGACY and inputs='features' give the old network; window scaling with no inputs is no longer refused at short daily horizons, so the refusal test names inputs='features'). tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 830. Files: run_returns_followup.py, returns_followup.csv, returns_followup_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources "), T("(ideas from Sections 3.103 and 3.105): RevIN, "), Link("https://iclr.cc/virtual/2022/poster/6034"), T(", "), Link("https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN"), T("; Mehtab and Sen, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.13891"), T("; Fischer and Krauss, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the neural steps of the programme are done. Open leads, not scheduled, each needing its own rules and an unused draw: the LSTM beyond 13 sessions (Section 3.108; tested next and shipped, Section 3.110), CNN-LSTM beyond 13 sessions (Section 3.103) and the TCN ranked by error (Section 3.106). Prophet is the optional next model."));
children.push(H2("3.110 The LSTM and CNN-LSTM beyond 13 sessions on own returns: shipped on the LSTM, CNN-LSTM at only 4 of 6 dates"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-11 as the next step after Section 3.109. The input that shipped on the GRU beyond 13 sessions, the stock's own returns with each window scaled by its own volatility, was tested on the LSTM and on CNN-LSTM beyond 13 sessions, under Section 3.109's rules, on 12 stocks no earlier test had used. The LSTM passed every rule and ships: its 20-90 session score fell from 130.40% to 119.30%, lower at all 6 dates, and its error from 1.17 to 1.03 times no change's. CNN-LSTM's score was lower pooled (123.01% against 126.22%) and its error fell from 1.10 to 1.01, but the score was lower at only 4 of the 6 dates; it keeps the nine features beyond 13 sessions."));
children.push(P("Why this test. The LSTM shares DL_model with the GRU; in Section 3.108 its shipped error at 20-90 sessions on Section 3.95's fresh draw was 1.5-1.7 times no change's (1.61 pooled), and on the 32 choosing forecasts own returns had cut it from 1.48 to 1.07; Section 3.109's rules were set before that result, so the LSTM was not in it. CNN-LSTM takes the input up to 13 sessions since Section 3.109; beyond 13 it kept the nine features, and in Section 3.103 the input lowered its 20-90 session error, reported but not ruled on. The input is the one of Sections 3.103-3.109, from RevIN (https://iclr.cc/virtual/2022/poster/6034, https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN), Mehtab and Sen (arXiv 2010.13891, https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.13891) and Fischer and Krauss (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054). No new research was done for this step."));
children.push(P("The draw (run_long_followup.py): Section 3.109's screen of the 80 most liquid NSE names before 2025, leaving out exchange-traded funds and every name in the earlier pre-2025 forecasting tests, now including Section 3.109's draw and the files of Sections 3.106 and 3.108. That left 21 names. Twelve were drawn with seed 20261112: AUROPHARMA, BAJAJ-AUTO, CENTURYTEX, COALINDIA, EICHERMOT, FEDERALBNK, GAIL, IDEA, JINDALSTEL, JSWSTEEL, RECLTD and UPL; Section 3.109's six origins, 2021-04-01 to 2024-02-13, 72 forecasts. The draw was computed offline before the run (no overlap with Section 3.109's names, data ending 2024-06-28), and the run drew the same names."));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running. "), T("For the LSTM and CNN-LSTM separately: the shipped configuration (LEGACY, passed explicitly) against own returns with window scaling, each run once at 90 sessions and scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90. The change becomes that model's default on daily bars beyond gating.MAX_DIRECTIONAL_SESSIONS (13) only if (C1) its 20-90 session score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, (C2) its 20-90 session error is no higher, pooled, and (C3) its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90%. These are Section 3.109's rules A1-A3. Hourly bars, the GRU, both models up to 13 sessions and every other model are untouched either way.")]));

children.push(P([B("What changed in the code. "), T("DL_model.LONG_MODELS is (\"GRU\", \"LSTM\"), and DL_model.resolve_switches now gives GRU_LONG (own returns with window scaling) to both on daily bars beyond 13 sessions; the name GRU_LONG is kept, since Section 3.109 documents it. CNN-LSTM's code is unchanged. Explicit values are still kept, and LEGACY still gives the old network.")]));
children.push(P([B("Served runs reproduce the test "), T("(serving.MODEL_REGISTRY with its default arguments; JSWSTEEL from 2024-02-13, steps 20, 40, 60 and 90). LSTM, 90 sessions: matches the new row exactly (maximum difference 0.000000); day-90 band 719.14-1006.45 around 850.75, against 732.86-1045.85 around 875.48 before; the price 90 sessions later, 931.50, was inside both. CNN-LSTM, 90 sessions: matches its unchanged shipped row to within 0.0008 rupees on a price of about 850, floating-point noise in the convolution layers rather than a different configuration (day-90 band 712.86-1017.55 around 851.69).")]));
children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("The LSTM now behaves as the GRU does: the nine features up to 13 sessions, own returns with window scaling beyond; its error beyond 13 sessions (1.03 times no change's) is still above no change, less wrong but not skilled on direction. Its gain is not even across horizons: large at 40 and 60 sessions, but at 20 the error was higher (1.12 against 1.02) and at 90 the score was higher (195.49% against 183.58%); the rules pool 20-90, as in Section 3.109, and were set before the run, so the per-horizon numbers are reported, not ruled on. Coverage on this draw is low at 90 sessions for both versions of both models (the LSTM 86.1% either way; CNN-LSTM 90.3% and 91.7%); pooled over 20-90 every version is above 90%, so no exclusion rule is triggered (gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED uses the pooled figure); it is a flag, not a result. This draw is much harder than Section 3.109's (scores about 120-130% of price against about 65-80%): three stocks rallied far outside any band after 2023-07-17, RECLTD from about 150 to 332 rupees in 90 sessions (the LSTM's upper band was 228), IDEA to 13.25 against an upper band of 12.25 and COALINDIA to 338 against 294; the 40-times miss penalty makes such dates dominate, and CNN-LSTM's loss at 2023-07-17 is mostly RECLTD and IDEA.")]));
children.push(P("CNN-LSTM is now split by horizon: own returns with window scaling up to 13 sessions (Section 3.109), the nine features beyond; its long-horizon error fell on both draws that measured it (Section 3.103 and here), but its score failed the breadth rule here, so it stays. The weekly cutoff batches run at five sessions, so this change does not reach them; it reaches any daily run longer than 13 sessions, in the app and in the validation scripts that use default arguments. Only 9 unused names are left in the 80-name screen, too few for another draw of 12; a further test of this kind needs a new source of untouched data, and the owner will be asked before one is used. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107). Run time: 115 minutes."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_dl_improve.py (4 tests): the long-horizon test now also checks LONG_MODELS and that the LSTM takes GRU_LONG beyond 13 daily sessions and LEGACY at 13 and on hourly bars; no test was added. tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 830. Files: run_long_followup.py, long_followup.csv, long_followup_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources "), T("(ideas from Sections 3.103-3.109): RevIN, "), Link("https://iclr.cc/virtual/2022/poster/6034"), T(", "), Link("https://github.com/ts-kim/RevIN"), T("; Mehtab and Sen, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.13891"), T("; Fischer and Krauss, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on 12-13 November (five sessions, report only). children.push(H2("3.111 Meta-Ensemble beyond 13 sessions: the XGBoost change it inherited, checked on unused draws and kept"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-11 as the first step of the non-neural part of the programme. Meta-Ensemble's XGBoost member takes ML_model's defaults, so when Section 3.102 shipped larger leaves and a shrunk centre for XGBoost on daily bars beyond 13 sessions, Meta-Ensemble changed too. That knock-on had not been tested or documented. It was checked against the XGBoost Meta-Ensemble was validated with, on 144 forecasts no earlier choice about XGBoost or Meta-Ensemble had used. The inherited change passed every rule written down before the run, so it stays: Meta-Ensemble's 20-90 session score fell from 81.30% to 80.32% of price, its error from 1.015 to 0.978 times no change's, and its coverage was 95.0%. Nothing in the app changes."));
children.push(P("Why this check. Meta-Ensemble is the per-step median of three members (Quantile-LGBM, XGBoost and ARIMA+GARCH) with the mean of their bounds (meta_ensemble._CONSTITUENTS). Its XGBoost member is called with model_type=\"xgboost\" only; since Section 3.102, ML_model.resolve_switches gives that call leaf_scale and shrink on daily bars beyond gating.MAX_DIRECTIONAL_SESSIONS (13). Up to 13 sessions, and on hourly bars, nothing changed. The knock-on was found while planning the non-neural models, by checking what each one calls."));
children.push(P("Which data. Section 3.102 chose XGBoost's change on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87 and confirmed it on Section 3.95's 72, so those sets would favour it. This check used the draws of Sections 3.109 and 3.110 instead: 24 names, the same six origins (2021-04-01 to 2024-02-13), 144 forecasts, recomputed with those scripts' own draw functions. Nothing about XGBoost, Quantile-LGBM, ARIMA+GARCH or Meta-Ensemble had been chosen on them."));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running. "), T("run_meta_xgb_check.py: two versions of Meta-Ensemble, each run once at 90 sessions and scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90, from the same Quantile-LGBM and ARIMA+GARCH outputs: inherited, the XGBoost member with ML_model's defaults, as served since Section 3.102; and legacy, the XGBoost member with ML_model.LGBM_LEGACY, as validated before Section 3.102. The inherited change stays only if, on the 144 forecasts, (K1) its 20-90 session score is no higher than legacy's, pooled, (K2) its 20-90 session error is no higher than legacy's, pooled, and (K3) its pooled coverage at 20-90 sessions is at least 90% (or no lower than legacy's, if legacy's is under 90%). Otherwise Meta-Ensemble's XGBoost member would be given LGBM_LEGACY explicitly. Per-draw, per-date and per-horizon numbers, and XGBoost on its own, are reported, not ruled on. The rule asks for no worse rather than better, and has no breadth test, because the question was whether to undo a change already shipped and validated on XGBoost.")]));
children.push(P("Meta-Ensemble and its XGBoost member at 20-90 sessions (144 forecasts at 4 horizons; widths and scores as % of price; error as a multiple of the no-change error):"));

children.push(P("K1 passes (80.32% against 81.30%), K2 passes (0.978 against 1.015), K3 passes (95.0%). Decision: the inherited change stays in Meta-Ensemble."));
children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("The margin is small and not even: the score was lower at only 4 of the 6 dates (higher at 2021-04-01 and 2023-07-17), higher on Section 3.110's harder draw (104.81% against 103.29%) and higher at 90 sessions (128.17% against 126.65%); it was lower for 119 of the 144 forecasts (83%), averaged over the four horizons, and the error was lower at 4 of the 6 dates and at every horizon. The inherited version is narrower and covers a little less: width 54.06% against 58.81% of price, coverage 95.0% against 96.4%, and 91.0% against 93.1% at 90 sessions. Meta-Ensemble's error beyond 13 sessions is now just below no change's (0.978): less wrong, not a skilled call on direction, and the app still withholds the directional call beyond 13 sessions. XGBoost's own change held up on a third set of names (83.29% against 90.40%, error 0.964 against 1.038); reported, not ruled on, and Section 3.102 is unchanged. On these draws XGBoost alone had a lower error than Meta-Ensemble (0.964 against 0.978) but a higher score (83.29% against 80.32%); nothing was decided on that comparison. The draws are now used for Meta-Ensemble and XGBoost; the next non-neural tests choose on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87 and confirm on Section 3.95's 72, still unused for Quantile (LGB), Stacking, ARIMA-GARCH, ARIMAX-GARCH, Kalman, Chronos-2 and Prophet. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107).")]));
children.push(P([B("What changed in the code "), T("(behaviour unchanged). meta_ensemble.run_meta_ensemble_prediction is split into run_constituents (runs the three members) and combine (median point, mean bounds, skips a member that failed), so the check could run each member once and combine two ways. A new xgb_kw argument is passed to the XGBoost member on top of model_type; the default, None, passes nothing, so the app is unchanged. Served run: before the run, the served Meta-Ensemble (serving.MODEL_REGISTRY with its default arguments) equalled the check's inherited version exactly (maximum difference 0.0).")]));
children.push(P("Run time: 729 minutes with three workers, far longer than the 3-4 hours estimated: each forecast fits all three members plus a second XGBoost, and the workers competed for the processor."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_meta_ensemble_switch.py (2 tests, new, on stub members with no training): combine takes the median point and the mean bounds and skips a failed member; xgb_kw reaches only the XGBoost member, and None changes nothing. tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 832. Files: run_meta_xgb_check.py, meta_xgb_check.csv, meta_xgb_check_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources "), T("(no new research; the change checked is that of Sections 3.98 and 3.102): Boudoukh, Israel and Richardson, Financial Analysts Journal 2019, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1080/0015198X.2018.1547056"), T("; Campbell and Thompson, Review of Financial Studies 2008, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on Thursday 13 November (five sessions, report only). Then Quantile (LGB), the next non-neural model: research with sources, switches off by default, rules written down first, chosen on the 32 forecasts and confirmed on the 72. Then, in order: Stacking, ARIMA-GARCH and ARIMAX-GARCH, Kalman, Chronos-2 and Prophet."));
children.push(H2("3.112 Quantile (LGB): larger leaves, a shrunk centre and conformalized quantile regression tested, none kept"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-11 as the second non-neural step, after Section 3.111. Quantile (LGB) is the one tree model whose band is not calibrated: its bounds are the raw 2.5% and 97.5% LightGBM quantile fits of each step's cumulative return. Three published fixes and their combinations were tested at five sessions and at 20-90 sessions, under rules written down first. Nothing ships. At five sessions larger leaves were chosen by 0.01 point (20.25% against 20.26%), then lost on the 72 confirming forecasts (24.14% against 24.01%, lower at only 3 of the 6 dates), though their error was lower on both sets. At 20-90 sessions every arm raised the score, so none was eligible, and conformalized quantile regression widened the band rather than narrowing it."));
children.push(P("Why this test. On the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, Quantile (LGB) covered 93.8% at five sessions and 96.9% at 20-90 sessions, more than the 95% asked for at long horizons, so the band there might be wider than needed. A trial run before the rules were set (HDFCBANK from 2022-01-14, one of the 32) showed the wandering centre Section 3.98 found in LightGBM: at 90 sessions the band was 1147-1613 rupees around a median of 1550."));
children.push(P("Research (ideas only; nothing downloaded was run):"));
children.push(table([4200, 3000, 2160], [
  ["Idea", "Source", "Used here"],
  ["Conformalized quantile regression (CQR): score a held-out block by E = max(lo - y, y - hi) and move both bounds by the finite-sample 95% quantile of E; Q is negative when the raw band over-covers, so CQR can also narrow; its guarantee needs exchangeable data", "Romano, Patterson and Candès, NeurIPS 2019, https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.03222, https://github.com/yromano/cqr", "cqr"],
  ["Calibration per horizon for multi-step forecasts", "Stankeviciute, Alaa and van der Schaar, NeurIPS 2021, https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2021/hash/312f1ba2a72318edaaa995a67835fad5-Abstract.html", "Read: cqr is calibrated per step"],
  ["Larger leaves and a centre shrunk toward no change", "Sections 3.98 and 3.102; Boudoukh, Israel and Richardson, https://doi.org/10.1080/0015198X.2018.1547056; Campbell and Thompson, https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055", "leaf, shrink"],
  ["Quantiles that never cross, by sorting", "Chernozhukov, Fernández-Val and Galichon, Econometrica 2010, https://arxiv.org/abs/0704.3649", "Already done, kept"],
  ["A 2026 benchmark of conformal methods for time series (split conformal per horizon most efficient, on sales data)", "Sabashvili, https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18509", "Read, not tested"],
  ["Quantile regression forests; with conformal calibration for Value at Risk", "Meinshausen, https://jmlr.org/papers/v7/meinshausen06a.html; Wang, Liang, Zhang and Zhu, https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01912", "Read, not tested"],
]));
children.push(P("Purged early stopping was tried in Sections 3.98 and 3.102 and chosen in neither, so it was not an arm here."));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(Quantile_model.run_quantile_prediction; all off by default, QUANTILE_LEGACY). leaf_scale: at least min(20 h, a fifth of the rows) rows per leaf (min_leaf), as in Section 3.98. shrink: the three quantiles move together toward no change by the calibration block's least-squares slope of realised on predicted medians, clipped to [0, 1]. cqr: CQR on the most recent 20% of the training rows, with h rows purged before them, as the other tree models' calibration block. Quantile_model.quantile_paths gives every shrink/cqr setting from one set of fits, and a test checks it equals run_quantile_prediction with the same switches. With every switch off the model is unchanged: two five-session forecasts matched the old code exactly (maximum difference 0.0).")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_quantile_improve.py): seven arms (shipped, leaf, shrink, cqr, leaf_shrink, leaf_cqr, all), each run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90). Two scopes, each decided on its own as in Section 3.109: five sessions (a change would become the default on daily bars up to 13 sessions) and 20-90 sessions (beyond 13). Choosing, on the 32 forecasts: an arm is eligible for a scope if its pooled coverage there is at least 90%, its score is lower than shipped's and its error no higher; the eligible arm with the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, once per scope, on Section 3.95's 72 forecasts: a candidate ships only if (1) its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, (2) its error is no higher, pooled, and (3) its pooled coverage is at least 90%. If anything shipped, Meta-Ensemble's Quantile member would have been given QUANTILE_LEGACY explicitly, so it would not change untested (Section 3.111's lesson); nothing shipped, so that did not arise.")]));
children.push(P("Choosing at five sessions (32 forecasts; widths and scores as % of price; error as a multiple of the no-change error):"));

children.push(P("Rule 1 fails (24.14% against 24.01% pooled, lower at 3 of the 6 dates); rule 2 passes (error 0.968 against 0.995); rule 3 passes (95.8%). Decision: nothing ships at five sessions; Quantile (LGB) is unchanged."));
children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("CQR widened the band at every horizon: at 20-90 sessions it took coverage from 96.9% to 99.2% and width from 52.67% to 72.84% of price. Its calibration block, the most recent fifth of the training rows, saw more misses than the raw band made on the test dates, so the correction was positive; with overlapping h-step returns that block holds only a few independent outcomes at long horizons (about two at 90 sessions), so the correction is noisy, which breaks the exchangeability CQR's guarantee needs. Larger leaves lowered the error on both sets and at both scopes but not the score: at five sessions 1.025 to 0.968 on the 32 and 0.995 to 0.968 on the 72; at 20-90 sessions larger leaves with a shrunk centre cut the error from 1.252 to 1.027 while the score rose from 59.73% to 60.75% (lower at 20 and 90 sessions, much higher at 40, 73.17% against 57.95%). That is the GRU's error-first pattern again, a centre that is less wrong inside a band that is no better; a lead, not a result. Quantile's long-horizon centre is worse than no change (error 1.25 times no change's at 20-90 sessions on the 32) and stays as it is; the app already gives no up/down call beyond 13 sessions, and the band covered 96.9%. Shrinking alone made the five-session score worse (21.61% against 20.26%), with coverage and width unchanged: in 9 of the 32 forecasts the calibration slope was at or above 1 and clipped to 1, leaving the forecast as it was, and in the other 23 the centre moved toward no change and missed more. Meta-Ensemble is unaffected, since nothing shipped. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107).")]));
children.push(P([B("Speed, found during the run (a lead, not yet changed). "), T("The model trains 3 LightGBM models per step, 270 for a 90-session forecast, each using every logical processor (n_jobs=-1, 22 here) on about 900 rows; LightGBM's own documentation advises against many threads on a small dataset. Measured: one fit of the three models took 0.23 seconds on one thread against 0.82 on all of them, and whole forecasts about a third of the time (HDFCBANK at 90 sessions, all switch settings: 215 against 661 seconds). The forecasts were identical for HDFCBANK but differed by up to 0.39 rupees on a price of about 550 (0.07%) for SBIN at 20 sessions: LightGBM promises the same result across thread counts only with its deterministic setting, which the model does not use. So fewer threads would change the app's forecasts slightly, and is left for the owner to decide, with its own check. Source: "), Link("https://lightgbm.readthedocs.io/en/latest/Parameters.html"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Run time: 128 minutes to choose and 5 minutes to confirm, far less than the 8-10 hours first estimated."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_quantile_switches.py (3 tests, new): the defaults are QUANTILE_LEGACY and explicit values are kept; adjust_quantiles shrinks the centre and moves the band by CQR's correction, narrowing and widening, without crossing the median; every switch off leaves the forecast unchanged and quantile_paths equals single runs with the same switches. tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 835. Files: run_quantile_improve.py, quantile_improve.csv, quantile_improve_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Romano, Patterson and Candès, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.03222"), T(", "), Link("https://github.com/yromano/cqr"), T("; Stankeviciute, Alaa and van der Schaar, "), Link("https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2021/hash/312f1ba2a72318edaaa995a67835fad5-Abstract.html"), T("; Chernozhukov, Fernández-Val and Galichon, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/0704.3649"), T("; Sabashvili, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18509"), T("; Meinshausen, "), Link("https://jmlr.org/papers/v7/meinshausen06a.html"), T("; Wang, Liang, Zhang and Zhu, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01912"), T("; Boudoukh, Israel and Richardson, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1080/0015198X.2018.1547056"), T("; Campbell and Thompson, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055"), T("; LightGBM, "), Link("https://lightgbm.readthedocs.io/en/latest/Parameters.html"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on Thursday 13 November (five sessions, report only); the thread-count speed-up, if the owner approves it, as its own documented step; then Stacking, followed by ARIMA-GARCH and ARIMAX-GARCH, Kalman, Chronos-2 and Prophet."));
children.push(H2("3.113 Tree models on one thread: faster, the same forecast on any computer, and neutral on accuracy"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12 (\"take which one will be better\"), after they noted that a machine-learning model should not take this long. Every tree model now trains on one thread, with LightGBM's deterministic setting: Quantile (LGB), LightGBM, XGBoost, Stacking, and Meta-Ensemble through its members. Before, each used every logical processor (n_jobs=-1, 22 on the machine used). The change ships: on the 32 forecasts every model passed every rule written down beforehand, at five sessions and at 20-90. It is faster in the app (a 90-session forecast took less time for every model, from 9% for Quantile, 69.7 against 63.5 seconds, to 31% for LightGBM, 12.7 against 8.7; Stacking, timed twice more, took 85 seconds against 100-143), much faster in testing (the 32 forecasts at both horizons took 26 minutes, 8 at a time, against 143 minutes, 1 at a time), and gives the same forecast on any computer: XGBoost's result depended on the number of threads, and \"all processors\" differs from machine to machine."));
children.push(P([B("Why it was slow. "), T("Each model trains on about 1,000 rows, and Quantile fits 3 LightGBM models per step (270 for a 90-session forecast). LightGBM's parameter guide advises against many threads on a small dataset, since starting and joining them costs more than the work they share. With nothing else running, one thread was already faster: one fit took 0.068 against 0.094 seconds (LightGBM quantile), 0.051 against 0.082 (LightGBM Huber) and 0.087 against 0.098 (XGBoost). With several forecasts side by side, as in every validation script, each forecast's 22 threads competed for the same processors: in Section 3.112 one fit of Quantile's three models took 0.82 seconds on all processors against 0.23 on one, and a whole forecast 661 against 215 seconds. That 3-fold gap was mostly this competition, not the gain inside one forecast. Source: "), Link("https://lightgbm.readthedocs.io/en/latest/Parameters.html"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Why the forecasts could move. LightGBM gives the same result on any number of threads only with deterministic, which the models did not use. XGBoost's result changed with the thread count, by up to 0.018 in a predicted log return. Section 3.30 had already found the cross-sectional classifier not reproducible with n_jobs=-1 (abstention 0.9461 against 0.9552 on the same seed), and cross_sectional_model has used one thread with deterministic since then; this step applies the same setting to the served tree models."));
children.push(P("The change (model_utils.LGB_THREADS, XGB_THREADS, lgb_threads(), xgb_threads()), used by ML_model, Quantile_model and Ensemble_model: LightGBM n_jobs=1, deterministic=True, force_col_wise=True; XGBoost n_jobs=1. For checks only, STOCKAPP_TREE_THREADS=legacy restores n_jobs=-1."));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_tree_threads.py): the five models with the app's default arguments, on the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, each run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90), with the old threads and with the new. The new threads stay only if, for every model and in both scopes: (T1) the interval score is at most 2% higher than before (relative); (T2) the error, as a multiple of the no-change error, is at most 0.02 higher; (T3) the coverage is at most one forecast lower at five sessions (1 of 32) and at most 1 point lower at 20-90 sessions. These are noise bounds: the question was whether the change is neutral, not whether it helps. If any model failed any rule, every tree model would have gone back to n_jobs=-1. Speed was reported, not ruled on.")]));
children.push(P("Five sessions (32 forecasts; scores as % of price; error as a multiple of the no-change error):"));

Single timings on this machine vary by up to 40% (100.5 against 143.0 seconds for the same forecast), so the per-model figures are a guide, not a measurement to the second."));
children.push(P([B("What this does and does not change. "), T("Earlier recorded results stay as they were: Sections 3.102, 3.111 and 3.112 were measured on all processors and are not re-run; a re-run now would differ slightly wherever XGBoost is involved, by up to 3.6% in a predicted price. No model's settings changed other than its thread count. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107). Validation scripts can now run more forecasts side by side, since each uses one processor; Stacking, the next model in the plan, is tested that way.")]));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_tree_threads.py (3 tests, new): the settings are one thread, LightGBM is deterministic, and legacy is all processors; every tree model the app builds takes one thread and its LightGBM models are deterministic; STOCKAPP_TREE_THREADS=legacy restores all processors. tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 838. Files: run_tree_threads.py, tree_threads.csv, tree_threads_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("LightGBM, "), Link("https://lightgbm.readthedocs.io/en/latest/Parameters.html"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on Thursday 13 November (five sessions, report only); then Stacking, followed by ARIMA-GARCH and ARIMAX-GARCH, Kalman, Chronos-2 and Prophet."));
children.push(H2("3.114 Stacking beyond 13 sessions: its combiner ignored its members; a shrunk centre with larger leaves and purged chunks shipped"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12 as the third non-neural step, after Sections 3.112 and 3.113. Stacking blends XGBoost, LightGBM and Ridge through a Ridge combiner. That combiner gave its members almost no weight, so the forecast was the average return of an older block of the training data. On daily bars beyond 13 sessions, a centre shrunk toward no change, larger leaves and purged chunks now ship. On the 72 confirming forecasts at 20-90 sessions the score fell from 100.55% to 66.44% of price, lower at all 6 dates; the error fell from 1.79 to 1.20 times no change's; and coverage held at 93.8% (94.1% before). Up to 13 sessions nothing changes: the candidate was lower at only 3 of the 6 dates, and its error was higher."));
children.push(P("What was wrong. For each step, the stack fits its three members on the first 60% of the training rows and predicts the next 20%, then fits Ridge(alpha=1) with an intercept on those predictions. The members' predictions are small numbers, a standard deviation of 0.0003 to 0.07 in log return. Against them alpha = 1 is a heavy penalty, so the weights go to about zero and the forecast is the intercept: the mean h-step return of the 20% block, which ends a fifth of the window (about 180 sessions) before the forecast date. A trial run before the rules were set (HDFCBANK from 2022-01-14, one of the 32) showed it:"));
children.push(table([900, 2700, 1800, 1800, 2160], [
  ["Step", "Weights (XGBoost, LightGBM, Ridge)", "Intercept", "Forecast", "Outcome"],
  ["1", "0.00, 0.00, 0.00", "+0.13%", "+0.13%", "-1.54%"],
  ["20", "-0.01, 0.00, +0.01", "+3.28%", "+3.24%", "-4.73%"],
  ["40", "0.20, 0.15, 0.12", "+6.88%", "+7.25%", "-8.17%"],
  ["90", "0.20, 0.11, 0.14", "+20.02%", "+22.16%", "-8.60%"],
]));
children.push(P("On the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, the stack's error at 20-90 sessions was 1.46 times no change's, and its score 93.9% of price against 58-60% for the other tree models (Section 3.113)."));
children.push(P("Research (ideas only; nothing downloaded was run):"));
children.push(table([3400, 3800, 2160], [
  ["Idea", "Source", "Used here"],
  ["Stacking: combine out-of-sample predictions of several models", "Wolpert, 'Stacked generalization', Neural Networks 1992, https://doi.org/10.1016/S0893-6080(05)80023-1", "The design, unchanged"],
  ["Combine by least squares with non-negative weights", "Breiman, 'Stacked regressions', Machine Learning 1996, https://doi.org/10.1007/BF00117832; SciPy nnls, https://docs.scipy.org/doc/scipy/reference/generated/scipy.optimize.nnls.html", "nnls: non-negative weights, no intercept"],
  ["The forecast combination puzzle: a plain average often beats estimated weights, because the weights are estimated with large error", "Smith and Wallis 2009, https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1468-0084.2008.00541.x; Claeskens, Magnus, Vasnev and Wang 2016, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijforecast.2015.12.005; review by Wang, Hyndman, Li and Kang 2023, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijforecast.2022.11.005, https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.04216", "mean: the plain average of the three members"],
  ["A centre shrunk toward no change; larger leaves for overlapping long-horizon returns", "Sections 3.98 and 3.102; Campbell and Thompson, https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055; Boudoukh, Israel and Richardson, https://doi.org/10.1080/0015198X.2018.1547056", "shrink, leaf"],
  ["Purged splits: drop training rows whose labels reach into the next block", "López de Prado, Advances in Financial Machine Learning 2018, ch. 7, https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/-/9781119482086/", "purge"],
]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(Ensemble_model.run_ensemble_prediction). meta: the combiner, ridge (shipped), nnls or mean (Ensemble_model.combiner). shrink: the stack's return moves toward no change by the calibration block's least-squares slope of realised on predicted returns, clipped to [0, 1], and the band comes from the residuals of the shrunk forecast, as in Section 3.98. leaf_scale: at least min(20 h, a fifth of the rows) rows per leaf in both tree members. purge: h rows dropped at the end of each training chunk inside the stack, so no member's training label reaches into the block it is scored on. Ensemble_model.stack_paths gives every (meta, shrink) setting from one set of fits, and a test checks it equals single runs. With every switch off the model is unchanged: two five-session forecasts matched the old code exactly (maximum difference 0.0). The members still stop early on the block the combiner is fitted on; that is left as it is.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_stack_improve.py): 12 arms, the three combiners each with and without shrink, with the members as shipped and with leaf and purge both on (prefix lp_). Each forecast was run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90), and the two scopes were decided separately, as in Sections 3.109 and 3.112. Choosing, on the 32 forecasts: an arm is eligible for a scope if its pooled coverage there is at least 90%, its score is lower than shipped's and its error no higher; the eligible arm with the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, once per scope, on Section 3.95's 72 forecasts: a candidate ships only if (1) its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, (2) its error is no higher, pooled, and (3) its pooled coverage is at least 90%. No other model uses the stack (Meta-Ensemble's members are Quantile (LGB), XGBoost and ARIMA+GARCH), so nothing else changes either way. A risk named before running: nnls weights have no upper limit; in the trial they turned tiny member predictions into a +1.7% one-day move (with shrink), against +0.1% shipped.")]));
children.push(P("Choosing at five sessions (32 forecasts; widths and scores as % of price; error as a multiple of the no-change error):"));

children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("The gain is largest where the stack was worst: at 90 sessions the score fell from 193.97% to 102.60% and coverage rose from 84.7% to 91.7%; at 20 sessions the change was small (40.53% against 42.24%). Why shrink works here: it scales the forecast by how well the stack's forecasts predicted the calibration block, clipped to [0, 1], and the stale average of an old block predicts little, so the forecast moves toward no change. The stack is still worse than no change beyond 13 sessions (error 1.20 times no change's on the 72); that stays as it is, since the app gives no up/down call beyond 13 sessions and the band covered 93.8%. Non-negative weights without shrink blew up: in lp_nnls, 12 of the 128 forecast-steps at 20-90 sessions had a band wider than twice the price, and the widest reached about 10^21 % of price (HDFCBANK from 2023-01-25, 60 and 90 sessions); in nnls it was 4 of 128, widest 3,160%. The weights have no upper limit, and the calibration fit uses them too; with shrink, neither non-negative arm's band exceeded 174% of price. The plain average lowered the five-session error (0.981 against 1.110) but raised the score (21.10% against 19.68%). Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107).")]));
children.push(P("Served output reproduces the test: the app's Stacking forecast for TCS from 2022-12-19 (one of the 72) matched the test's lp_ridge_shrink rows at 90 sessions (maximum difference 4.5e-13 rupees) and the shipped rows at five sessions (0.0). Run time: 15 minutes to choose and 37 minutes to confirm, 8 forecasts at a time (Section 3.113)."));

children.push(H2("3.115 ARIMA-GARCH and ARIMAX-GARCH: filtered historical simulation, centred regressors and no calendar terms tested, none kept"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12 as the next non-neural step, after Section 3.114. Three published fixes were tested on the two statistical models, each model and scope decided on its own, under rules written down first. Nothing ships; both models are unchanged. ARIMA-GARCH: filtered historical simulation was not eligible at either scope, and raised the error both times. ARIMAX-GARCH at 20-90 sessions: future regressors at their training means, with no calendar terms, cut the score on the 32 forecasts from 78.81% to 59.55%; on the 72 it was lower pooled (61.88% against 67.42%) but at only 2 of the 6 dates, so it failed, though its error was lower on both sets. ARIMAX-GARCH at five sessions: the candidate was lower at only 1 of the 6 dates."));
children.push(P("Why this test. On the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, at 20-90 sessions, ARIMA-GARCH covered 98.4% (100% at 60 and 90 sessions), more than the 95% asked for, with a score of 62.7% of price against 53.1% for Kalman; ARIMAX-GARCH covered 93.0% with a score of 78.8%, missing most at 40-90 sessions. Two trial runs before the rules were set (HDFCBANK from 2022-01-14 and SBIN from 2023-01-25, both among the 32) found that ARIMA-GARCH chose order (0, 0, 0) with no intercept, so its centre is no change, and its band at 90 sessions was close to square-root-of-time scaling. ARIMAX also chose (0, 0, 0) with no intercept, but its regressors act as one: the future bar statistics decay toward fixed neutral levels (RSI 50, the median range and volatility, zero volume ratio and momentum), not their training means, and day of week (0-4) and month (1-12) enter as plain numbers. Over 90 steps the terms added up to a drift:"));
children.push(table([4560, 2400, 2400], [
  ["Contribution over 90 steps", "HDFCBANK", "SBIN"],
  ["RSI (decays toward 50)", "-38.6%", "-37.7%"],
  ["High-low range", "+23.1%", "+23.6%"],
  ["Day of week (0-4)", "+10.9%", "+9.1%"],
  ["Month (1-12)", "+6.9%", "+14.6%"],
  ["Volatility", "-8.9%", "-14.0%"],
  ["ARIMAX forecast, 90 sessions", "-6.6%", "-7.1%"],
  ["Outcome", "-8.6%", "+3.7%"],
]));
children.push(P("Dropping the calendar terms removed most of that drift (90 sessions: +0.5% and +1.3%). Filtered historical simulation added a drift of its own: the standardized residuals average above zero, and resampling them moved the 90-session centre up 5-8%."));
children.push(P("Research (ideas only; nothing downloaded was run):"));
children.push(table([3400, 3800, 2160], [
  ["Idea", "Source", "Used here"],
  ["Filtered historical simulation: draw the shocks from the model's own standardized residuals rather than a fitted distribution", "Barone-Adesi, Giannopoulos and Vosper, Journal of Futures Markets 1999, https://doi.org/10.1002/(SICI)1096-9934(199908)19:5%3C583::AID-FUT5%3E3.0.CO;2-S, https://ideas.repec.org/a/wly/jfutmk/v19y1999i5p583-602.html; arch's forecast(method='bootstrap'), which draws the estimation data's standardized residuals with replacement, https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/latest/univariate/forecasting.html", "fhs, both models"],
  ["Unknown future regressors in a dynamic regression must be forecast or assumed; the book's example uses the historical mean", "Hyndman and Athanasopoulos, Forecasting: Principles and Practice, 3rd ed., section 10.3, https://otexts.com/fpp3/forecasting.html", "centre, ARIMAX"],
  ["Calendar effects enter a regression as dummy variables, not as one number", "Same book, section 7.4, https://otexts.com/fpp3/useful-predictors.html", "calendar=False drops day of week and month; dummies were not tried"],
]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(all off by default). fhs: ARIMA_GARCH.run_statistical_prediction(fhs=) and ARIMAX_model.run_arimax_prediction(fhs=), through model_utils.simulate_price_paths(method='bootstrap'), seeded so it is reproducible. centre: each bar statistic decays toward its mean over the training rows (ARIMAX_model._build_future_exog(centre=)). calendar=False: no day-of-week, month or hour regressors (ARIMAX_model._get_exog_cols). ARIMA_GARCH.arima_paths and ARIMAX_model.arimax_paths give the settings that share a fit, and a test checks they equal single runs. With every switch off both models are unchanged: forecasts at 5 and 20 sessions for HDFCBANK and SBIN matched the old code exactly (maximum difference 0.0), for ARIMA-GARCH, ARIMAX-GARCH and ARIMAX without regressors. ARIMAX without regressors, the meta-ensemble's member (Section 3.111), takes no default from these switches, so it cannot change through them.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_arima_improve.py). Arms: ARIMA-GARCH shipped and fhs; ARIMAX-GARCH every combination of centre, nocal and fhs (8 arms), plus a reference arm noexog (no regressors), reported and never eligible. Each forecast was run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90). Choosing, on the 32 forecasts: an arm is eligible if its pooled coverage is at least 90%, its score is lower than shipped's and its error no higher; the eligible arm with the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, once per model and scope, on Section 3.95's 72 forecasts: a candidate ships only if (1) its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, (2) its error is no higher, pooled, and (3) its pooled coverage is at least 90%.")]));

children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("The drift's harm depends on the date: on the 72, shipped ARIMAX did much better than on the 32 (score 67.42% against 78.81%, error 1.115 against 1.207); the drift points one way for all stocks at a given date, so it either helps or hurts a whole date together. Centred regressors without calendar terms lowered the 20-90 session error on both sets (1.207 to 1.024 on the 32, 1.115 to 1.026 on the 72) but not the score at most dates; a lead, not a result, since an error-first test would need its own rules and an unused draw. ARIMAX without regressors (the reference arm) beat every ARIMAX arm at five sessions on the 32 (17.18%) and shipped ARIMAX at 20-90 sessions (62.43% against 78.81%), which agrees with Section 3.60's finding that ARIMAX's regressors made it worse; it was not eligible by design, since ARIMAX without regressors would be ARIMA-GARCH again, and whether to keep ARIMAX is the owner's decision (Section 3.107). The drift that filtered historical simulation adds was the risk named beforehand; it raised ARIMA-GARCH's error at both scopes. Meta-Ensemble is unaffected, since nothing shipped and its member takes no default from these switches. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107).")]));
children.push(P("Run time: 2 minutes to choose and 2 to confirm, 8 forecasts at a time. Checks: tests/test_arima_switches.py (3 tests, new): the defaults are ARIMA_LEGACY and ARIMAX_LEGACY, explicit values are kept, and calendar=False drops every calendar column; future regressors start at the last bar and reach the neutral levels, or with centre the training means; every switch off leaves both models unchanged, the paths functions equal single runs, and ARIMAX without regressors takes no default. tools/ui_check.py scenarios passes. The suite was then 844. Files: run_arima_improve.py, arima_improve.csv, arima_improve_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Barone-Adesi, Giannopoulos and Vosper, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1002/(SICI)1096-9934(199908)19:5%3C583::AID-FUT5%3E3.0.CO;2-S"), T(", "), Link("https://ideas.repec.org/a/wly/jfutmk/v19y1999i5p583-602.html"), T("; arch, "), Link("https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/latest/univariate/forecasting.html"), T("; Hyndman and Athanasopoulos, "), Link("https://otexts.com/fpp3/forecasting.html"), T(", "), Link("https://otexts.com/fpp3/useful-predictors.html"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on Thursday 13 November (five sessions, report only); then Kalman, followed by Chronos-2 and Prophet."));
children.push(H2("3.116 Kalman: an adaptive quantile level and per-horizon calibration tested, none kept"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12 as the next non-neural step, after Section 3.115. Kalman's band is the 95% quantile of its one-step errors times the square root of h. Two published fixes and their combination were tested under rules written down first; they change only the band, so the centre and error are unchanged. Nothing ships; Kalman is unchanged. Both candidates were lower at only 3 of the 6 dates on the 72 confirming forecasts: at five sessions the adaptive quantile level scored 22.59% against 23.31% pooled; at 20-90 sessions the adaptive level with per-horizon calibration scored higher, 61.88% against 59.06%, with 88.9% coverage at 90 sessions."));
children.push(P("Why this test. On the 32 forecasts, Kalman covered 90.6% at five sessions (Section 3.87), below the 95% asked for, and 97.7% at 20-90 sessions (Section 3.84), above it, with the lowest interval score of any model there (53.1% of price). The band is the 95% quantile of the absolute one-step innovations over the last 20% of the window, times the square root of h. Too narrow near and too wide far is what that gives when volatility clusters and the tails are fat: far out, the one-step tail quantile overstates the tail of an h-step sum; near, a static quantile misses a recent change in volatility."));
children.push(P("Research (ideas only; nothing downloaded was run):"));
children.push(table([3400, 3800, 2160], [
  ["Idea", "Source", "Used here"],
  ["Adaptive conformal inference: walk the score stream in time order and move the quantile level after each miss or cover", "Gibbs and Candès, 'Adaptive conformal inference under distribution shift', 2021, https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.00170; already model_utils.aci_quantile, and the dynamically tuned form is HAR-RV's shipped cone (Section 3.101)", "aci"],
  ["Calibration per horizon for multi-step forecasts", "Stankeviciute, Alaa and van der Schaar, 'Conformal time-series forecasting', NeurIPS 2021, https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2021/hash/312f1ba2a72318edaaa995a67835fad5-Abstract.html", "hstep: each step takes the quantile of the tail's own h-step errors, from the filtered state at each origin"],
  ["Volatility-scaled (locally weighted) scores", "Lei, G'Sell, Rinaldo, Tibshirani and Wasserman, JASA 2018, https://arxiv.org/abs/1604.04173", "Not tested: Section 3.85 tested exactly this (RiskMetrics volatility on the no-change forecast), better in the 2020 crash and wider and worse the rest of the time"],
]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(kalman_model.run_kalman_prediction; both off by default, KALMAN_LEGACY). aci: the one-step quantile's level adapts along the calibration stream (model_utils.aci_quantile, target 5%, learning rate 0.05). hstep: step h's half-width is the 95% quantile of the tail's h-step errors (kalman_model.hstep_errors); at h = 1 these are the one-step innovations, so the first step is unchanged, and below 20 errors a step falls back to the one-step rule. kalman_model.kalman_paths gives every setting from one fit, and a test checks it equals single runs. With both switches off the model is unchanged: forecasts at 5 and 90 sessions for HDFCBANK and SBIN matched the old code exactly (maximum difference 0.0). A risk named before running: in a trial (HDFCBANK from 2022-01-14, one of the 32), hstep halved the 90-session band (log width 0.25 against 0.51); the tail holds about 200 sessions, so its 90-session errors come from one or two independent outcomes, the weakness of Section 3.112's conformalized quantile regression.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_kalman_improve.py): arms shipped, aci, hstep and aci_hstep, each run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90), the two scopes decided separately with the rules of Sections 3.112 and 3.115. Choosing, on the 32 forecasts: eligible if coverage is at least 90%, the score lower than shipped's and the error no higher; the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, on Section 3.95's 72 forecasts: the score lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, the error no higher, and coverage at least 90%. Rule 2 always holds here, since the switches do not move the centre.")]));

children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Gibbs and Candès, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.00170"), T("; Stankeviciute, Alaa and van der Schaar, "), Link("https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2021/hash/312f1ba2a72318edaaa995a67835fad5-Abstract.html"), T("; Lei, G'Sell, Rinaldo, Tibshirani and Wasserman, "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/1604.04173"), T(".")]));

children.push(H2("3.117 Prophet: a flat trend with its change shrunk toward no change, shipped on daily bars and back beyond 13 sessions"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12 as the last non-neural step for now. Chronos-2 is deferred: the owner chose to do Prophet first, because a Chronos-2 gain on pre-2025 data could be pretraining contamination and would need data from the reserved period to confirm. Two switches and their combination were tested under rules written down first. On daily bars, a flat trend with Prophet's predicted change shrunk toward no change now ships at every horizon, and Prophet runs again beyond 13 sessions. On the 72 confirming forecasts at five sessions the score fell from 29.47% to 25.66% of price, lower at 5 of the 6 dates, and the error from 1.32 to 0.98 times no change's; at 20-90 sessions the score fell from 125.09% to 60.56%, lower at all 6 dates, the error from 2.15 to 1.01, and coverage rose from 91.0% to 96.9%. Hourly bars keep the shipped model."));
children.push(P("Why this test. Section 3.90 found that every configuration of Prophet forecast worse than no change at every horizon, and shipped the least bad one: the forecast starts from the last price, the trend is stiffer, and there are no monthly or quarterly seasonalities. On forecasts it was not chosen on, its error was still 1.27 times no change's at five sessions and 1.5-2.0 times at 20-90 sessions, and its range there held 89.1%, below the 90% bar, so Prophet was left out beyond 13 sessions (gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED). Its band (Section 3.93) is split-conformal on 20 past origins, refitted as the forecast is."));
children.push(P("Research (ideas only; nothing downloaded was run):"));
children.push(table([3400, 3800, 2160], [
  ["Idea", "Source", "Used here"],
  ["Shrink a return forecast toward no change by how well it has predicted: the change that shipped for LightGBM, XGBoost and the stack beyond 13 sessions", "Campbell and Thompson, Review of Financial Studies 2008, https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055; Sections 3.98, 3.102, 3.114", "shrink: per step, beta = clip(sum(p y) / sum(p p), 0, 1) over the 20 calibration origins, and the band from the shrunk errors"],
  ["A flat trend, for series with seasonality rather than trend changes; the documentation warns that without a constant trend the noise term absorbs it", "Prophet documentation, 'Flat trend', https://meta.github.io/prophet/docs/additional_topics.html; Section 3.90's diagnosis (flat with weekly seasonality only: the lowest 90-day error, 1.10 times no change, but a worse five-day one, 1.42)", "flat: growth='flat' and no yearly seasonality"],
  ["The model", "Taylor and Letham, 'Forecasting at scale', The American Statistician 2018, https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.2017.1380080, https://peerj.com/preprints/3190.html", "Background"],
]));
children.push(P([B("The switches "), T("(fb_prophet_model.run_prophet_prediction). The calibration refits already gave, at each of the 20 origins, Prophet's forecast and the actual prices; calibration_logs now also returns the log price at each origin, so the predicted and realised changes are known and shrink costs no extra fit. prophet_paths gives both shrink settings from one set of fits, and a test checks it equals single runs. With both switches off the model is unchanged: forecasts at 5 and 90 sessions for HDFCBANK and SBIN matched the old code exactly (maximum difference 0.0). Seen in a trial before the rules were set (HDFCBANK from 2022-01-14 and SBIN from 2023-01-25, both among the 32): shrink cut HDFCBANK's 90-session drift from -33.5% to -10.6% (outcome -8.6%), but its per-step slopes come from 20 overlapping origins and ranged from 0.03 to 0.89 on SBIN; flat moved SBIN's forecast +16.9% at step 1.")]));
children.push(P([B("Written down before running "), T("(run_prophet_improve.py): arms shipped, shrink, flat and flat_shrink, each run once at five sessions and once at 90 (scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90), the two scopes decided separately with the rules of Sections 3.112 to 3.116. Choosing, on the 32 forecasts: eligible if coverage is at least 90%, the score lower than shipped's and the error no higher; the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, on Section 3.95's 72 forecasts: the score lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, the error no higher, and coverage at least 90%. A long-horizon pass also takes Prophet off gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED, since rule 3 is Section 3.90's bar for that list.")]));
