children.push(P("Rule 1 passes (60.56% against 125.09% pooled, lower at all 6 dates); rule 2 passes (error 1.007 against 2.149); rule 3 passes (96.9%). Decision: flat_shrink ships on daily bars at every horizon (fb_prophet_model.PROPHET_DAILY, through resolve_switches), and Prophet comes off gating.LONG_HORIZON_EXCLUDED, which now holds only Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM. Hourly bars keep PROPHET_SWITCHES_LEGACY."));
children.push(P([B("What this does and does not show. "), T("Prophet now mostly forecasts no change: in 40-48% of the forecast-steps (44% at five sessions on the 32, 40% on the 72; 48% and 44% at 20-90) the slope was clipped to 0, so the forecast was exactly the last price, and the rest were shrunk toward it. The gain comes from removing what Prophet's trend added, not from Prophet finding a signal. Its error is now about no change's, not better: 0.98 at five sessions and 1.01 at 20-90 on the 72. The card note shown when Prophet runs now says so: \"On daily NSE data before 2025, Prophet's own trend and seasonality forecast worse than no change (README section 89). On daily bars its predicted change is now shrunk toward no change by how well it predicted at 20 recent dates, often to none, and its error is then about that of a no-change forecast (README section 116). Weigh its range, not its line.\" Its band is the calibrated part: it comes from the shrunk forecast's own errors at the 20 origins, and held 95.8% and 96.9% on the 72. The slopes are noisy (20 overlapping origins per step); the confirmation on a fresh draw is what makes the change safe to ship, not the slopes themselves. No other model uses Prophet, so nothing else changes. Every model stays selected by default (Section 3.107).")]));
children.push(P("Served output reproduces the test: the app's Prophet forecast for TCS from 2022-12-19 (one of the 72) matched the test's flat_shrink rows at 90 sessions (maximum difference 4.5e-13 rupees) and at five sessions (0.0). Run time: 13 minutes to choose and 30 to confirm, 8 forecasts at a time."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_prophet_switches.py (3 tests, new): the defaults are PROPHET_DAILY on daily bars and PROPHET_SWITCHES_LEGACY hourly, explicit values are kept, and shrink_band follows its formula and clips the slope; the flat trend has no trend and no yearly seasonality; both switches off leave the forecast unchanged, prophet_paths equals single runs, and the shrunk change is beta times the unshrunk one. tests/test_prophet_band.py: the configuration test now also checks that flat reaches every refit, on daily bars by default and not hourly; the anchoring test runs with the switches off; the card-note test checks the new note. tests/test_gates.py: Prophet is no longer left out beyond 13 sessions. tools/ui_check.py scenarios uses Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM as the left-out model at 60 days, and passes. The suite was then 850. Files: run_prophet_improve.py, prophet_improve.csv, prophet_improve_parts.csv."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Taylor and Letham, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.2017.1380080"), T(", "), Link("https://peerj.com/preprints/3190.html"), T("; Prophet documentation, "), Link("https://meta.github.io/prophet/docs/additional_topics.html"), T("; Campbell and Thompson, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Next in the plan: the weekly cutoff check after the close on Thursday 13 November (five sessions, report only); Chronos-2, once the owner decides how to confirm a result on data after its pretraining."));
children.push(H2("3.118 Hourly forecasts: a saved copy of hourly bars, and a split that reads nothing from the reserved period"));
children.push(P("Approved by the owner on 2026-11-12, after a check of every fix in the app showed that all of them are daily-only: on hourly bars every model still has its original settings. The owner asked for hourly forecasts to be improved the way the daily ones were. This section sets up the data; no model has been changed for hourly yet."));
children.push(P("Why the daily method could not simply be copied. The project's own history is daily: NSE bhavcopy files have one row per stock per day, so there were no hourly bars from before the reserved period on disk. The app's hourly bars come live from Yahoo Finance (get_data.get_stock_data_using_yfinance, period=\"730d\"), so they move forward every day and a test on them could not be repeated. And live data is blocked in the test runner (tools/run_drivers.py), because it reaches into the reserved period (Section 3.22)."));
children.push(P("What was approved and done. One download, saved and never refreshed (tools/hourly_cache.py download): the app's own call, yf.Ticker(SYMBOL.NS).history(period=\"730d\", interval=\"1h\", auto_adjust=False), with the app's column cleaning, for the 20 names of the daily tests. The download is in the holdout ledger, and the tool refuses a second one. A manifest (tests/validation/hourly_cache/manifest.json) records the download time, the yfinance version (1.4.1), each file's rows and dates, and a SHA-256 fingerprint; every load checks its file against the manifest first, and python tools/hourly_cache.py verify checks them all."));
children.push(P("What the download found. 19 of 20 names saved: TATAMOTORS returned no bars, because Tata Motors split into two listed companies in 2025 and Yahoo has nothing under the old symbol. The bars start on 2023-11-29, not late 2024: for hourly bars Yahoo's 730 days are trading days, so about 13 months (about 1,870 bars per stock) are from before the reserved period. The data is clean: every one of the 267 sessions before 2025 has 7 bars (09:15 to 15:15), and there are no zero or missing prices."));
children.push(P("The split the owner chose, after that finding: the daily tests' design, with nothing read from the reserved period."));
children.push(table([1400, 5000, 2960], [
  ["Stage", "Names", "Bars it reads"],
  ["Choose", "HDFCBANK, HEROMOTOCO, IOC, KOTAKBANK, LT, SBIN, SUNPHARMA, YESBANK (the 8 names of the 32 daily choosing forecasts)", "2023-11-29 .. 2024-12-31"],
  ["Confirm", "BPCL, DLF, HINDALCO, HINDUNILVR, ICICIBANK, INDUSINDBK, LUPIN, ONGC, PNB, RELIANCE, TCS (Section 3.95's confirming names but TATAMOTORS)", "2023-11-29 .. 2024-12-31"],
  ["Reserved", "every name", "from 2025-01-01: refused unless opened with the owner's approval, and then ledgered"],
]));
children.push(P([B("How the split is enforced. "), T("hourly_cache.load(symbol, stage, purpose) returns a stage's bars only for its own names, and only up to 2024-12-31. The bars from 2025 on are in the copy, because the download could not avoid them, but no stage reads them; opening them takes allow_holdout=True, given only with the owner's approval, and is written to the ledger. This replaces the split first approved (choose to 2025-10-31, confirm November 2025 to April 2026), which would have read inside the daily reserved period; the manifest keeps both, the one at download time and the one in force. Only hourly settings may be chosen on these bars. The months July to December 2024 were the daily work's confirmation window once; they have never been used for hourly bars. Chronos-2's clean test is not part of this approval: it would need bars after October 2025, which stay reserved.")]));
children.push(P("What the hourly tests will measure. Hourly runs in the app offer 1, 2, 3 or 5 sessions, which is 7, 14, 21 or 35 bars, all within the 13-session up/down horizon. So hourly has one scope: a 35-bar run, scored at bars 7, 14, 21 and 35, with forecast origins from about May to December 2024. The models keep up to 500-750 bars of history, about 3.5 months."));
children.push(P("Next: the hourly model programme, one model at a time, with the daily rules: choose on the choosing names, confirm once on the confirming names, and rules written down before each run. A shipped hourly change would apply to hourly bars only."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_hourly_cache.py (3 tests, new): the download keeps the app's columns, refuses a second copy, and is ledgered; each stage reads only its own names and only bars before 2025, research loads write nothing to the ledger, and the reserved period is refused unless opened, then ledgered; a changed or missing file is caught. python tools/hourly_cache.py verify: every file matches the manifest. The suite was then 853. Files: tools/hourly_cache.py, tests/validation/hourly_cache/ (19 files and the manifest), tests/test_hourly_cache.py."));
children.push(H2("3.119 Hourly bars, step 1: the fast models' daily fixes tested on hourly bars; Prophet's shipped, the other four kept as they were"));
children.push(P("The first step of the hourly programme (Section 3.118). Every change shipped before this section was chosen on daily bars and applies to daily bars only. Five of them belong to models that run in minutes, so they were the cheapest and best-supported candidates for hourly bars. Each was tested on its own, against that model's current hourly settings, with the rules written in tests/validation/run_hourly_fast.py before it ran."));
children.push(table([1800, 5160, 2400], [
  ["Model", "Candidate (its daily change)", "From"],
  ["LightGBM", "larger leaves and a shrunk centre", "Section 3.98"],

  ["Stacking", "shrunk centre, larger leaves, purged chunks (STACK_LONG)", "Section 3.114"],

children.push(P("How it was tested (hourly bars before 2025 only). One scope: hourly runs in the app are 7, 14, 21 or 35 bars (1 to 5 sessions), so each forecast is one 35-bar run, scored at bars 7, 14, 21 and 35; the origin is the last bar (15:15) of the origin date. Choosing: the 8 choosing names at 4 origins (2024-05-21, 07-16, 09-10, 11-05), 32 forecasts; a candidate went on if its pooled coverage was at least 90%, its interval score lower and its error no higher. Confirming, once: the 11 confirming names at 6 other origins (2024-05-28, 06-25, 08-06, 09-24, 10-22, 12-03), 66 forecasts, with no two origins' outcome windows overlapping; a candidate ships only if its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 origins, its error is no higher, and its pooled coverage is at least 90%. The data: Yahoo does not adjust intraday bars (adj_close equals close), so an ex-dividend day is a small drop, as in the app; there were no splits before 2025; both arms saw the same bars."));
children.push(P("Choosing (32 forecasts): all five candidates went on."));

children.push(P("What it shows. Prophet's hourly forecasts had the same fault as its daily ones: its own trend made its price error 3.7 times that of a no-change forecast, and its range 52% wide. With a flat trend and its change shrunk toward no change, its error is about no change's (1.015) and its score fell from 57.95% to 22.73%, better at every one of the 6 origins and at every bar from 7 to 35. The other four candidates narrowed their ranges and cut their error, but lost on the range score at 2 or 3 of the 6 origins. The losses were at 2024-08-06, 2024-09-24 and 2024-10-22, in the large moves of August to October 2024: at those three origins the narrower ranges held 89-91% of outcomes, against 91-93% for the current ones. On the choosing set three of them had also been better at only 3 of 4 origins (HAR-RV at 4 of 4). The rule asks for 5 of 6, so none of them is kept. HAR-RV's error is no change's in both arms, because its centre is the last price; DtACI changed only its range, and it was no better at 4 of 6 origins. The confirming scores are about twice the choosing ones (about 21% against 11%) for every model and both arms, because the confirming origins include larger moves: the no-change error averaged 3.1% at 2024-10-22 against 1.4% at 2024-12-03."));
children.push(P("What changed in the app. Prophet on hourly bars now uses the flat trend with the shrunk change (fb_prophet_model.PROPHET_HOURLY; resolve_switches picks it on hourly bars, and PROPHET_SWITCHES_LEGACY restores the old behaviour). Its daily settings are unchanged. The app's default run reproduces the tested forecasts to within 1e-12 (checked on two confirming name/origin pairs). Prophet's card note now cites this section for hourly bars as well. LightGBM, XGBoost, Stacking and HAR-RV keep their hourly settings, and their daily settings are unchanged. Meta-Ensemble needs no change: the rule written down before the run was to pin its XGBoost member's hourly settings if XGBoost's change shipped (the lesson of Section 3.111), and it did not ship."));
children.push(P("Next on hourly bars (step 2, not started): the neural models' own-returns input, which helped several of them on daily bars. That step is slower, and it starts only when the owner agrees."));

children.push(H2("3.120 Hourly bars, step 2: the neural models' own-returns input tested on hourly bars; shipped on N-BEATS and the Transformer"));

children.push(table([1300, 1150, 1300, 1200, 1600, 1500, 1310], [
  ["Model", "Score, now", "Score, candidate", "Origins better", "Error (× no change)", "Coverage", "Result"],

children.push(P("What it shows. N-BEATS gained the most, as it did on daily bars (Section 3.95): on the nine features its hourly error was 1.34 times a no-change forecast's and its range 17.3% wide; on its own returns the error is 1.09 times and the range 13.4%. Its score is 10% lower, at 5 of 6 origins (it lost at 2024-05-28). The Transformer passed by a small margin, again as on daily bars (Section 3.104): its score is 1% lower (22.61% to 22.35%), at 5 of 6 origins (it lost at 2024-09-24), and its error a little lower. CNN-LSTM's gain on the choosing set did not hold: on the confirming set its current hourly version was already about as accurate as no change (error 1.044); own returns made it worse at 5 of 6 origins and raised its error to 1.179. TCN repeated its daily pattern the other way round: on daily bars (Section 3.106) its range score was lower at every date but its error a little higher; on hourly bars its error fell sharply (1.331 to 1.031) but its score was lower at only 4 of 6 origins. The rule asks for 5, so it is not kept; the error result is a lead. The GRU and the LSTM were worse on own returns already on the choosing set, as on daily bars at 13 sessions or fewer, where their own-returns input was not kept either."));
children.push(P("What changed in the app. N-BEATS on hourly bars now reads its own returns (NBeats_model.DEFAULT_INPUTS['hourly'] = 'returns'; inputs='features' restores the old network). The Transformer on hourly bars now reads its own returns with window scaling (Transformer_model.DEFAULTS['hourly']; Transformer_model.LEGACY restores the old network). Both apply to hourly bars only; their daily settings were already these. The app's default runs reproduce the tested forecasts to within 1e-12 (N-BEATS on TCS and the Transformer on PNB, confirming origins). CNN-LSTM, GRU, LSTM and TCN keep the nine features on hourly bars. No neural model is a Meta-Ensemble member, so nothing else moves."));
children.push(P("Where the hourly programme stands: every daily change that is chosen by bar size has now been tested on hourly bars. Three of the ten carried over (Prophet in Section 3.119, N-BEATS and the Transformer here). ARIMA-GARCH, ARIMAX-GARCH, Kalman, Quantile (LGB) and Hybrid ARIMA+LSTM kept no daily change, so they have nothing to carry over; Regime Switching's and Vol-LSTM's fixes (Sections 3.89, 3.84) and the one-thread setting (Section 3.113) do not depend on bar size; Chronos-2 is not in the programme."));
children.push(P("Sources: no new research; each candidate is an input already built for daily bars. Oreshkin, Carpov, Chapados and Bengio, N-BEATS: https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.10437. Nie et al., PatchTST: https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14730. Fischer and Krauss, European Journal of Operational Research 2018: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejor.2017.11.054. Bai, Kolter and Koltun: https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.01271."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_nbeats_improve.py: the hourly default is own returns, and a default hourly run equals one with inputs='returns'. tests/test_transformer_improve.py: the hourly default is own returns with window scaling, and a default hourly run equals the tested candidate. The suite was then 853. Files: NBeats_model.py, Transformer_model.py, tests/test_nbeats_improve.py, tests/test_transformer_improve.py, tests/validation/run_hourly_neural.py, tests/validation/hourly_neural.csv, tests/validation/hourly_neural_parts.csv."));
children.push(H2("3.121 Cutoff checks: a weekend session file left every stock out; the tool now counts weekdays only, as the loader does"));
children.push(P("The weekly cutoff batch from 2026-11-05 (Section 3.88), started on 2026-11-13, stopped before any forecast: every one of the 20 stocks was left out with \"no prices for the cutoff and the 5 days after it\". No model ran, and nothing was scored."));
children.push(P("The cause: two parts of the code disagreed about which days are sessions. The cutoff tool counted every saved NSE file as a session, and one of them is Sunday 2026-11-08, NSE's Diwali Muhurat session (a short evening session), saved by a fetch on 2026-11-09; so the tool's 5 scored days were 6, 8, 9, 11 and 12 November. The loader reads weekdays only: bhavcopy.load_cache (and fetch_range, which downloads) walk pd.bdate_range, so the Sunday file is never in a stock's history. Each stock had 4 prices after the cutoff, not 5, and the tool left it out. It had not shown before because the four earlier batches were imported from the October run, not chosen by the tool; the Sunday file is the only weekend file in the cache."));
children.push(P("The fix: cutoff_check.choose_cutoff now drops weekend days before choosing the cutoff and its scored days, so it agrees with the loader. Weekdays only is the consistent choice: earlier weekend Muhurat sessions (for example Sunday 2023-11-12) were never downloaded, so no model was ever trained or scored on one. A 5 November cutoff now scores 6, 9, 11, 12 and 13 November (10 November has no NSE file); the tool refuses the batch until NSE publishes 13 November's file after that day's close, then runs it. The empty batch was removed from the batch list: it had stopped with 0 stocks done and no saved rows. Its two reads stay in the holdout ledger: the batch start, and the diagnosis, which read prices from 2026-10-01 only. Report only, as before: no model, gate or threshold changed."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_cutoff_check.py: a new test that a weekend session file is not a scored day (the Sunday is skipped, a batch is refused until the fifth weekday is saved, and the Sunday cannot be a cutoff). The suite was then 854. Files: tools/cutoff_check.py, tests/test_cutoff_check.py, tests/validation/cutoff_checks/batches.csv."));
children.push(H2("3.122 Meta-Ensemble: the mean of its members, the median of their bounds and a pooled band tested; none kept"));
children.push(P("The last daily model in the programme apart from Chronos-2. Meta-Ensemble runs three members (Quantile (LGB), XGBoost, and ARIMA on ARIMAX's returns) and combines them per step: the median of their point forecasts, and the mean of their lower and upper bounds. Before this section only the XGBoost change it inherited had been checked (Section 3.111). On the 32 forecasts its range held 93.8% of outcomes at five sessions and 96.1% at 20-90, and its error was about no change's. This section tested how the members are combined, with the rules written in tests/validation/run_meta_improve.py before it ran."));
children.push(P("Research (sources verified; ideas only, nothing downloaded was run). Averaging the bounds is quantile averaging: Lichtendahl, Grushka-Cockayne and Winkler (Management Science 2013, https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.1120.1667) show it is always sharper than averaging the distributions, and it did better on the Survey of Professional Forecasters. Averaging the distributions is a linear pool: Gneiting and Ranjan (Electronic Journal of Statistics 2013, https://doi.org/10.1214/13-EJS823) show a linear pool of calibrated forecasts is overdispersed (too wide); it is the candidate that widens the band when the members disagree. The median of the members' bounds and of their point forecasts is SCUM (Petropoulos and Svetunkov, International Journal of Forecasting 2020, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijforecast.2019.01.006), 6th of the M4 competition's methods for point forecasts and for prediction intervals; it is robust to one member's band being far wider than the others'. A plain mean of the point forecasts: Smith and Wallis 2009, the forecast combination puzzle. With three members the median is the middle one."));
children.push(table([1500, 1000, 3360, 3500], [
  ["Arm", "Point", "Band", "Source"],
  ["shipped", "median", "mean of the members' bounds (quantile averaging)", "—"],
  ["point_mean", "mean", "mean of the members' bounds", "Smith and Wallis 2009 (the forecast combination puzzle)"],
  ["band_median", "median", "median of the members' bounds", "SCUM: Petropoulos and Svetunkov 2020"],
  ["band_pool", "median", "linear pool: an equal mixture of the members' bands", "Gneiting and Ranjan 2013; Lichtendahl et al. 2013"],
]));
children.push(P("How it was tested. The members ran once per forecast and horizon, and every arm combined the same member forecasts, so the arms differ only in the combination; the switches are meta_ensemble.combine(point=..., band=...), and META_LEGACY reproduces the shipped model exactly (difference 0.0). Two scopes, each decided on its own: five sessions, and one 90-session run scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90. Choosing: the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87; an arm is eligible if its pooled coverage is at least 90%, its score lower and its error no higher, and the eligible arm with the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, once: Section 3.95's draw (72 forecasts), on which nothing about Meta-Ensemble had been chosen; a candidate ships only if its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 origins, its error is no higher, and its coverage is at least 90%. It took 41 minutes (choosing 11, confirming 30)."));
children.push(P("Choosing (32 forecasts)."));
children.push(table([1500, 1300, 1200, 1100, 1500, 1500, 1260], [
  ["Arm", "Coverage, 5", "Score, 5", "Error, 5", "Coverage, 20-90", "Score, 20-90", "Error, 20-90"],
  ["shipped", "93.8%", "18.74%", "1.009", "96.1%", "58.34%", "1.020"],
  ["point_mean", "93.8%", "18.73%", "1.007", "96.1%", "57.57%", "0.981"],
  ["band_median", "96.9%", "18.42%", "1.009", "96.9%", "57.84%", "1.020"],
  ["band_pool", "93.8%", "19.40%", "1.009", "96.9%", "63.09%", "1.020"],
]));
children.push(P("At five sessions point_mean and band_median were eligible, and band_median had the lower score; it was better at only 2 of the 4 origins. At 20-90 sessions point_mean and band_median were eligible, and point_mean had the lower score; it was better at 2 of 4. The pool was not eligible at either scope: it was wider and scored higher, as Gneiting and Ranjan's result predicts."));
children.push(P("Confirming (72 forecasts): neither candidate passed."));
children.push(table([1400, 1300, 1600, 1100, 1500, 1400, 1060], [
  ["Scope", "Candidate", "Score", "Origins better", "Error (× no change)", "Coverage", "Result"],
  ["Five sessions", "band_median", "22.57% → 22.68%", "4 of 6", "0.987 → 0.987", "95.8% → 97.2%", "not kept (rule 1)"],
  ["20-90 sessions", "point_mean", "55.89% → 55.82%", "5 of 6", "1.014 → 1.035", "98.3% → 98.6%", "not kept (rule 2)"],
]));
children.push(P("What it shows. How the three members are combined hardly matters: every arm's score was within 2% of shipped's on the choosing set, and within 0.5% on the confirming set, except the pool. The members' forecasts are close together, so their median, their mean, and the mean or median of their bounds are close too. The mean of the point forecasts helped on the 32 but not on the 72: on the 32 it lowered the 20-90 session error from 1.020 to 0.981; on the 72 it raised it from 1.014 to 1.035, worst at 90 sessions (0.985 to 1.042), so rule 2 failed. At 20-90 sessions Meta-Ensemble's range is wider than it needs to be: on the 72 it held 98.3% of outcomes against the 95% asked for. That width comes from the members' own bands, not from the combination: the median of the bounds was only 1-4% narrower. Narrowing it would mean changing the members; Quantile (LGB)'s band was tested in Section 3.112 and nothing was kept. This is a lead, not a change."));
children.push(P("What changed in the app: nothing. Meta-Ensemble keeps the median of its members and the mean of their bounds at every horizon. The switches stay in meta_ensemble, off by default (resolve_switches returns META_LEGACY). Where the programme stands: of the models in the programme's plan, only Chronos-2 is left (Regime Switching and Vol-LSTM had fixes before the programme, Sections 3.89 and 3.84, and were not in its plan). A Chronos-2 test needs data after its training cut-off, which is in the reserved period, and that is the owner's decision."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Lichtendahl, Grushka-Cockayne and Winkler, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.1120.1667"), T(", "), Link("https://ideas.repec.org/a/inm/ormnsc/v59y2013i7p1594-1611.html"), T("; Gneiting and Ranjan, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1214/13-EJS823"), T(", "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/1106.1638"), T("; Petropoulos and Svetunkov, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijforecast.2019.01.006"), T(", "), Link("https://ideas.repec.org/a/eee/intfor/v36y2020i1p110-115.html"), T("; Smith and Wallis, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1468-0084.2008.00541.x"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_meta_combine.py (3 tests, new): the defaults are the median point and the mean band, and unknown choices are refused; the mean point and the median band are what they say; the pool equals a member's band when the members agree, spans both when they are far apart, and its 2.5% point is where the mixture's distribution function is 0.025. The suite was then 857. Files: meta_ensemble.py, tests/test_meta_combine.py, tests/validation/run_meta_improve.py, tests/validation/meta_improve.csv, tests/validation/meta_improve_parts.csv."));
children.push(H2("3.123 Chronos-2: the target alone, a band from past errors and a shrunk centre tested; none kept"));
children.push(P("The last model in the programme. Chronos-2 is a pretrained foundation model, run zero-shot: it is not fitted to our data, so the only things to change are what it is given and what is done with its output. Before this section it forecast no better than no change on the October 2026 check (error 1.03 times no change's, Section 3.88). At 20-90 sessions its range held 98.4% of the outcomes of the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87, more than the 95% asked for. The rules were written in tests/validation/run_chronos_improve.py before it ran."));
children.push(P("The data, and a caveat written down first. The owner chose on 2026-11-13 to test on NSE data before the reserved period only, not to open it. Pretraining contamination: Chronos-2 was pretrained by others. Its technical report (Ansari et al., arXiv 2510.15821, October 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15821) and its model card (https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) name the pretraining sources but give no data cut-off, and do not say whether NSE series are in them. Every forecast here is before 2025, so the model may have seen the outcomes. Both arms of each comparison carry that risk, but a change can interact with it: a band set from past errors is narrower if those errors are small because the model has seen those prices. So a change that shipped would have been recorded as possibly helped by that overlap. The weekly cutoff checks (Section 3.88), which are after October 2025, keep watching the served model."));
children.push(P("Research (sources verified; ideas only, nothing downloaded was run). Covariates: the model reads, besides the price, OHLCV and nine engineered covariates as past-only covariates. The technical report finds covariates help when they carry information the target does not; most of these are functions of the price itself. Candidate: the target alone. A band from the model's own errors at 20 past origins, 5 sessions apart (split conformal), instead of the model's quantiles. The centre shrunk toward no change by how well it predicted at the same origins (Campbell and Thompson, Review of Financial Studies 2008, https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055), with the band from the shrunk errors; this is the change that shipped for Prophet on daily and hourly bars (Sections 3.117 and 3.119). Named risk, before running: the 20 origins are 5 sessions apart, so at 90 sessions their errors overlap heavily and the band rests on few independent outcomes; Section 3.116's per-horizon calibration missed at 90 for that reason."));
children.push(table([1300, 1700, 2000, 1900, 2460], [
  ["Arm", "Input", "Band", "Centre", "Source"],
  ["shipped", "OHLCV and nine engineered covariates", "the model's 2.5% and 97.5% quantiles", "the model's median", "—"],
  ["nocov", "the target alone", "the model's quantiles", "the model's median", "Ansari et al. 2025 (covariates help when they carry information the target does not)"],
  ["conformal", "as shipped", "the 95% quantile of its own errors at 20 past origins", "the model's median", "split conformal, as Prophet's band (Section 3.117)"],
  ["shrink", "as shipped", "from the shrunk errors at the same origins", "shrunk toward no change by β = clip(Σpy / Σp², 0, 1)", "Campbell and Thompson 2008; shipped for Prophet (Section 3.117)"],
  ["nocov_shrink", "the target alone", "from the shrunk errors", "shrunk toward no change", "both"],
]));
children.push(P("How it was tested. One forecast and one batch of 20 calibration forecasts per stock, date and horizon, and every band setting was computed from them (chronos_model.chronos_paths). Cross-learning is off, so the batch equals 20 separate calls: the largest difference was 1.6e-6 in log price, against forecasts rounded to 0.01. CHRONOS_LEGACY reproduces the served model exactly (difference 0.0 at 5 and at 90 sessions, TCS from 2022-12-19). Two scopes, each decided on its own: five sessions (a change would become the default on daily bars up to 13), and one 90-session run scored at 20, 40, 60 and 90 (the default beyond 13); hourly bars keep the served model. Choosing: the 32 forecasts of Sections 3.84 and 3.87; an arm is eligible if its pooled coverage is at least 90%, its score lower and its error no higher, and the eligible arm with the lowest score is the candidate. Confirming, once: Section 3.95's draw (12 stocks, six dates 2021-04-01 .. 2024-02-13, 72 forecasts), on which nothing about Chronos-2 had been chosen; a candidate ships only if its score is lower pooled and at 5 or more of the 6 dates, its error is no higher, and its coverage is at least 90%. It took 83 minutes (choosing 38, confirming 45)."));
children.push(P("Choosing (32 forecasts)."));
children.push(table([1500, 1300, 1200, 1100, 1500, 1500, 1260], [
  ["Arm", "Coverage, 5", "Score, 5", "Error, 5", "Coverage, 20-90", "Score, 20-90", "Error, 20-90"],
  ["shipped", "93.8%", "21.03%", "0.967", "98.4%", "69.50%", "1.057"],
  ["nocov", "93.8%", "20.54%", "0.925", "99.2%", "74.94%", "1.123"],
  ["conformal", "93.8%", "25.70%", "0.967", "95.3%", "59.22%", "1.057"],
  ["shrink", "93.8%", "24.74%", "1.011", "94.5%", "60.67%", "0.993"],
  ["nocov_shrink", "93.8%", "24.05%", "1.008", "90.6%", "60.86%", "1.076"],
]));
children.push(P("At five sessions only nocov was eligible. Its score was 2% lower and its error 4% lower, but it was lower at only 2 of the 4 dates. The band from past errors was wider than the model's own (25.70% against 21.03%). At 20-90 sessions conformal and shrink were eligible, and conformal had the lower score: 15% below shipped, lower at 3 of the 4 dates. More than half of the gain came from one date, 2021-01-01, where the model's own band was very wide (score 103.73% against 79.40%)."));
children.push(P("Confirming (72 forecasts): neither candidate passed."));
children.push(table([1400, 1300, 1600, 1100, 1500, 1400, 1060], [
  ["Scope", "Candidate", "Score", "Dates better", "Error (× no change)", "Coverage", "Result"],
  ["Five sessions", "nocov", "21.36% → 21.88%", "2 of 6", "1.011 → 1.069", "95.8% → 97.2%", "not kept (rules 1 and 2)"],
  ["20-90 sessions", "conformal", "60.34% → 62.82%", "2 of 6", "1.067 → 1.067", "97.9% → 95.5%", "not kept (rule 1)"],
]));
children.push(P("What it shows. At five sessions the covariates help on the new draw: without them the error rose from 1.011 to 1.069 times no change's and the score was higher at 4 of 6 dates; the gain on the 32 did not carry over. At 20-90 sessions the band from past errors was no narrower on average, only less reliable: on the 72 its width was on average the model's (57.28% against 57.60%), but it ranged from 0.36 to 2.19 times the model's band from one forecast to the next, and it held 95.5% of outcomes against 97.9%. The extra misses cost more than the width saved. This is the risk named before the run: 20 overlapping 90-session errors are too few to set a band per forecast. The model's own range was narrower on the new draw: at 20-90 sessions it was 65.04% wide on the 32 and 57.60% on the 72, and still held 97.9% of outcomes; the 32's widest bands were largely one date. The caveat did not decide anything: nothing shipped, so no result here rests on what the model may have seen."));
children.push(P("What changed in the app: nothing. Chronos-2 keeps its covariates, its own quantiles and its median at every horizon. The switches stay in chronos_model, off by default (resolve_switches returns CHRONOS_LEGACY; conformal and shrink are refused on hourly bars). Where the programme stands: every model has now been through it. Regime Switching and Vol-LSTM had their fixes before the programme (Sections 3.89, 3.84). Every bar-size-dependent daily change has also been tested on hourly bars (Sections 3.119, 3.120)."));
children.push(P([B("Sources: "), T("Ansari et al., "), Link("https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15821"), T("; the Chronos-2 model card, "), Link("https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2"), T("; Campbell and Thompson, "), Link("https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhm055"), T(".")]));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_chronos_switches.py (3 tests, new), with a stand-in pipeline that forecasts the last price, so no model weights are needed: the defaults are the served model at 5 and 90 sessions, and conformal and shrink are refused on hourly bars; covariates=False gives the model the id, the dates and the price only, and the calibration origins end where they should; the conformal band is the largest error with 4 origins, and the shrunk centre uses beta = sum(p y) / sum(p p) and is no change when the model predicted no change. The suite was then 860. Files: chronos_model.py, tests/test_chronos_switches.py, tests/validation/run_chronos_improve.py, tests/validation/chronos_improve.csv, tests/validation/chronos_improve_parts.csv."));
children.push(H2("3.124 Chronos-2 forecast on NSE holidays and flattened them in its history: fixed, and section 122's test re-run, nothing kept"));
children.push(P("An outside review of the project (13 November 2026) found that Chronos-2 dated its forecasts on NSE holidays. It was right, and the fault was larger than the dates."));
children.push(P("The fault. chronos_model.prepare_timeseries_data put the daily history on a weekday grid and forward-filled it (df.asfreq(\"B\").ffill()). Every NSE holiday became a flat, zero-return day in the input: TCS's history from 2018 to mid-2024 has 94 weekdays with no session, about 14 a year, so the series looked calmer than it is. The forecast's dates ran on weekdays: in the 6 November watchlist run every other model forecast 9, 11, 12, 13 and 16 November, while Chronos-2 forecast 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13 November, and 10 November is a holiday. The forward record matches each step to the last close on or before its date (Section 3.79), so that step will be scored against 9 November's close, and its five-session forecast covers four sessions. The hourly path had half of it: its input was already a plain step counter, but its output dates skipped weekends and not exchange holidays."));
children.push(P("The fix. The input keeps the sessions as they traded, on a plain step counter; no row is added for a holiday, and the real dates are kept for the calendar covariates (day of week, month). The forecast's dates come from model_utils.generate_future_dates, the NSE calendar every other model uses, for daily and hourly bars (chronos_model._real_future_dates). The calibration batch of Section 3.123 passes no date column to the model. On real data (TCS, before 2025) the old code dated its 90-session forecasts on holidays and the new code dates them on the next NSE sessions; the forecasts moved a little (from 2022-12-19 the 90-session centre went from 3022.79 to 2986.72)."));
children.push(P("Section 3.123's test, re-run on the fixed model. Its tests scored each step against the next real session by position, so the wrong dates did not change them, but the flat holidays in the input did. The rules were written in tests/validation/run_chronos_dates.py before it ran: the fix ships whatever it shows, because a forecast dated on a holiday is wrong; the served model before and after the fix is reported only; and Section 3.123's arms and rules are applied again, unchanged. It took 101 minutes."));
children.push(P("The served model before and after the fix (report only)."));
children.push(table([2600, 2200, 2300, 2260], [
  ["Forecasts", "Coverage", "Score", "Error (× no change)"],
  ["Five sessions, the 32", "93.8% → 93.8%", "21.03% → 21.66%", "0.967 → 0.932"],
  ["20-90 sessions, the 32", "98.4% → 99.2%", "69.50% → 67.14%", "1.057 → 1.046"],
  ["Five sessions, the 72", "95.8% → 95.8%", "21.36% → 23.61%", "1.011 → 1.022"],
  ["20-90 sessions, the 72", "97.9% → 97.2%", "60.34% → 59.92%", "1.067 → 1.058"],
]));
children.push(P("At 20-90 sessions the fix helped slightly on both sets: lower score and lower error. At five sessions it was mixed: on the 32 the error fell (0.967 to 0.932) and the score rose; on the 72 both rose, the score from 21.36% to 23.61% and the error from 1.011 to 1.022. The fix stays: the old forecast's better five-session score came from a model whose dates were wrong."));
children.push(P("Choosing on the fixed model (32 forecasts)."));
children.push(table([1500, 1300, 1200, 1100, 1500, 1500, 1260], [
  ["Arm", "Coverage, 5", "Score, 5", "Error, 5", "Coverage, 20-90", "Score, 20-90", "Error, 20-90"],
  ["shipped", "93.8%", "21.66%", "0.932", "99.2%", "67.14%", "1.046"],
  ["nocov", "96.9%", "21.15%", "0.937", "99.2%", "76.74%", "1.115"],
  ["conformal", "93.8%", "25.90%", "0.932", "96.1%", "61.16%", "1.046"],
  ["shrink", "93.8%", "24.11%", "1.028", "94.5%", "60.15%", "1.002"],
  ["nocov_shrink", "93.8%", "24.03%", "1.014", "89.1%", "58.93%", "1.076"],
]));
children.push(P("At five sessions no arm was eligible: the target alone, the candidate in Section 3.123, now has a slightly higher error than the served model (0.937 against 0.932). At 20-90 sessions the shrunk centre was the candidate (score 60.15% against 67.14%, error 1.002 against 1.046); nocov_shrink scored lower but held 89.1%, under the 90% bar. A rounding detail: conformal keeps the model's centre, so its error equals the served model's; it came out 2e-16 higher and failed \"no higher\". It would not have been the candidate, as shrink scored lower. Later tests should compare errors with a tolerance."));
children.push(P("Confirming (72 forecasts): not kept. The shrunk centre scored 62.19% against 59.92%, lower at only 2 of 6 dates (rule 1 failed); its error was lower (1.042 against 1.058) and it held 95.8%."));
children.push(P("What changed in the app: Chronos-2's dates and input, as above. Its configuration is unchanged: covariates, its own quantiles and its median. What is not changed: 17 rows of the forward record keep their old dates, the 10 Chronos-2 rows of the 6 November watchlist that target 10 November and 7 rows of one hourly Chronos-2 forecast from the app that target 10 November; they are not rewritten, so read their 10 November step as 9 November's close. The cutoff checks (Section 3.88) from 13 November on use the fixed model and earlier batches the old one, so their Chronos-2 rows are not strictly comparable. The NSE calendar the models share (pandas_market_calendars) does not know the holiday NSE declared at short notice on 22 January 2024; it affects every model's dates on that day alike and is noted here only."));
children.push(P("The weekly watchlist's time. The first watchlist run took 883 minutes against the 6-7 hours of Section 3.79. Nine of the ten companies were done in 306 minutes; the Windows power log shows the laptop in standby from 23:28 to 08:11, while the tenth company was running, and twice for about 50 minutes earlier. Section 3.79 now says to keep the computer awake during the run."));
children.push(P("Checks: tests/test_chronos_dates.py (4 tests, new): the daily history keeps every session and price as it traded, on a regular step counter, with no zero-return day added; a five-step forecast from 2026-11-06 lands on the same dates as every other model's, without 10 November, with and without covariates; the calibration frames carry no date column; an hourly forecast from the last bar of Monday 9 November starts at 09:15 on Wednesday 11 November. Three of the four fail on the old code; the calibration one passes there, because the old daily path had no date column. The full suite is 864 tests. Files: chronos_model.py, tests/test_chronos_dates.py, tests/validation/run_chronos_dates.py, tests/validation/chronos_dates.csv, tests/validation/chronos_dates_parts.csv."));
children.push(H1("4. Reading the Screen: A Decision Guide"));
children.push(P("The app puts a lot of numbers on one page, and they are NOT equally load-bearing. This chapter is the user's guide to them: what each one is, what value counts as strong or weak, and — the part that matters — which decision it can actually support."));
children.push(P([B("The one-sentence version. "), T("This app is built to tell you HOW UNCERTAIN the next move is, and WHETHER ANY MODEL HAS DEMONSTRABLE SKILL on this ticker right now. It is not built to tell you where the price is going. The decisions it genuinely supports are therefore abstain / size / set a stop — not buy / sell. Every design choice below follows from that, including the ones that deliberately withhold a number you might want.")]));

children.push(H2("4.1 Read it in this order"));
children.push(P("The layout is deliberately top-down: each block gates the one below it. Reading it out of order is the single most common way to reach a wrong conclusion from a correct screen."));
children.push(num("Data-quality banner — is the INPUT sound? If this is red, stop here; nothing below it is meaningful."));
children.push(num("Abstention banner — how many of the selected models beat a random walk on this ticker and horizon."));
children.push(num("Consensus cards — median forecast, and the bullish / bearish / abstained split."));
children.push(num("Confidence and Basis columns — the per-model verdict and WHY it got that verdict."));
children.push(num("Risk & Probability panel — the actual deliverable: range, VaR, expected shortfall, probabilities."));
children.push(num("Signal Score and Conviction — worth reading only for rows that cleared step 4."));
children.push(num("The charts — all models together, then each model's 95% cone against the grey dotted random-walk line."));
children.push(P([B("Row order in the Model Comparison table is not a ranking. "), T("The table is sorted for display, not by merit, and the lowest-error model is not a winner: across the full set, White's Reality Check and Hansen's SPA found no selection-bias-free edge over a random walk (p ~ 0.9; Section 6.5). Reading the top row as “the best model” is exactly the data-snooping error the whole evaluation layer exists to prevent.")]));

children.push(H2("4.2 Step 1 — the data-quality banner decides whether to continue"));
children.push(table([2900, 1500, 4960], [
  ["Banner", "Mode", "What to do"],
  ["Data quality OK (score n/100)", "normal", "Continue."],
  ["Data-quality caveats", "warn", "Continue, but widen your margin — something in the feed is imperfect."],
  ["Event risk detected — showing the risk cone", "risk_cone_only", "NO DIRECTIONAL CALL. An earnings date or a gap is inside the window; the range is the only output."],
  ["Forecast withheld — data quality insufficient", "abstain", "STOP. Directional calls are already suppressed. Refetch, or pick another ticker."],
]));
children.push(P("Open “Data-quality details” for the reason codes. This gate runs on the fetched series at request time and catches a broken INPUT; it says nothing about whether the model is any good — that is step 2. (Mechanics: Section 3.18.)"));

children.push(H2("4.3 Step 2 — the abstention banner is the honest headline"));
children.push(table([3100, 3100, 3160], [
  ["Banner", "Meaning", "Decision"],
  ["No model showed a statistically reliable edge", "zero of n models reached High or Medium", "Treat the RANGE as the entire output. Do not act on the point forecast."],
  ["Only k of n models did better than a no-change forecast in a quick check of 3 recent forecast dates here, which is not proof", "fewer than half", "Weight the range heavily; the point line is weak evidence."],
  ["k of n models did better than a no-change forecast in a quick check of 3 recent forecast dates here: a small sample, not proof", "at least half", "The strongest state this app produces — and still small-sample (see 4.5)."],
  ["Unseen market regime — every directional call is withheld", "the regime-novelty gate (off by default) rated the latest window 'unprecedented'; replaces the three above", "Treat the RANGE as the entire output."],
]));
children.push(P("The red banner is the EXPECTED state for most tickers at short horizons. It is a finding, not a malfunction."));
children.push(P([B("Which models the banner counts: "), T("the rows marked High or Medium in the Confidence column of the Model Comparison table, below the charts. The Basis column beside them gives the reason and the least the chance of no edge can be. The same table is printed in the console.")]));
children.push(P([B("Under the banner, on every run that can show an up/down call: "), T("the line \"No model's up/down call has yet proven reliable across separate weeks of testing. Read a Bullish or Bearish label as a pointer, not a prediction.\" It is the same for every stock, because it comes from the larger tests, not this run (Section 3.99).")]));

children.push(H2("4.4 Step 3 — consensus cards, and why the vote count is not a vote"));
children.push(P("Median Forecast is the median across selected models, with its percentage change. Models Bullish / Bearish counts the directional calls that survived gating, and a caption reports how many were withheld."));
children.push(P("Two cautions that matter more than the numbers:"));
children.push(bullet([B("The models are not independent voters. "), T("They share the same OHLCV history, largely the same engineered features, and in several cases the same family. “14 of 19 bullish” is emphatically NOT fourteen independent opinions — it is closer to one opinion, computed fourteen ways, with correlated errors. Treat agreement as weak corroboration, never as a probability.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("The median is more robust than any single row, "), T("which is precisely why it is the headline. If the median and your chosen model disagree sharply, the model is the outlier, not the median.")]));

children.push(H2("4.5 Step 4 — Confidence and Basis: the only skill claim on the page"));
children.push(P("Confidence comes from a walk-forward backtest run at request time on recent history, and it is the single most decision-relevant column. The rule (backtest.confidence_bucket) is fixed and auditable:"));
children.push(table([1500, 4200, 3660], [
  ["Bucket", "Rule", "How to read it"],
  ["High", "RMSE improvement > 5% over the best naive baseline, AND Diebold-Mariano p < 0.10, AND 95% coverage within 0.85-0.99", "A measurable edge with trustworthy bands."],
  ["Medium", "improvement > 2% and DM p < 0.20", "A modest, weakly significant edge."],
  ["Low", "improvement > 0, but not significant", "Better than the random walk WITHIN NOISE. Not evidence."],
  ["No edge", "no improvement", "No better than assuming the price stays put. The directional call is WITHHELD."],
  ["No data", "insufficient history for the check", "Unassessed — treat as No edge."],
]));
children.push(P([B("The honest caveat, which belongs next to every High you ever see. "), T("This gate runs 3 ORIGINS on at most 600 rows (_GATE_SPLITS = 3), because it must return while a user waits. Three origins is enough to catch a model that is clearly broken; it is NOT enough to establish skill. A High here means “worth a closer look with run_full_backtest.py on more origins”, not “proven”. Conversely, No edge on three origins is quite reliable — it is much easier to fail to beat a random walk convincingly than to beat one by luck three times.")]));
children.push(P([B("What the larger tests add. "), T("With 20 models, one or two High or Medium rows in a run are about what luck alone gives. The larger tests found no model that reliably beats a no-change forecast: on price, on 20 stocks over four October 2026 weeks (Section 3.88); on direction, on 12 separate weeks before 2025 (Section 3.92). So read a single High or Medium as a pointer to that model on that stock, not as a winner; the range is the output. Whether any model has an edge will show in the forward record (Section 3.83), which scores forecasts made before their outcomes existed.")]));
children.push(P("Basis is the plain-language reason, so no row shows a bare verdict:"));
children.push(table([4200, 5160], [
  ["Basis text", "What happened"],
  ["beats random walk (DM p=..., cov ...%; P(no edge) >= ...%)", "High — with the p-value, coverage, and the least the probability of no edge can be (Section 3.52)."],
  ["weak edge (DM p=...; P(no edge) >= ...%)", "Medium. A p-value of 0.14 still leaves at least a 43% chance of no edge."],
  ["within noise (+...% vs RW, DM p=...)", "Low — the improvement is stated so you can see how small it is."],
  ["no edge vs random walk (DM p=...)", "No edge."],
  ["withheld — data quality", "The data-quality gate overrode the skill test."],
  ["withheld — unseen market regime", "The novelty gate fired: current conditions are off-manifold, so a backtest-derived bucket would be an extrapolation (Section 3.26; ships off by default)."],
  ["... · unusual regime", "An advisory suffix — conditions are unusual but not unprecedented; the verdict stands, with a caveat."],
]));
children.push(P([B("HAR-RV always reads No edge, and that is correct. "), T("It makes no point-forecast claim by design; its entire output is the calibrated risk cone (Section 3.12). Do not read its bucket as failure — and do not deselect it, because the fat-tailed risk panel depends on it.")]));

children.push(H2("4.6 Step 5 — the Risk & Probability panel is the deliverable"));
children.push(table([2600, 3200, 3560], [
  ["Metric", "What it is", "What decision it supports"],
  ["Likely range (95%)", "the calibrated interval at the horizon", "THE PRIMARY OUTPUT. Stop placement, position sizing, whether the trade you had in mind is even distinguishable from noise."],
  ["P(price up)", "probability of finishing above today", "Direction only if it is far from 50% — see the calibration warning below."],
  ["P(gain > +x%) / P(loss > x%)", "probability of a move beyond your threshold (set it yourself)", "“Is my target plausible at this horizon?” Often the most useful number on the page."],
  ["P(a -x% stop is hit)", "chance the price TOUCHES -x% at any point before the horizon - about twice P(loss), which only counts where it ends", "Whether a stop that tight will just get you stopped out (Section 3.61)."],
  ["Stop with a 1-in-5 chance of being hit", "the stop distance sized by this stock's volatility over this horizon", "Where to put a stop, instead of one percentage for every stock (Section 3.61)."],
  ["P(a -x% trailing stop is hit)", "chance a stop x% below the highest price since you set it is hit before the horizon", "Whether a trailing stop that tight will last: the book's 8% is hit within a quarter on almost every NSE stock (Section 3.63)."],
  ["P(+x% is touched)", "chance the price touches +x% at any point before the horizon - about twice P(gain)", "Whether a sell order at your target is likely to fill (Section 3.63)."],
  ["Break-even move (costs, tax, inflation)", "the gross move a long trade needs to come out level: round-trip costs plus inflation over the horizon grossed up for tax", "The floor any target must clear. About +0.3% at 5 days, almost all of it STT (Section 3.64)."],
  ["P(the move clears break-even)", "the chance the price ends above that move", "Read next to P(price up): the gap is what costs take (Section 3.64)."],
  ["Market breadth caption", "the last 10 sessions' advance/decline ratio, its fifth since 2018, and the market's measured next-month volatility for that fifth", "Context for the whole market's risk; this stock's range is not adjusted for it (Section 3.64)."],
  ["Results notice", "NSE's calendar shows results inside the horizon; the panel above is then widened", "A results day carries ~3-4x an ordinary day's variance. Every panel number already includes it; the per-model charts do not (Section 3.65)."],
  ["What the options market prices", "live implied volatility, expected move, the market's results-day move and a protective put's cost (F&O stocks)", "A second, risk-neutral opinion on the spread. A ratio well below 1 against the panel usually means a recent spike is still in the models' history (Section 3.65)."],
  ["O'Neil-disciple checklist", "the book's pocket pivot, gap-up, moving-average and NIFTY distribution-day signals for this stock, each beside what it measured on NSE", "The book's view, honestly labelled: pocket pivots showed no edge, gap-ups reversed, the market model's timing was luck; only the heavy-volume 50-day break held up, as a lead. Not a buy or sell call (Section 3.66)."],
  ["95% downside (VaR)", "the 5th-percentile price", "A worst-case-but-not-extreme reference for sizing."],
  ["Expected shortfall", "the AVERAGE outcome in that worst 5%", "What a bad day actually costs, not just where it starts. Always worse than VaR."],
  ["Downside / upside vol", "dispersion split by direction", "Whether the risk is symmetric."],
  ["Vol skew (down/up)", "their ratio", "> 1 means the downside is the fatter side. Size smaller when it is."],
]));
children.push(P("Two things to check before trusting this panel:"));
children.push(bullet([B("Read the caption. "), T("“Probabilities are counted directly off the empirical, fat-tailed return distribution” means HAR-RV (or Vol-LSTM) was in the run and the tail is real. “Approximate read” means no fat-tailed model was selected and each 95% band was approximated as log-normal — which UNDERSTATES TAIL RISK, the one thing you least want understated. Add HAR-RV to the selection and re-run.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Treat P(up) as ordinal, not cardinal. "), T("A probability read second-hand off a regression interval is not a calibrated probability, and this project measured that directly: the same construction scored ECE ~ 0.31 in the cross-sectional work (Section 3.30 / 6.14), i.e. a “60%” could be off by roughly 30 points. Use it to rank (“further from 50% than yesterday”), not as a number to bet proportionally on. The properly calibrated alternative — a classifier asked DIRECTLY for a probability, reaching ECE 0.0139 — exists in cross_sectional_model.py but is NOT wired into the UI.")]));

children.push(P([B("Below the panel, three collapsed expanders hold information rather than forecasts (Section 3.62). "), T("Company check scores the company against six measurable hallmarks of a great stock and the Appendix B ratio tests, with the book's rule beside each result. Use it to ask whether you would want to own the company at all, which no price model can answer. Stock screens (Section 3.68) says which of Hough's screen recipes the stock passes and runs his checks on a screen survivor. Risks this forecast cannot see lists the kinds of risk the models ignore. None of them changes a number above it.")]));
children.push(H2("4.7 Step 6 — Signal Score and Conviction, for rows that earned it"));
children.push(P("Signal Score (%) is the model's forecast percentage change to the horizon. It is the value the Bullish/Bearish call is computed from, and on its own it is close to meaningless: a +3% signal from a No-edge model is a number a random walk would also produce."));
children.push(P("Conviction = the forecast move divided by half the model's own 95% band."));
children.push(table([2200, 7160], [
  ["|Conviction|", "Reading"],
  ["< 0.5", "Within the model's own noise. Ignore the direction."],
  ["0.5 - 1.0", "A real move relative to its uncertainty, but inside the band."],
  [">= 1.0", "The move exceeds the model's 95% band — the current price sits outside it. High conviction."],
  ["—", "The model emits no interval."],
]));
children.push(P([B("The trap: "), T("conviction is measured against THE MODEL'S OWN band, so a model with a badly narrow band manufactures high conviction. That is why the two columns must be read together — a high Conviction on a row whose Basis reports poor coverage is an over-confident model, not a strong signal. Coverage in the 0.85-0.99 window is one of the three conditions for High, which is why High + Conviction >= 1 is the only combination worth weight.")]));

children.push(H2("4.8 Step 7 — the charts"));
children.push(P("Above the per-model sections, All models together draws every selected model's median path from the last close, the median of them and the median of their 95% ranges, against the random walk (Section 3.71): use it to see whether the models agree, and read it with the Confidence column. A Line / Candles switch sets how history is drawn; candles are adjusted like the forecasts. Each model then has two tabs, Chart and Predicted values, both full width at any screen size (Section 3.67). Each chart shows history, a dashed last-close line, the forecast, its 95% range as a cone fanning out from the last close, and a GREY DOTTED RANDOM-WALK BASELINE, with weekends and holidays removed from the axis. The question the chart answers is not “where does the line go” but “is the forecast line meaningfully outside the grey line's band?” Usually it is not. That is the honest picture of short-horizon equity forecasting, and seeing it is the point of drawing the baseline at all."));

children.push(H2("4.9 Feature flags: one registry, with the evidence attached"));
children.push(P("Six USE_* toggles decide what the app actually does, and they used to be defined wherever they happened to be consumed - five in UI.py, one in ML_model.py. That is fine until something needs to read ALL of them at once, and the console startup banner does. Building it meant importing a flag out of a model module purely to display it: the UI depending on ML_model for a piece of configuration, which is the wrong direction. config.py is now the single source of truth, and it imports nothing from this project, so anything may import it."));
children.push(table([3400, 1400, 4560], [
  ["Flag", "Ships", "Owning module"],
  ["USE_MARKET_CONTEXT", "off", "market_context.py"],
  ["USE_IV_ADJUST", "off", "iv_data.py"],
  ["USE_ASYMMETRIC_CONE", "off", "HAR_RV_model.py"],
  ["USE_INTRADAY_FEATURES", "ON", "ML_model.py"],
  ["USE_REGIME_NOVELTY", "off", "autoencoder_regime.py"],
  ["USE_PREDICTION_LOG", "ON", "prediction_log.py"],
]));
children.push(P([B("The rationale lives with the flag. "), T("Each registry entry carries the measured result behind its default, so “why is this off?” is answerable without hunting through a documentation section. Four of the six are off because an A/B said so. Running python config.py prints the whole set with reasons.")]));
children.push(P([B("The banner is generated, not hand-listed. "), T("active_flags() derives it from the registry, so a flag added here appears in the console automatically. A flag the operator cannot see is a flag that silently changes results, and a hand-maintained display list is how one goes missing — test_active_flags_covers_the_whole_registry pins it.")]));
children.push(P([B("Every flag has an environment override. "), T("STOCKAPP_USE_MARKET_CONTEXT=1 flips one for a single run, and STOCKAPP_USE_INTRADAY_FEATURES=0 is exactly the A/B this project still owes on the intraday features. Editing a constant to run an experiment is how experiments end up committed by accident. A malformed value falls back to the default rather than raising: a stray export should fail to take effect, not stop the app. An active override is called out in the banner, because a run with one is not the shipped configuration and its numbers need reading that way.")]));
children.push(P([B("The serve-time gate constants moved here too "), T("(GATE_SPLITS = 3, GATE_MIN_TRAIN, GATE_MAX_ROWS) and are env-tunable, which makes the trade-off visible rather than buried: raise STOCKAPP_GATE_SPLITS for a stronger confidence check at the cost of a slower Submit. See Section 4.5 for why three origins is a filter and not proof.")]));
children.push(P("One deliberate wrinkle: ML_model does `from config import USE_INTRADAY_FEATURES` rather than reading it through the module, so ML_model.USE_INTRADAY_FEATURES stays monkeypatchable by the existing parity tests."));

children.push(H2("4.10 The same story in the terminal"));
children.push(P("Running streamlit run UI.py from cmd used to print a long, interleaved trace of per-model chatter — “Fitting Auto-ARIMA...”, “Simulating 10000 price paths...”, “Prediction Complete.” — and NOTHING about what the app concluded. Nineteen models would announce completion and the terminal could not tell you whether a single one of them had beaten a random walk. The conclusion is the product; it was browser-only. console.py closes that gap by adding three blocks around the existing trace."));
children.push(P([B("The startup banner "), T("answers a question the console previously could not: WHICH VERSION OF THE APP IS THIS? Five USE_* flags materially change what the models produce (market-context, IV-tilt, asymmetric-cone, intraday-features, regime-novelty, prediction-log), and an operator watching cmd had no way to tell which were active. The seed and the compute device are printed for the same reason — a reported number is not reproducible unless you know both.")]));
children.push(P([B("The RUN header "), T("frames each Submit — ticker, interval, horizon, model count, timestamp — so one run's trace is separable from the next in a long scroll.")]));
children.push(P([B("The RESULT block is the console mirror of the browser's decision layer, "), T("and it maps line-for-line onto the sections above:")]));
children.push(table([2600, 6760], [
  ["Console line", "UI equivalent"],
  ["data quality", "the data-quality banner (Section 4.2)"],
  ["regime", "the novelty gate's Basis annotation (Section 4.5)"],
  ["confidence", "the Confidence + Basis columns (Section 4.5)"],
  ["consensus", "the consensus cards (Section 4.4)"],
  ["verdict", "the abstention banner (Section 4.3)"],
  ["logged", "browser-silent: rows appended to the forward prediction log (Section 3.29)"],
]));
children.push(P("A representative RESULT block:"));
children.push(P([Code("RESULT  RELIANCE.NS  1d  h=5  (37.4s)")]));
children.push(P([Code("  data quality    OK (score 92/100) -> forecasts usable")]));
children.push(P([Code("  regime          novelty gate off (USE_REGIME_NOVELTY = False)")]));
children.push(P([Code("  confidence      HAR-RV         No edge   no edge vs random walk (DM p=0.55)")]));
children.push(P([Code("                  Meta-Ensemble  Medium    weak edge (DM p=0.14; P(no edge) >= 43%)")]));
children.push(P([Code("                  Quantile-LGBM  Low       within noise (+1% vs RW, DM p=0.31)")]));
children.push(P([Code("  consensus       median 1,423.55 (+0.84%)  |  bullish 2  |  bearish 1  |  withheld 1")]));
children.push(P([Code("  verdict         Only 1 of 4 models did better than a no-change forecast in")]));
children.push(P([Code("                  a quick check of 3 recent forecast dates here, which is")]));
children.push(P([Code("                  not proof. Weight the range over the point line.")]));
children.push(P([Code("  logged          4 row(s) -> .prediction_log/predictions.jsonl")]));
children.push(P([B("It renders verdicts; it never recomputes them. "), T("The buckets come from the same conf_map, the reasons from the same gating.confidence_reason, and the verdict sentence from the same _honesty_banner the browser renders — passed through strip_markdown so the one authored sentence serves both channels. Re-deriving any of it here is precisely how two surfaces start telling a user different things. The one deliberate ordering choice: the honesty banner is re-read AFTER the data-quality and novelty gates have been applied, so a degraded run reports the withheld verdict rather than the pre-gate one.")]));
children.push(P([B("Two robustness details that are not cosmetic. "), T("Output is plain ASCII and every write is wrapped: a Windows cmd session on a legacy code page cannot encode a rupee sign or an em dash, and an unguarded print there raises UnicodeEncodeError — which would let a cosmetic log line kill a forecast. _write degrades to a replacement character instead, and test_write_survives_a_legacy_console_encoding pins that behaviour. The banner is also printed ONCE PER PROCESS via a module-level flag rather than st.session_state, because Streamlit re-executes UI.py top-to-bottom on every widget interaction and a naive banner would reprint on every click.")]));
children.push(P("Set STOCKAPP_QUIET=1 to silence all of it; the per-model chatter is unaffected either way."));

children.push(H2("4.11 Putting it together"));
children.push(table([4200, 5160], [
  ["Screen state", "Supported decision"],
  ["Data quality red (abstain)", "Act on nothing. Fix the data."],
  ["Event risk / risk_cone_only", "Size for the range. No direction, whatever the table says."],
  ["Red abstention banner, all No edge", "Range-only. If your thesis needs a direction, this app does not supply one — that is information, and it is worth acting on."],
  ["Mixed, your model High + Conviction >= 1 + agrees with the median", "The strongest state available. Still 3 origins — confirm with run_full_backtest.py before sizing up."],
  ["High conviction, but Basis shows poor coverage", "An over-confident model. Discount it."],
  ["P(loss > x%) materially exceeds P(gain > +x%) at the same x, vol skew > 1", "Asymmetric downside. Size down or widen the stop."],
  ["“Approximate read” caption", "Re-run with HAR-RV before using any tail number."],
]));

children.push(H2("4.12 What the screen cannot tell you"));
children.push(P("Stated plainly, because a guide that only explains the numbers implies they cover more than they do:"));
children.push(bullet([B("Whether to buy. "), T("No entry signal is produced anywhere, by design.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Anything about fundamentals, news, or announcements. "), T("Inputs are OHLCV and a market proxy. A result that will be obsolete the moment an earnings release lands is not flagged as such beyond the event-risk gate. The Company check expander shows today's fundamentals against the book's rules, but as information beside the forecast, not in it (Section 3.62). Scheduled results are the exception: the results notice widens the risk panel when they fall inside the horizon (Section 3.65).")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Which model is best. "), T("SPA and the Reality Check say the data cannot support that claim.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Real-world probability. "), T("Every probability is conditional on the model and on the past resembling the future. It is model uncertainty, not certainty.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Anything validated at longer horizons. "), T("The evidence base here is short-horizon daily and hourly.")]));
children.push(P([B("Before acting, three questions. "), T("Is the data-quality banner green? Does the RANGE — not the point forecast — still make the position worth taking? And is there a single number on the page that would look different if the model had no skill at all? If the answer to the last one is no, the screen is telling you to do nothing, which is a legitimate output and the one it produces most often.")]));

children.push(H2("4.13 How good is the prediction, and how confident can I be?"));
children.push(P([B("Short answer: little confidence in the direction or the exact price; reasonable confidence in the range "), T("- how far the price might move.")]));
children.push(H3("The price or direction call"));
children.push(bullet([B("Across all models, "), T("none beats the simple guess \'tomorrow\'s price = today\'s price\' once you allow for the fact that many models were tried. White\'s Reality Check and Hansen\'s SPA gave p = 0.90 and 0.87 (Section 3.16): no reliable edge.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Per model, "), T("most rows end up as \'No edge\'. When a model does pass its quick check, the Basis column also prints how likely it still is to have none; at the \'High\' cut-off that is at least 38% (Section 3.52).")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Across stocks, "), T("a cross-sectional model does pick up a small real pattern - stocks that rose sharply in the last week tend to give some of it back - but it is a long-known effect rather than something the model learned (Section 3.39). It breaks even at about 6 bp per round trip, against at least 22 bp of statutory cost to buy and sell shares in India, so it cannot be profited from (Sections 3.40, 3.56).")]));
children.push(P("So if a model says \'Bullish, +1.8%\', treat it as a coin flip with a story attached."));
children.push(H3("The range"));
children.push(bullet([B("Coverage. "), T("The 95% bands from the volatility models (HAR-RV, Vol-LSTM) and the corrected ARIMAX hold the actual price about 92-96% of the time in walk-forward tests - roughly right, not exact. In the Section 3.41 configuration it was about 92%, a little below target.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Risk figures. "), T("The Risk & Probability panel\'s 5% worst-case loss (VaR), the average loss in that worst 5% (expected shortfall) and the probability of a move larger than X% are read straight off the volatility models\' measured past errors. They do not assume a bell curve.")]));
children.push(P([B("How to use it. "), T("Check the data-quality banner first; if it withholds the forecast or shows the range only, stop there. Read the Confidence column, not the row order - \'No edge\' means ignore that model\'s direction. Treat the Risk & Probability panel and the shaded band as the actual answer: the price will most likely land between Rs X and Rs Y. Use Conviction only on rows that earned it; even then, a value above 1 means the predicted move is larger than the model\'s own uncertainty, not that it will happen.")]));
children.push(P([B("Caveats. "), T("The coverage figures come from a handful of stocks and test dates, so they are approximate. Hourly forecasts have not been validated the way the daily ones have. The truest measure will be the forward prediction log (Section 3.29), which checks every served forecast once its outcome is known - but it needs some weeks of use before its numbers mean anything.")]));
children.push(P([B("In plain terms: use the app to size your risk, not to pick a direction.")]));

children.push(H1("5. Evaluation and Backtesting"));
children.push(P("A model can't be trusted until tested out-of-sample on a moving window. backtest.py provides this."));
children.push(H2("5.1 Walk-forward backtesting"));
children.push(P("walk_forward_backtest picks several origins through history; at each it re-fits on data up to that point, forecasts the next horizon steps, and compares to what actually happened — mimicking real deployment (you only know the past)."));
children.push(H2("5.2 Baselines"));
children.push(P("Every forecast is compared to a ladder of baselines, not just one, because beating the random walk is a surprisingly weak bar:"));
children.push(bullet([B("Naive (random walk) "), T("— the next horizon prices all equal the last observed price. The primary benchmark; a model that can't beat it adds no value.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Drift "), T("— random walk plus the mean historical log-return; beats naive whenever there's any trend.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("AR(1) "), T("— a first-order autoregression on log returns; beats naive whenever there's short-horizon return autocorrelation.")]));
children.push(P("The leaderboards report naive_rmse, drift_rmse and ar1_rmse side by side."));
children.push(H2("5.3 Is the edge real? The Diebold-Mariano test"));
children.push(P("RMSE deltas on near-random-walk returns are often inside the noise band, so a smaller RMSE on a handful of origins can be luck. The Diebold-Mariano test (backtest.diebold_mariano) asks whether the loss differential between the model and the naive walk is statistically different from zero. It uses a HAC (Newey-West) long-run variance with h−1 lags — to account for the autocorrelation overlapping multi-step forecasts create — plus the Harvey-Leybourne-Newbold small-sample correction. The harness reports the statistic (negative means the model is more accurate), a two-sided p-value, and a plain verdict (significantly better / not significant / significantly worse); the leaderboards carry dm_p and dm_sig."));
children.push(H2("5.4 Metrics"));
children.push(table([2200, 5360, 1800], [
  ["Metric", "What it measures", "Good value"],
  ["RMSE / MAE", "Average price error", "lower"],
  ["SMAPE", "Symmetric percentage error", "lower"],
  ["MASE", "Error scaled by the naive forecast's error (comparable across tickers)", "< 1 beats naive"],
  ["Directional accuracy", "Fraction of steps with correct up/down direction", "> 0.5 beats chance"],
  ["Coverage", "Fraction of actuals inside the 95% interval", "approx 0.95"],
]));
children.push(P("MASE and directional accuracy are scale-free, so they compare across stocks; raw RMSE is not (it depends on the price level)."));
children.push(P([B("Economic metrics run alongside them. "), T("walk_forward_backtest also returns a strategy block (strategy_metrics.evaluate_strategy) that turns the same forecasts into a cost-charged return stream and reports Sharpe, Sortino, information ratio versus buy-and-hold, maximum drawdown, information coefficient, hit rate and turnover — because a good RMSE and a profitable forecast are separate claims, and only the second is what a user of a forecast wants (Section 3.23). Pass strategy=False to skip it, cost_bps= to change the cost assumption, and watch the overlapping_origins flag: if the origin spacing is shorter than the horizon the trades overlap and every risk-adjusted figure is optimistic.")]));
children.push(H2("5.5 Running the backtests"));
children.push(P("The manual evaluation drivers live in tests/validation/ (run from the repo root):"));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_full_backtest.py AAPL")]));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_multi_backtest.py \"AAPL,MSFT,GOOGL\"")]));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_multi_backtest.py \"AAPL,MSFT,GOOGL\" --fast")]));
children.push(P("Both drivers checkpoint to CSV and are resumable if interrupted."));
children.push(P("Three further drivers answer the questions the statistical leaderboards do not — is it profitable, is the risk number calibrated, and does the deep volatility model beat the four-parameter regression:"));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_strategy_eval.py RELIANCE.NS [--fast] [--cost 25] [--deadband]")]));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_var_backtest.py RELIANCE.NS [--alpha 0.01] [--horizon 5]")]));
children.push(P([Code("python tests/validation/run_vol_comparison.py RELIANCE.NS [--quick] [--online]")]));

// 5. Findings
children.push(H1("6. Empirical Findings"));
children.push(P("Numbers below are indicative, not definitive — a few tickers and origins — so treat them as directional evidence. The point is the method: walk-forward + a baseline ladder + the Diebold-Mariano significance test, so claims are grounded."));
children.push(P([B("Every ARIMAX+GARCH row in this chapter is ARIMA without regressors. "), T("The regressors never reached the model (pmdarima ignored the keyword they were passed under; Section 3.60). The rows are kept because they are correct for the model that ran, which the meta-ensemble still uses. The context A/B\'s \'neutral for ARIMAX\' is therefore not a finding about market regressors: ARIMAX could not see them. Fitted honestly (lagged), the regressors cost ~3% RMSE.")]));
children.push(H2("6.1 Multi-ticker leaderboard"));
children.push(P("RELIANCE.NS / TCS.NS / INFY.NS, 4 splits, 5-day horizon, ranked by mean RMSE:"));
children.push(table([2700, 1700, 1900, 1500, 1560], [
  ["Model", "RMSE mean", "beats-naive", "DM wins", "MASE"],
  ["Ensemble-Stack", "28.06", "3/3", "TCS (p=0.0002)", "1.37"],
  ["ARIMA+GARCH", "32.11", "3/3", "TCS (p=0.003)", "1.54"],
  ["RegimeSwitching", "32.47", "3/3", "TCS, INFY", "1.55"],
  ["ARIMAX+GARCH", "32.53", "1/3", "TCS (worse on INFY)", "1.56"],
  ["Meta-Ensemble", "33.15", "1/3", "-", "1.59"],
  ["XGBoost / LightGBM / Quantile", "33.4-33.8", "0-1/3", "-", "~1.6"],
  ["LSTM", "33.98", "0/3", "-", "1.57"],
  ["N-BEATS", "63.78", "0/3", "worse (TCS 111)", "2.41"],
]));
children.push(H2("6.2 What the evidence says"));
children.push(bullet([B("The Diebold-Mariano test is essential. "), T("Several models beat naive on RMSE, but statistically-significant edge is rare and ticker-specific (concentrated on TCS; none on RELIANCE). Beating the random walk on a handful of origins is often inside the noise band.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Most consistent: "), T("the Ensemble-Stack and the statistical models (ARIMA / Regime-Switching) beat naive on all three tickers and are the only DM-significant wins.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("MASE > 1 almost everywhere "), T("— against the one-step naive, genuine multi-step skill is thin at a 5-day horizon.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("N-BEATS is still unstable "), T("(blew up to RMSE 111 on TCS); the earlier capacity reduction helped on US data but isn't sufficient here. Neural nets remain high-variance — never trust a single run's ranking.")]));
children.push(bullet([B("Prophet over-extrapolates "), T("and was under-covered (~0.7) on some tickers. That was 1.2.2's native band; on 1.5.0 the band is calibrated on Prophet's own errors and held 96.3% of outcomes on NSE (Section 3.77).")]));
children.push(H2("6.3 Did market context help? No (the honest result)"));
children.push(P("A controlled A/B (context ON vs OFF, same models and splits) on the two large-cap Indian tickers. Delta is the change in RMSE; positive means worse:"));
children.push(table([3360, 2000, 2000, 2000], [
  ["Model", "RELIANCE", "TCS", "Effect"],
  ["XGBoost", "+10.3%", "+6.4%", "worse"],
  ["LightGBM", "+5.0%", "+10.9%", "worse"],
  ["Quantile-LGBM", "+2.9%", "-0.1%", "worse / neutral"],
  ["Ensemble-Stack", "+0.2%", "+0.3%", "neutral"],
